Rails/Svelte have excellent-but-exceptional examples (Rails `baa1ca5f2`, Svelte PR #18620, the latter AI-drafted and disclosed); Astro's Changes/Testing/Docs template looks consistently filled-in rather than exceptional; Vue.js sample was jargon-heavy and inconclusive. Sibling entry `broad-audience-dev-tools-eslint-and-homebrew-write-outsider-clear-prs-prettier-and-recent-vscode-mostly-dont.md` covers the developer-tools half (ESLint/Homebrew positive, Prettier/VS Code negative) of the same research brief.

- [Torvalds's actual commit history splits sharply by repo: on torvalds/linux his own recent commits are almost entirely terse merges and version bumps (the craft reputation does not hold there today), but on his subsurface side project his non-merge commits are genuinely excellent outsider-readable exemplars — full causal chains, named regressions, explicit uncertainty, self-correction](writing-craft/torvalds-real-commits-show-the-craft-reputation-holds-on-subsurface-but-not-on-the-modern-kernel-where-hes-almost-entirely-a-merge-integrator.md) — 2026-08-14 — Pulled 29 real commits (6 kernel non-merge + 23 subsurface) via `gh api search/commits`. GitHub search caps at 1,000 results; of the full 1,000-commit torvalds/linux sample, only 6 are neither a Merge commit nor a version-bump tag — he's almost entirely an integrator on the modern kernel. Those 6 are still genuinely good (`31e62c2ebbfd` ptrace dumpable fix, `1f5ffc672165` self-correcting a bad merge-conflict resolution). On subsurface the pattern the corpus flagged holds strongly: `da6c75350215`, `05e1294be9f4`, `a0a631122ab3`, `a2028cd6ef4a` show named-regression citation, plain-language symptom description, explicit "I don't know why this works but the docs say" honesty, and self-correction of an earlier wrong diagnosis — not every subsurface commit clears the bar (`47b0a9ce65e1`, `3446dd512597` are one-line, no-symptom terse), so the excellent ones are a real identifiable subset, not the median.

- [Among widely-used, non-specialist-audience dev tools, ESLint and Homebrew/brew consistently write outsider-clear PR bodies with causal explanation, Prettier's PR bodies are almost all unfilled templates, and VS Code's current commit stream is dominated by feature-internal jargon (and increasingly AI-authored) rather than clarity for a generalist reader](writing-craft/broad-audience-dev-tools-eslint-and-homebrew-write-outsider-clear-prs-prettier-and-recent-vscode-mostly-dont.md) — 2026-08-14 — ESLint PR #21204 (`no-var` catch-shadowing fix) and Homebrew PR #23525 (batch-install failure isolation) both pass a strict outsider test using only standard-language/CLI vocabulary, not tool-internals jargon; both projects' PR templates structurally require a "why" section and contributors actually fill it in. Prettier ships an identical-looking template but 9/11 examined merged PRs left "## Description" as the unfilled placeholder comment. VS Code's separately-authored monthly release notes (code.visualstudio.com/updates) are NOT the same artifact as its PR/commit bodies — sampled live commits are majority Copilot-coding-agent-authored (`Co-authored-by: Copilot`, `Copilot-Session` trailers), and even the single clearest human-readable example found (#330910) is self-labeled "(Commit message generated by Copilot)"; most human PR bodies assume undefined VS Code-internal jargon ("Agent Host," "ADR 004," "CAPI proxy"). One genuine outsider-clear VS Code exception: PR #330923 (Proxy/thenable deadlock guard), needs only standard JS Promise vocabulary.

- [A fresh, differently-angled search for git-commit/PR-writing-craft reputation finds two genuinely new convergent signals outside the Linux-kernel cluster (PostgreSQL's commit-message discipline, and Pieter Hintjens/ZeroMQ's C4 Problem:/Solution: format with 5+ independent project adoptions) plus one strong single-source find (Simon Tatham/PuTTY)](writing-craft/fresh-angle-fan-out-finds-postgresql-zeromq-c4-angular-and-simon-tatham-as-new-convergent-or-near-convergent-commit-craft-signals.md) — 2026-08-14 — ZeroMQ's C4 "Problem:"/"Solution:" commit format (Lobsters-praised, independent of the prior HN-only sweep) was wholesale adopted, by name, by at least 5 other projects (OSSEC, JeroMQ, CSIRT Gadgets, DigitalOcean's captainslog, geomet) — adoption is a costlier signal than citation. PostgreSQL has a dedicated wiki "Commit Message Guidance" page plus independent Crunchy Data corroboration, but only one source-family deep (Postgres-ecosystem-adjacent), not fully independent 3+. Angular's commit template is the most mechanically-propagated format (direct credited ancestor of Conventional Commits) but that's convergence on a machine-parseable schema, not on prose-writing craft. Simon Tatham (PuTTY)'s "Writing commit messages" essay is a real, HN-discussed single find, same tier as antirez — not a second cluster. Four angles came up genuinely empty on the writing-craft-specifically axis: language/runtime core teams (rustc, CPython — commit *organization* advice only), security-critical projects (OpenSSH, Chromium — real policies, no outside craft praise found), formal-methods projects (seL4 — proof-integrity process discipline, not writing craft; TLA+/Lamport — clean miss, zero connection to git practice), and a specific woman/non-US engineer with a documented git-craft reputation (checked Sarah Sharp, Julia Evans, and four other named individuals directly — real reputations exist but for other things, not commit-writing craft; this sweep can't tell you if that's a real gap or an indexing blind spot).

- [Salvatore Sanfilippo's real Redis commits demonstrate his stated 'commit messages are not titles' philosophy in practice — they pass the outsider test better than a terse Beams-style title because they restate the causal chain in the commit's own words rather than deferring to a linked issue, but this costs scannability and a minority of his commits drift into implementation-internals prose](writing-craft/antirez-real-redis-commits-show-dense-synopsis-beats-terse-why-not-what-for-self-contained-outsider-clarity-at-the-cost-of-scannability.md) — 2026-08-14 — Pulled and read 12+ real antirez commits from `redis/redis` (canonical repo) across 2013 and 2020. Best examples (`27db38d069`, `48cde3fe47`, `94e8c9e77e`, `b09ea1bd90`, `41d3147344`) pass a strict outsider test: numbered causal chains, explicit tradeoffs, reproduction commands. Checked commit `27db38d069` against its linked issue #1449 directly — the issue is a raw log dump with zero root-cause narrative, so antirez's commit-body causal explanation is strictly better sourced than the terse-title-plus-link alternative would have been. Honest counter-example found in the same sample: `db862e8ef0` lapses into internals-narration in its last two paragraphs without re-grounding the "why" for an outsider — roughly 4/5 hit rate, not "all of them." Real cost: no 50/72 discipline, `git log --oneline` is close to useless on his commits — a different reader-query pattern (read-one-fully vs. scan-many), not a strictly better convention.

- [Privy classes WebAuthn passkeys as authorization keys rather than users, so a quorum of passkey P-256 public keys gives device-bound m-of-n approval that signs the canonicalized request as the WebAuthn challenge — avoiding user JWTs, the backend-owned-HPKE-keypair weakness, and any custom signing CLI](validator-key-custody/privy-passkeys-are-authorization-keys-not-users-so-a-passkey-quorum-gives-device-bound-threshold-approval-without-user-jwts-or-a-custom-cli.md) — 2026-08-14 — Privy lists "a biometric key or passkey, following the WebAuthn standard" as an *authorization key* (same class as server-held app keys), and the key-quorum create API takes `public_keys` as a first-class member array beside `user_ids`/`key_quorum_ids` — so N passkeys form an m-of-N quorum with no user members and `/v1/wallets/authenticate` never in the flow. The passkey recipe shows the canonicalized `{version,method,url,body,headers}` payload base64url-encoded and passed as the **WebAuthn challenge**, signature submitted as `webauthn:<authenticatorData>:<clientDataJSON>:<signature>` — the hardware signs the request itself, not a server-supplied description, which is exactly the property an approval UI needs. Contrast with the *user*-member path: `/v1/wallets/authenticate` mints a time-bound key HPKE-encrypted to a caller-supplied `recipient_public_key`, and that API "can be called from either your app's frontend or backend" — client-side SDKs encrypt "from the TEE to the client" (genuinely non-custodial), but the documented "advanced" backend-owned-keypair pattern lets a server holding 3 JWTs mint 3 valid signatures with no human present. MFA does NOT close that gap: it gates "actions that use the embedded wallet's private key" and is cached per an MFA-verification-duration, not per request. Also: replay protection is a caller-chosen `privy-request-expiry` with no server nonce — idempotency needs `privy-idempotency-key` + app-side closure. Nested quorums count as one parent approval with no special signing flow. Method caution: the pricing page yields *opposite* entitlement answers as flattened text vs. parsed DOM — by DOM row containers, "Policy engine" and "Key quorum approvals" both show Developer+Enterprise checks and "Available as add-on" belongs to the adjacent Advanced SSO row.

- [Independently-sourced reputation for exceptional PR/commit-message/code-review craft converges most strongly on the Linux kernel cluster (Torvalds, kernel docs, Peter Hutterer, tpope, Chris Beams as its popularizer), not on any single tech company](writing-craft/independent-craft-reputation-for-git-commit-and-pr-writing-converges-on-the-linux-kernel-cluster-not-any-single-tech-company.md) — 2026-08-14 — 6+ separate Hacker News threads (2012-2024) independently name Torvalds/kernel commit style, including an outsider citing Torvalds's unrelated hobby project (`subsurface`) as worth emulating; Chris Beams's essay explicitly credits the kernel, Git itself, and tpope as pre-existing exemplars before stating its rules. Google is the strongest company-level convergence but mainly as a cited doctrine/textbook source (eng-practices docs, *Software Engineering at Google* book), not as outsider-celebrated PR/commit craft — no source found pointing at specific celebrated Google PRs. Checked and ruled out for weak/no writing-craft-specific sourcing: Bitcoin Core/Pieter Wuille (real reputation, but for review volume and technical judgment, not writing craft), any single maintainer "famous for kind reviews" (no named individual surfaced), Stripe/Linear (general culture reputation only, no dedicated writing-craft reputation). Salvatore Sanfilippo (antirez) has one genuine, distinct, independently-argued position ("commit messages are not titles") worth citing as a dissenting view, not a second convergence cluster.

- [Favicons need an opaque brand-color plate, not a bare glyph, because no single flat color clears 3:1 contrast against both light and dark browser tab chrome](frontend-design/favicons-need-an-opaque-brand-plate-not-a-bare-glyph-because-no-single-color-clears-3-1-on-both-light-and-dark-browser-tabs.md) — 2026-08-14 — Stripe/Linear ship an opaque background-plate square in brand color; GitHub/Vercel/Anthropic/Notion ship a bare near-black/near-white glyph, never a saturated brand hue as a bare glyph. `prefers-color-scheme` inside a favicon SVG works in an isolated SVG document (verified via Playwright colorScheme emulation) but real cross-browser favicon support is disputed/inconsistent in 2026, and every setup still needs a non-adaptive ICO fallback anyway.

- [Matrix/Synapse is the clearest real-world precedent for a reference implementation being demoted into a commercial product, and it shows the neutral foundation created to prevent capture can still end up financially dependent on the very commercial entity it was meant to check](lfdt-labs-prior-art/matrix-synapse-shows-a-neutral-foundation-can-still-end-up-financially-captured-by-the-commercial-spinout-it-was-built-to-check.md) — 2026-08-14 — Matrix.org Foundation (UK CIC, 2019) never became financially independent of Element (still ~£3M/yr donated core dev as of 2024) and declined to fund a fork when Element relicensed Synapse to AGPLv3 in Nov 2023 — legal separation without funding independence still ends in capture. GraphQL's contrasting "implementation gates ratification" RFC rule and LSP's capability-negotiation handshake (no reference impl at all) are covered as the other two ends of the spectrum, both with real drift incidents despite their mechanisms (Synapse/Dendrite federation break; graphql-java/Apollo `@defer` divergence).

- [Bluesky AT Protocol is the closest structural analog to PDP-Connect (spec plus PDS reference implementation plus app), and it is devolving protocol governance to neutral venues piecemeal (IETF for core sync, a Swiss Association for identity) rather than via one all-encompassing foundation, while keeping the reference PDS in the same monorepo as the spec's lexicons](lfdt-labs-prior-art/bluesky-atproto-is-devolving-protocol-governance-piecemeal-to-neutral-venues-while-keeping-the-reference-pds-in-its-own-monorepo.md) — 2026-08-14 — Lexicon versioning's bidirectional-validity rule ("old data valid under new schema AND new data valid under old schema," breaking changes require a new schema name, not a version bump) is the most directly transferable mechanism found for `data-connectors` pinning against `pdpp`. Home Assistant's nonprofit-owned core (Open Home Foundation, funded but not controlled by Nabu Casa) plus machine-checked, auto-downgradable Integration Quality Scale is a strong second precedent for connector-ecosystem tiering.

- [OpenTelemetry keeps its specification and per-language SIG implementations aligned without lockstep releases through three concrete, checkable mechanisms: a published per-feature compliance matrix distinguishing not-implemented from not-applicable, a stricter multi-language-prototype bar for stabilizing vs adding a spec feature, and machine-readable spec-version pins inside SIG build tooling](lfdt-labs-prior-art/opentelemetry-uses-a-published-per-language-compliance-matrix-plus-machine-readable-spec-version-pins-not-lockstep-releases-to-keep-sigs-aligned.md) — 2026-08-14 — Four-state (+/-/N-A/blank) compliance matrix, "1 prototype to go experimental, ~3 independent-language prototypes to stabilize" rule, and opentelemetry-python's checked-in `SEMCONV_VERSION` build variable are all directly copyable for a `pdpp`-vs-`data-connectors` compliance matrix and version pin.

- [Kubernetes' CNCF Conformance Program and the OpenID Foundation's OIDC Certification both run on self-certification against a shared open-source test suite with publicly reviewable results rather than third-party audits, and the Kubernetes API deprecation policy gives exact numeric alignment timelines that PDP-Connect can copy directly; the Terraform BSL relicensing and OpenTofu fork show a provider ecosystem survives a governance rupture mainly because the wire protocol stayed shared even as the registry and trust roots fragmented](lfdt-labs-prior-art/kubernetes-and-oidc-certification-programs-show-conformance-suites-work-as-alignment-mechanisms-only-when-self-certification-is-backed-by-a-published-audit-trail.md) — 2026-08-14 — K8s: beta APIs deprecated/removed "no more than 9 months or 3 minor releases (whichever is longer)" after introduction/deprecation; GA-endpoint-must-be-hit-by-conformance-tests-within-1-minor-version is a checked KEP release-signoff item. OpenTofu (LF-launched Sep 2023, GA Jan 2024, CNCF Apr 2025) kept the shared gRPC provider protocol working across both ecosystems but forked the registry (`registry.opentofu.org` vs `registry.terraform.io`) and the provider signing key — the wire contract survived, the trust/distribution layer didn't.

- [Public interactive API/protocol sandboxes cluster into three delivery models (shared multi-tenant mock, per-visitor ephemeral instance, local-run try-it), each forcing a different auth, state-reset, and abuse-mitigation architecture on the underlying app](developer-environments/public-api-sandbox-delivery-models-and-their-architecture-requirements.md) — 2026-08-14 — Stripe now offers anonymous, no-registration per-visitor sandbox creation via CLI (`stripe sandbox create`), the strongest precedent for a stranger-facing grant-scoped demo; Plaid/Petstore/Matrix instead share one mocked environment with fixed fixture data and account-level rate limits; MCP-specific research shows the July 2026 spec went stateless specifically to avoid the sticky-session/backpressure abuse surface that a public anonymous tool-calling endpoint creates, and real RCEs have already hit both a popular MCP OAuth-proxy and Anthropic's own Inspector.

- [Readability formulas (Flesch-Kincaid, Gunning Fog, SMOG, Coleman-Liau, Dale-Chall) measure lexical/syntactic surface proxies, not semantic or conceptual difficulty, and this gap is empirically documented, not just folk criticism](writing-craft/readability-formulas-measure-lexical-and-syntactic-surface-features-not-semantic-or-conceptual-difficulty.md) — 2026-08-14 — Bailin & Grafstein (2001) formalize the critique; Coh-Metrix (Graesser/McNamara 2004) was built to add cohesion/world-knowledge dimensions the formulas lack. O'Reilly & McNamara (2007) and Ozuru/Dempsey/McNamara (2009) found comprehension can invert relative to surface ease — the "reverse cohesion effect" — depending on reader domain knowledge, direct evidence that short-word/short-sentence text can still be conceptually hard. Consumer Health Vocabulary research found term recognition and conceptual understanding are separable even for lexically-simple medical terms. Gruteke Klein et al. (arXiv:2502.11150, 2025) used eye-tracking as ground truth and found traditional formulas, NLP methods, and frontier LLMs are all poor predictors of real reading ease versus simple psycholinguistic word-property baselines.

- [AI-generated PR/commit descriptions measurably misalign with the actual diff because agents reason locally (per-commit) rather than for a reader who lacks the agent's process context; no literature yet names this failure "context collapse" as a term of art](writing-craft/ai-generated-pr-descriptions-fail-because-agents-summarize-their-own-process-not-the-readers-missing-context.md) — 2026-08-14 — Two 2026 arXiv preprints measure the symptom directly: 23,247 agentic PRs found 406 (1.7%) high-inconsistency, dominated by "Phantom Changes" (45.4%, claims unimplemented work) with 51.7% lower acceptance and 3.5x longer merge time; a second study of 33,596 agentic vs 6,618 human PRs found agents beat humans at commit-level messages (0.72 vs 0.68 similarity) but lag at PR-level summarization (0.86 vs 0.88), because "commit message length is the strongest predictor of description quality" — agents reason per-commit, not over the full changeset for the reader. Anthropic's context-engineering docs name "falsely assumes shared context" as a documented failure, but only for humans prompting models, not models writing for humans — the inverse-direction fix ("write for a reader who wasn't in this conversation") is a reasonable inference, not yet a documented finding. The internal/pipeline-vocabulary-leak angle specifically (CI job names, tool names) turned up no dedicated source anywhere in this search — a real gap, not a search failure.

- [Rubber duck debugging, the Feynman Technique, and ELI5 are three independently-originated 'explain to a naive listener' forcing functions, only one of which (rubber ducking) is explicitly documented at its source](writing-craft/rubber-duck-feynman-technique-and-eli5-are-explain-to-an-outsider-forcing-functions.md) — 2026-08-14 — Rubber duck debugging traces cleanly to Hunt & Thomas's The Pragmatic Programmer (1999), corroborated by GitHub's own engineering blog. The "Feynman Technique" is folklore: Feynman never named or codified it; the 4-step version is a later popularization (chiefly Scott Young, ~2011), who himself calls it "only loosely based on" Feynman. ELI5 (r/explainlikeimfive, created by "bossgalaga" 2011) has the strongest academic grounding of the three — a dedicated discourse-analysis study (Pflugfelder) and an ACL 2019 NLP corpus paper (Fan et al., 270k threads) both identify self-contained, no-prior-knowledge answers with distinct linguistic AND explanatory (what-to-include) simplification as what makes ELI5 answers work. Only one direct prior adaptation into engineering writing was found (a blog post explicitly linking rubber-duck debugging to commit-message writing) — no established "ELI5 your PR" or "Feynman your PR" convention exists; a PR-description prompt pattern built on this would be a novel synthesis, not existing practice.

- [Self-hosted privacy-OSS peers (Synapse, Vaultwarden, Nextcloud) put the plain-language 'why this matters' explanation of a security/privacy fix in the PR body, GHSA advisory, or a dedicated changelog fragment — not in the commit title, and often not in the commit message body either](writing-craft/self-hosted-privacy-oss-peers-explain-security-fixes-in-the-pr-body-or-changelog-fragment-not-the-commit-title.md) — 2026-08-14 — Synapse's shipped security-fix commit titles are terse ("Anchor the ends of our path patterns to prevent path traversal vulnerabilities") with only `Fixes:` links in the body; the real plain-language impact statement lives in the public GHSA advisory ("this can lead to a bypass of reverse proxy rules, allowing unintended endpoints to be exposed") and, for ordinary bugfixes, in a required towncrier `changelog.d/<PR>.bugfix` fragment kept separate from the commit. Nextcloud's PR #60884 is the strongest found example of security-fix prose done well in a PR body: names the vulnerable line, explains "Remote Code Execution via PHP Object Injection" in plain terms with attacker-capability framing, shows before/after code, cites existing codebase precedent. Nextcloud's CONTRIBUTING.md also documents Conventional Commits plus an `Assisted-by: AGENT_NAME:MODEL_VERSION` AI-disclosure trailer distinct from DCO `Signed-off-by`. Bad-contrast examples confirmed real: Vaultwarden PR #7558 "Misc fixes and updates" bundles 4+ unrelated changes under one opaque title; Standard Notes PR #3029 "fix: Fixes hardware key authentication" has a null PR body on an auth code path.

- [Stripe and Linear treat documentation/changelog writing as reviewed product work with named process owners, not a post-hoc polish pass](writing-craft/stripe-and-linear-treat-docs-writing-as-reviewed-product-work-not-polish.md) — 2026-08-14 — Stripe: Documentation Manager Dave Nunez (primary 2020 interview) describes reviewing writing like code review, leverage-tiered review rigor, and a writing-mentor program; no published public style guide found. Linear: co-founder Karri Saarinen's own Medium post gives explicit changelog rules (1-3 features + a fixes bucket, screenshots, audience-matched voice); the Method site is a product-philosophy manifesto, not a style guide. Live-fetched changelogs show a real structural split — Stripe's API changelog is WHAT-only terse table rows, Linear's changelog is WHAT+WHY narrative entries with visuals — contradicting third-party claims that Linear's entries are "mostly one sentence."

- [Tufte's data-ink ratio, Carroll's minimalist instruction, and Sweller's cognitive load theory independently converge on the same rule: cut everything that doesn't carry meaning for the reader](information-design/tufte-carroll-and-sweller-converge-on-cutting-what-doesnt-carry-meaning-for-the-reader.md) — 2026-08-14 — Tufte (1983): data-ink ratio = 1.0 − (erasable proportion); five principles culminate in "above all else show the data," with documented empirical pushback on maximizing the ratio literally. Carroll's Nürnberg Funnel (1990): four principles (action-oriented, anchor in task domain, support error recognition/recovery, support reading to do/study/locate) came from field studies showing people skip reading and act first — his 25-card minimal manual beat a 94-page manual, reaching competency in half the time. Sweller's CLT: extraneous load (poor organization, redundant restatement) directly competes with working memory available for germane load; the redundancy effect shows restating the same fact in a second form costs coordination effort rather than reinforcing it. All three target the same failure mode — the writer has more context than the reader needs, and the job is subtraction, not addition.

- [Self-hosted OSS peer projects (Home Assistant, Immich, Paperless-ngx, Syncthing, n8n) reward plain-language causal PR descriptions over template compliance or jargon](writing-craft/self-hosted-oss-peer-projects-reward-plain-language-causal-prs-not-jargon-or-templates-alone.md) — 2026-08-14 — Real merged PRs from trusted maintainers often skip the narrative template entirely (`Fixes #30685`, nothing else) when the linked issue carries context, so template compliance is a weak proxy for clarity; the best examples (HA #179090 ESPHome key-identity bug, Syncthing #10842 deadlock fix) state the general failure mode before the specific trigger, define every internal term inline, and disclose uncertainty honestly. Immich, Home Assistant, and Paperless-ngx templates all now require explicit AI-disclosure fields — Paperless-ngx embeds a literal anti-slop verification token instruction ("ASLOP-PR-VERIFY") aimed at LLM-drafted PRs. n8n enforces a separate Angular-style `type(scope): summary` grammar on PR titles specifically because titles are mechanically extracted into the changelog.

- [Amazon (Bezos, 2004) and 3M (Gordon Shaw, 1998) independently banned bullet-point business writing because bullets let a writer state conclusions without ever stating the logic connecting them](writing-craft/amazon-and-3m-independently-banned-bullet-points-because-bullets-let-writers-hide-missing-logic.md) — 2026-08-14 — Bezos's 2017 shareholder letter: narrative memos "force better thought... how things are related" while bullets/slides "give permission to gloss over ideas... and ignore the interconnectedness of ideas," extending the ban to any bulleted Word doc, not just PowerPoint. 3M's Gordon Shaw (HBR, May-June 1998) found bullet-point strategic plans "usually aren't anything more than lists of 'good things to do'" because they "don't specify critical relationships between the points." Complements (not duplicates) the existing `inverted-pyramid-and-bluf-...` entry: that one covers conclusion-first ordering (AR 25-50/BLUF), this one covers prose-vs-bullets format and the "bullets hide missing logic" mechanism — independent axes.

- [The Linux kernel docs, tpope's 2008 post, and Chris Beams's seven rules are three distinct, independently verifiable primary sources for git commit-message conventions, each with its own stated rationale](writing-craft/linux-kernel-tpope-and-chris-beams-give-concrete-primary-source-rules-for-git-commit-messages.md) — 2026-08-14 — Kernel's `submitting-patches.rst` requires problem justification + user-visible impact + quantified trade-offs before technical detail, but has no 50/72 rule and no explicit "describe alternatives considered" instruction (a common miscitation). Tpope's 2008 tbaggery.com post (not the broken tpope.net mirror) is narrower than its reputation: just the 50/72 formatting convention plus imperative mood, justified by tool compatibility (rebase, log's non-wrapping pager, format-patch's email conversion) — no "why not what" content guidance. Beams's 2014 essay (cbea.ms/git-commit/, chris.beams.io now redirects there) explicitly distills prior convention rather than inventing it, and adds two real originals: the "if applied, this commit will ___" imperative-mood test and the explicit what/why/how three-way split crediting Peter Hutterer for "a diff tells you what, only the commit message tells you why."

- [Several domains separate a named consumption-cost rating (complexity/difficulty/effort) from the quality rating, and every crowd-sourced version of it is an acknowledged-subjective proxy, not a validated measurement](complexity-rating-systems/several-domains-separate-a-named-consumption-cost-rating-from-the-quality-rating.md) — 2026-08-14 — BGG Weight (1-5 Light-to-Heavy, crowd-voted, no official algorithm, community itself complains BGG conflates "weight"/learning-difficulty with "complexity"/decision-load) and Flesch/Flesch-Kincaid (1948 AP origin, 1975 US Navy grade-level variant, AHRQ/PubMed both formally document "does not measure comprehension") are the clearest cases. Ski trail colors (Disney-designed for a 1960s unbuilt resort, adopted industry-wide by 1968) are explicitly resort-relative, not globally calibrated. Kubernetes alpha/beta/GA is an adjacent but different axis — adoption-risk, not learning-effort. Hemingway/Grammarly both compute Flesch-family scores but pair the headline number with sentence-level highlights rather than showing it bare. BGG's own wiki pages 403'd automated fetch; claims rest on convergent secondary sources, flagged draft pending primary-page confirmation.

- [Google's style guides, Kernighan & Pike, and Ousterhout independently converge on the same rule: comments should explain why (or non-obvious what), not restate what the code already says, and Ousterhout gives the sharpest test — a comment at the same abstraction level as the code is useless](code-quality/comments-should-explain-why-not-what-across-google-kernighan-pike-and-ousterhout.md) — 2026-08-14 — Google's docguide, C++, and Python style guides plus PEP 8 all instruct "never describe the code" / comment only "tricky, non-obvious" parts, with PEP 8's canonical minimal pair (`# Increment x` bad vs. `# Compensate for border` good). Kernighan & Pike (*Practice of Programming* p.23-25) give "don't belabor the obvious" and "when the comment outweighs the code, the code probably needs fixing." Ousterhout's *A Philosophy of Software Design* Ch.13 ("Comments Should Describe Things that Aren't Obvious from the Code," §13.6 "Implementation comments: what and why, not how") gives the sharpest mechanism: comments must sit at a different abstraction level than the code (lower = precision, higher = intuition); same-level comments are redundant noise. Caveat: "why not what" is necessary but not sufficient — low-level facts the code can't self-express (units, null-handling, boundary inclusivity) are legitimate "what" comments that survive the test.

- [The curse of knowledge is a named, empirically-tested cognitive bias that Pinker identifies as the chief cause of bad technical writing, and its only well-evidenced fix is contact with real naive readers, not better imagining of them](writing-craft/curse-of-knowledge-is-a-named-empirically-tested-bias-with-known-but-only-partial-debiasing-fixes.md) — 2026-08-14 — Camerer, Loewenstein & Weber (1989, JPE) proved the bias in incentivized economic forecasting; Elizabeth Newton's 1990 Stanford tapper-listener study (popularized by Made to Stick) proved it in communication, with listeners guessing only 3 of 120 tapped songs against tappers' 50% prediction. Pinker's Sense of Style Ch. 3 calls it "the chief contributor to opaque writing" and warns that merely trying to imagine the reader doesn't work — his evidenced fixes are a real test reader and a content-frozen clarity-only revision pass. Hinds (1999) directly tested debiasing techniques on experts predicting novice difficulty and found experts resistant to them, while intermediate-expertise judges predicted best — the load-bearing implication being that asking the same expert/model to "imagine a naive reader" is the weakest fix in the literature.

- [Journalism's inverted pyramid and military/business BLUF independently converge on the same fix: put the conclusion first because the reader may stop at any point](writing-craft/inverted-pyramid-and-bluf-solve-the-same-problem-a-reader-who-may-stop-at-any-point.md) — 2026-08-14 — The inverted pyramid traces to 19th-century wire-service cost/reliability pressure (the "cut Civil War telegraph wire" origin is contested folklore per Poynter; Lincoln-assassination coverage, 1865, is the better-evidenced early exemplar) and lets editors cut a story from the bottom without rewriting it. AP's own internal guidance rejected a rigid Five-Ws checklist in favor of "clear, quick, complete, interesting." BLUF (Army Regulation 25-50) is explicitly documented as paralleling the inverted pyramid — deductive, conclusion-first structure — and pairs it with mandatory active voice for accountability. "Burying the lede" is the named, century-old failure mode for PR descriptions/commit messages that front-load gate-status trivia instead of what changed and why it matters.

- [Plain-language traditions treat the reader as someone who lacks the writer's context, and SEC Plain English shows a measured comprehension effect](writing-craft/plain-language-traditions-treat-the-reader-as-someone-who-lacks-the-writers-context-and-sec-plain-english-shows-a-measured-comprehension-effect.md) — 2026-08-14 — Zinsser's "clutter," Orwell's six rules (verbatim: no stale figures of speech, no long word where a short one will do, cut what can be cut, active over passive, no jargon/foreign phrase with an everyday equivalent, break these sooner than write barbarously), and the Plain Writing Act/plainlanguage.gov converge on writing the bridge from what the reader already knows to what they need, and on defining only genuinely necessary technical terms once in place rather than banning domain vocabulary. Best documented empirical link found is the SEC's 1998 Plain English rule: correlated with increased small-investor participation and an estimated ~$440K/year interest saving from a 75th→50th percentile readability improvement on a $430M bond — real but correlational disclosure-behavior evidence, not a controlled comprehension study.

- [High-craft engineering orgs converge on PR descriptions explaining why for a future reader, not restating the diff](writing-craft/high-craft-orgs-converge-on-pr-descriptions-explaining-why-for-future-readers-not-restating-the-diff.md) — 2026-08-14 — Google eng-practices, Shopify Engineering, and the Kubernetes contributor guide independently converge on near-identical doctrine: the diff shows what changed, the description's job is to capture why (rejected alternatives, decisions not visible in code) for "the next person that has to touch your code," not just today's reviewer. Shopify gives concrete title examples (bad: "Bug fix"; good: "Flag cross-DB transactions for Braintree remote events"). Chris Beams's seven-rules essay is the load-bearing ur-source Kubernetes's conventions trace to. No source gives a numeric length target — all scale detail to change complexity. Basecamp/37signals and Linear have no equivalent public "how we write PR descriptions" doctrine as of this search.

- [Google's tech writing course defines audience as a knowledge-gap formula and gives concrete instead-of-X-write-Y rules for jargon, abbreviations, and pronouns](writing-craft/googles-tech-writing-course-defines-audience-as-a-knowledge-gap-formula-and-gives-concrete-instead-of-x-write-y-rules.md) — 2026-08-14 — "good documentation = knowledge and skills your audience needs to do a task − your audience's current knowledge and skills," identified by role then proximity. Curse-of-knowledge framing ("experts forget novices don't know what you already know") extends to error messages read by someone with zero access to the engineering context, worked example: "server dropped your request... 92% CPU" rewritten as "So many people are shopping right now... we won't lose your shopping cart." Concrete rules: define/link terms on introduction, spell out acronyms on first use then parenthesize, and the style guide's word list bans e.g./i.e., authN/authZ, "aka" with prescribed replacements. Pronoun-antecedent guidance calls vague referents "the cognitive equivalent of a null pointer error."

- [SSH commit signing is the passwordless agent-native path while OpenPGP uses a bounded passphrase cache](developer-environments/ssh-commit-signing-is-the-passwordless-agent-native-path-while-openpgp-uses-a-passphrase-cache.md) — 2026-08-14 — GitHub verifies SSH signatures and recommends SSH as the simplest signing choice for most individuals; Git can sign through an existing SSH agent and verify locally with an allowed-signers file, while GnuPG's portable low-friction model is a bounded in-memory passphrase cache rather than permanent keychain storage.

- [Airbnb's user-facing data-export surface is well documented publicly, but no named employee is publicly tied to data portability/export specifically](data-portability/airbnb-data-export-surface-is-well-documented-but-no-named-employee-publicly-works-on-portability-specifically.md) — 2026-08-12 — Airbnb Privacy Engineering's Aziel Epilepsia and Fernando Rubio (named via a 2024 USENIX PEPR talk bio on consent management) are the best moderate-confidence WHO candidates, not confirmed as export-team-specific. Airbnb's DYD flow exports HTML/JSON/Excel across 30+ categories; Ireland's DPC reprimanded Airbnb in 2023 for over-collecting ID to verify DSAR requesters and for an incomplete first DSAR response. Ruled out: the PEPR'23 "Building Export Ecosystem" talk is Uber's, and DSAR researcher Arthur Borem (Data Transfer Initiative alum) has no Airbnb affiliation. One earlier AI-search claim (a "dual verification + expiring link" DSAR mechanism for reservation data) did not survive checking Airbnb's own Host Privacy Standards page and should be treated as unconfirmed.

- [Prysm terminal deposit contracts require canonical transition, current state, and replay-boundary proofs](distributed-systems/prysm-terminal-deposit-contracts-require-canonical-transition-current-state-and-replay-boundary-proofs.md) — 2026-08-12 — A safe post-genesis terminal-deposit exception is an explicit proof system, not a naked fixed count: prove the canonical terminal transition, current proxy/implementation state, finalized consensus count/root, and local cache consistency before and after ordinary replay; restore the cursor on strict failure and deploy only as a digest-pinned, non-signing, beacon-only canary with independent rollback.

- [Hermetic test scratch needs invocation ownership, immediate resource teardown, and bounded orphan recovery](testing/hermetic-test-scratch-needs-invocation-ownership-immediate-teardown-and-bounded-orphan-recovery.md) — 2026-08-12 — Node, Bazel, Go, pytest, Rust, systemd, and GitHub Actions converge on three ownership levels: unique per-resource directories with normal-path cleanup, one private command-scoped run root inherited by descendants, and a separate bounded reaper for verifiably abandoned roots. Hooks and `finally` do not cover SIGKILL; a supplied temp base is placement authority, not permission to delete arbitrary paths.

- [Vana A15 remains open because its local-control and failure-domain premises are unproven](validator-key-custody/vana-a15-remains-open-because-its-local-control-and-failure-domain-premises-are-unproven.md) — 2026-08-12 — Finality is effective-balance weighted, the proposed local controls do not yet prove that cloud administrators cannot recover disk access, and one shared signer database is only one topology. Treat hardened local keystores as the urgent interim rotation path while A15 remains open for privilege-graph, withdrawal-custody, and partitioned-signer testing.

- [Developer bootstrap should be explicit except inside a trusted managed environment because Git cannot activate tracked hooks and package managers are restricting install-time execution](repo-hygiene-and-secret-scanning/developer-bootstrap-should-be-explicit-except-inside-a-trusted-managed-environment.md) — 2026-08-12 — Git cannot activate tracked hooks without prior local configuration. npm/pnpm are adding install-script approvals, Yarn 4.14 disables postinstall by default and advises against it, and mise/direnv require trust. Use one explicit idempotent bootstrap command with CI as the mandatory backstop; automate that same command only inside selected Codespaces/devcontainers or organization-managed workstation policy.

- [Public X post research works best by combining search indexes and topic trackers with X's official oEmbed endpoint](search-infrastructure/x-post-research-works-best-by-combining-search-indexes-topic-trackers-and-official-oembed.md) — 2026-08-12 — Discover candidate post IDs through search-engine RSS/indexes and credible topic trackers, then verify known IDs through X's unauthenticated official oEmbed endpoint; Nitter-style mirrors are fragile and this method cannot guarantee complete reply discovery.

- [Pointer-transparent overlays require explicit descendant controls to preserve their interaction](product-design/pointer-transparent-overlays-require-explicit-descendant-controls.md) — 2026-08-11 — `pointer-events: none` makes an overlay container transparent to pointer targeting without removing keyboard focus. Scope it to the intentional overlay state and explicitly restore native child controls; never apply it to a non-overlay card whose cells need normal interaction.

- [Geth recovery must match sync mode and state scheme, while signer-bearing snapshots require an isolated data-only trust boundary](distributed-systems/geth-recovery-must-match-sync-mode-state-scheme-and-snapshot-trust-boundary.md) — 2026-08-11 — Use snap sync for disposable ordinary nodes; build full path archives with unlimited state history; recover legacy hash archives only under their source-compatible Geth/storage contract. Treat boot-disk snapshots that may contain validator keys as offline data sources: restore to an isolated helper, replay the filesystem journal on a disposable clone, mount read-only, copy only complete execution chaindata including the freezer, and require checksum and rollback gates.
- [Agentic mutation testing should be a risk falsification service, not a runner or score](testing/agentic-mutation-testing-should-be-a-risk-falsification-service-not-a-runner-or-score.md) — 2026-08-11 — The terminal ideal accepts changed code, historical fault classes, domain invariants, and plain-text risks; generates a small portfolio of conventional and domain-specific faults; routes each mutant to the smallest trustworthy test set; and requires an agent to kill, classify, or record the gap. Persist mutation packets and backstop receipts, optimize productive falsification per reviewer- and compute-minute, and treat StrykerJS as one adapter rather than the architecture.
- [PDPP mutation testing should target domain fault models and existing falsifiability harnesses before any generic mutation score](testing/pdpp-mutation-testing-should-target-domain-fault-models-and-existing-falsifiability-harnesses.md) — 2026-08-11 — PDPP already has broken-driver conformance tests, mutation-killing connector tests, real backend isolation, and a mock-path mutation checker. Start with cursor/checkpoint/coverage, auth/grant, projection/filter, backend-parity, and concurrency fault models; run capped shadow packets with focused tests plus a backstop; promote only measured, actionable operators.
- [Virtualizing all external effects of a black-box process is proven at the HTTP layer and at the syscall layer, but no tool provides one generic 'record-all-bindings, replay-all-bindings' driver architecture spanning HTTP, browser, subprocess, and wire protocols](testing/virtualizing-all-external-effects-of-a-black-box-process-is-proven-for-http-and-syscalls-but-has-no-generic-multi-protocol-driver-precedent.md) — 2026-08-14 — FoundationDB/Antithesis virtualize beneath every binding uniformly (deterministic hypervisor), structurally unavailable to PDPP since it can't boot a third-party provider inside a VM it controls. rr/Pernosco confirmed unreviewable/unscrubbable (raw kernel/CPU state, no text-diffable transcript) — closes that family off for evidence-sharing. Mountebank is the closest real multi-protocol-imposter precedent (HTTP/HTTPS/TCP/SMTP, one server, pluggable matchers); Traffic Parrot/Parasoft add JMS/MQ/JDBC commercially. Browser replay has no single-layer answer: WprGo (network/DNS-layer, fits PDPP's existing HTTP-capture design) vs. rrweb/Meticulous (DOM-layer, decouples from backend correctness — wrong tool for PDPP's "did the connector correctly parse the provider" goal, with confirmed canvas/multi-tab gaps). cli-mock/bats-mock directly validate PDPP's strict-unmatched-fails replay design for the subprocess binding. DB/wire-protocol has no VCR-equivalent (Testcontainers-real vs. pg-mem-with-gaps vs. GreenMail-style seedable fake server — never recorded-cassette replay). Feeds PDPP inbox/8-13-26-connector-dx-strategy.md and PR #140's browser-driver follow-up.
- [StrykerJS is viable for PDPP only as a narrow pilot because node:test plus tsx loses runner intelligence](testing/strykerjs-is-viable-for-pdpp-only-as-a-narrow-pilot-because-node-test-plus-tsx-loses-runner-intelligence.md) — 2026-08-11 — StrykerJS has mature TypeScript mutation, incremental reuse, and Vitest integration, but no official native `node:test` runner; its generic command runner reports no test detail, and JIT `tsx` multiplies compilation cost. Use one narrow command-runner/AOT feasibility spike with zero thresholds before considering a Vitest-backed pure-test island or any CI integration.
- [Mutation testing should be sampled, diff-aware, and risk-specific feedback, not a global adequacy gate](testing/mutation-testing-should-be-sampled-diff-aware-risk-specific-feedback-not-a-global-adequacy-gate.md) — 2026-08-11 — Google and Meta converge on selected, useful, risk-specific mutants rather than exhaustive adequacy. For AI-authored systems, mutants provide an external falsification goal when implementation, tests, fixtures, and mocks may share one mistaken model; each relevant survivor should become a killed test, an evidenced equivalent/uninteresting classification, or a recorded gap.
- [Cross-harness session ledgers should pair message-level SQLite search with explicit source-retention state](agentic-cli-design/cross-harness-session-ledgers-should-pair-message-level-sqlite-search-with-explicit-source-retention-state.md) — 2026-08-11 — CodeBurn, Sessions, Agent Sessions, GitHub Copilot CLI, and Hermes converge on local session discovery, message-level SQLite search, bounded context, and exact resume actions. If normalized text survives deletion of raw logs, the database is durable product data rather than a cache: record source presence, archive verification, retained-content policy, and completeness separately; never present a locator or derived summary as source truth.
- [An immutable reusable workflow can combine required lexical secret scanning with optional verification in a second job without a second caller](repo-hygiene-and-secret-scanning/immutable-reusable-workflow-can-combine-required-lexical-scanning-with-optional-verification.md) — 2026-08-11 — GitGuardian explicitly recommends layered local, CI, and remote scanning; TruffleHog documents verification as detector-optional rather than a substitute for detection. Keep complete-range lexical scanning required and offline, then use a same-workflow second job for optional chain liveness. A full caller SHA plus `job.workflow_repository`/`job.workflow_sha` binds both jobs and co-located policy to one immutable revision; the documented job-identity mechanism is unavailable on GHES.
- [Testing policy should use a tiny contributor and agent doorway, an on-demand canonical guide, and machine-enforced execution authority](agentic-context-design/testing-policy-should-use-a-tiny-doorway-an-on-demand-canonical-guide-and-machine-enforced-authority.md) — 2026-08-11 — Rust, Node, Django, LLVM, Git, and Kubernetes separate discoverable contributor entry points and canonical testing guidance from runner and CI authority. For PDPP, route test-related work from one concise AGENTS line to `docs/reference/testing-strategy.md`, while `test-accounting.manifest.json` and CI remain executable truth.
- [Venmo personal-data reads have no documented public API and unofficial mobile API clients hit device-trust and interactive-verification gates](pdpp/venmo-personal-api-is-unofficial-and-device-trust-gated.md) — 2026-08-11 — Official sources found here describe new-device sign-in verification and merchant checkout; an OSS client reports the exact OAuth2 403 family and device-identifier rejection. Keep raw password-grant/device-ID transport out of production and require owner-assisted browser proof.
- [Test-suite audits should optimize marginal behavioral and fault-detection value per cost, not test count, test LOC, or coverage percentage](code-quality/test-suite-audits-should-optimize-marginal-fault-detection-value-per-cost-not-test-count-or-coverage.md) — 2026-08-11 — Coverage identifies execution, sampled mutation probes oracle strength, and runtime/flake data exposes cost; none alone licenses deletion. Pruning requires behavioral traceability, independent truth sources for AI-authored tests/mocks, and a reversible validation experiment. Use affected-test selection only with measured miss rates and a complete backstop.
- [A pinned Gitleaks CLI is safer than gitleaks-action for shared organization workflows because the action requires a commercial organization license and can under-scan large pull requests](repo-hygiene-and-secret-scanning/direct-gitleaks-cli-is-safer-than-gitleaks-action-for-shared-org-workflows.md) — 2026-08-11 — Use the MIT Gitleaks CLI directly, pinned by version and asset checksum. At v3.0.0, gitleaks-action requires a separate organization license, defaults to an older CLI, and obtains PR commits with one unpaginated REST request before building a first-parent range; GitHub's endpoint returns only 30 commits by default. A reusable workflow can securely check out its co-located policy at `job.workflow_repository` + `job.workflow_sha`, so callers can pin one immutable 40-character workflow SHA without policy drift.
- [Vana smart-contracts PR 69's end-state diff misses secrets present only in intermediate pushed commits and misses unchanged neighboring secret context](repo-hygiene-and-secret-scanning/pr69s-end-state-diff-misses-transient-commit-secrets-and-unchanged-context.md) — 2026-08-11 — The PR correctly identifies pre-push as the last safe point for an OSS key leak, but its hook scans endpoint trees rather than every commit object being uploaded. Reproduced: add an inline key in commit A, remove it in B, and keyscan reports 0 candidates while gitleaks commit-range scanning reports 1. Its zero-context diff also cannot see unchanged neighboring lines, so adding only a fresh key value beneath an existing `privateKey` declaration produces 1 candidate and 0 findings. The chain verifier is disabled in pre-push mode and CI is advisory.
- [No industry-named pattern exists for CI inferring absent local pre-commit hooks purely from its own scan findings, but vendors universally frame pre-commit + CI as preventive-vs-compensating layers with an explicit bypassability rationale; git signing/notes/push-options are never used as machine-configuration-compliance proxies; SLSA/SSDF name developer-endpoint requirements only loosely; MDM-enforced core.hooksPath has no found real-world example](secret-scanning-drift-detection/no-named-differential-detection-pattern-but-preventive-vs-compensating-control-framing-is-industry-standard.md) — 2026-08-10 — GitGuardian/Gitleaks/community sources consistently frame pre-commit as bypassable-preventive and CI/pre-receive as mandatory-enforcement/compensating, justified explicitly by `--no-verify` bypassability — but none make the further inferential leap of treating a CI finding as proof the hook was specifically absent (vs. outdated, bypassed, or ruleset-gap). `commit.template` is documented as purely advisory/unenforced. `user.signingkey` is authorship/identity only, never a machine-configuration signal. `git push -o` is real (GitLab routes custom push options to server-side hooks) but no real registration-for-compliance use was found. SLSA's "verified history" and two-person review apply to the source platform's record-keeping, not developer endpoints; SLSA explicitly excludes workstations from the build track. NIST SSDF PO.1.1 names "development endpoints" as in-scope but is deliberately tool-agnostic — no requirement ID names pre-commit hooks specifically. No MDM-pushed `core.hooksPath` example found, though the identical pattern is documented for other tools (e.g., Claude Code's Jamf/Kandji/Intune-pushed managed-settings.json).
- [The post-Readarr community is converging on separate acquisition and library layers, not one settled replacement](self-hosting/post-readarr-community-is-converging-on-separated-acquisition-and-library-layers-not-one-replacement.md) — 2026-08-10 — Recent r/selfhosted/rhomelab discussion has no agreed replacement: Shelfarr/Shelfmark/Chaptarr split request and acquisition from Calibre/Audiobookshelf-style library ownership; Bookshelf remains the closest Readarr-style manager but has much weaker current development momentum.
- [Institutional Ethereum staking operators split on validator-signing-key custody: Coinbase and Kiln run remote signers (Web3Signer/custom) with a shared anti-slashing DB, Web3Signer itself supports fetching keys from Vault/Azure/AWS (no manual export/import required), but Attestant/AWS-Cubist both independently confirm a single remote signer is a real SPOF that requires clustering (Dirk) or threshold signing (DVT) to fully mitigate](validator-key-custody/remote-signer-vs-keystore-on-host-institutional-eth-staking-2026-08.md) — 2026-08-10 — AWS KMS/GCP KMS cannot sign BLS12-381 (AWS's own Cubist blog: HSM firmware lacks BLS support, NIST-alignment gap). Coinbase Institutional (Nov 2025) confirms remote signer + high-watermark for ETH validators; a separate, earlier Coinbase post about shared beacon nodes is a different problem and should not be conflated. Web3Signer fetches keys from HashiCorp Vault/Azure Key Vault/AWS Secrets Manager (no per-use human export/import) and supports multi-instance shared-Postgres slashing protection with DB locking — Kiln ran this at ~100k-validator scale on Holesky and published latency-tuning learnings. Attestant's own founder and AWS/Cubist engineers independently call a single remote signer a real SPOF — but their fix is clustering (multiple Dirk instances) or DVT, not reverting to local keystores. Kraken fully deployed SSV DVT across all its ETH validators; EF staked 72k ETH via Obol DVT-lite in March 2026; MigaLabs found <1% performance difference vs traditional validators. No numeric industry-standard "keys per host" cap found. Sigma Prime/Lighthouse's stated caution on remote signing could not be verified verbatim (source blocked 403) — flagged unverified.
- [Gmail's documented IMAP limits are an account-wide daily bandwidth cap (2500 MB/day download) and a 15-simultaneous-connection ceiling, not a per-connection throughput allowance — so multi-connection fan-out for attachment FETCH is unproven and connection-count ceilings fail as a hard lockout, not a graceful slowdown](connectors/gmail-imap-throttling-is-documented-as-account-wide-bandwidth-not-per-connection-so-multi-connection-fanout-is-unproven-mitigation.md) — 2026-08-09 — Google Workspace bandwidth-limits doc frames usage in account-aggregate terms and explicitly warns multi-client access multiplies bandwidth use, circumstantial evidence against a per-connection allowance. 15-connection consumer Gmail ceiling (support.google.com); historical Mozilla bug documents a 10-connection trip triggering a full 24h IMAP lockout, not throttling; Chatwoot GitHub issue #6082 documents a real production account-bandwidth-exceeded disconnect. No authoritative source (Google docs, imapflow issues, other OSS Gmail sync tools) confirms N connections yield N× throughput — open question, recommend empirical validation with an instant-abort path before committing to the pattern.
- [Airbyte's connector-acceptance-test package has been removed from the live repo (relocated/deprecated by airbyte-ci), while Meltano's Singer SDK ships a live, named, subclassable test-template suite; Singer's 'any tap + any target' interoperability promise is documented-broken by version-pinning conflicts, not just folklore](connectors/airbyte-cat-and-singer-sdk-primary-source-verification-2026-08-07.md) — 2026-08-07 — `gh api` confirms `airbyte-integrations/bases/` on current master has no acceptance-test package (only base/base-java/base-normalization); the docs describing TestSpec/TestBasicRead/etc. are versioned/frozen (1.8), not current. Meltano SDK's `singer_sdk.testing` has confirmed real class names (TapDiscoveryTest, StreamPrimaryKeysTest, 6 named Attribute*Test classes). Singer interop failure verified via live GitHub issue singer-python#102 (pip dependency conflicts across tap/target pairs), not just blog claims. Airbyte certification promotion criteria is usage-based (workspace count) per 2023 blog, not purely a test-pass gate; current 4-tier docs don't document any promotion process at all.
- [H-E-B's own help docs describe passkey setup as account-settings-initiated, not an automatic post-login prompt, and this prompt class is commonly a native WebAuthn dialog rather than a page element](connectors/heb-passkey-post-login-prompt-is-undocumented-and-likely-a-native-webauthn-dialog-not-a-page-element.md) — 2026-08-07 — no fixture or captured DOM exists for H-E-B's real post-OTP passkey interstitial; safe automated dismissal blocked pending a real fixture capture.
- [Third-party integration directories (Home Assistant, n8n, simple-icons) vendor small flat SVGs of others' brand logos under a nominative-fair-use/identification-only posture, and treat a deterministic monogram+color as the correct default state, not an edge case](connectors/third-party-brand-icons-for-integration-directories-vendor-svg-under-nominative-fair-use-monogram-fallback-is-the-safe-default.md) — 2026-08-07 — HA's brands repo is one centralized CDN-served repo (core/custom_integrations by domain), 404s cleanly on missing icons, and as of 2026.3.0 lets custom integrations bundle their own icon checked before the central CDN fallback. simple-icons' SVGs are CC0 but its own LICENSE explicitly excludes trademarks from that waiver, and it runs a formal brand-owner takedown channel — proof takedowns are planned-for, not hypothetical. n8n is decentralized/per-author with no licensing review. Nominative fair use covers "we support connecting to X" identification without endorsement, but courts scrutinize actual logo use more than word-mark use. Monogram+deterministic-color fallback (GitHub, Slack, DiceBear, shadcn Avatar, Uber Base) is universal and treated as first-class, not degraded.
- [Self-hosted, user-owned software projects (Home Assistant, n8n, Immich, Nextcloud) universally require the operator to register their own OAuth app with the provider, but deliver the resulting client_id/secret via a DB-backed UI settings page with no restart, not env-var-only config](self-hosted-oauth-patterns/self-hosted-projects-deliver-byo-oauth-app-credentials-via-ui-settings-page-not-env-var-plus-restart.md) — 2026-08-07 — Home Assistant's Application Credentials, n8n self-hosted's OAuth2 credentials UI, Immich's Authentication Settings, and Nextcloud's OAuth2 provider/user_oidc admin UI all use runtime-editable, DB-backed settings with immediate effect. Jellyfin's SSO plugin is the sole outlier requiring a restart, and is a documented complaint about Jellyfin's plugin architecture, not a deliberate pattern. n8n's "credential overwrites" is the only genuine env-var path, and it's an admin convenience layered on the UI, not a replacement. Google classifies Calendar/Contacts as "sensitive" (lighter tier, no CASA audit) and exempts personal-use apps from verification; the real gotcha for unverified apps is the 7-day refresh-token expiry, not the 100-test-user cap.
- [EU Data Act (Regulation 2023/2854) Article 2(12) user definition, Article 5(1) third-party sharing right, and the two application dates (12 Sept 2025 general; 12 Sept 2026 for connected products placed on market) are all verified against primary/authoritative sources](regulatory-verification/eu-data-act-article-2-12-5-1-and-application-dates-verified-2026-08-07.md) — 2026-08-07 — Article 2(12) defines "user" as a natural or legal person owning/renting a connected product or receiving related services. Article 5(1) is the user-directs-data-holder-to-third-party flow (the same triangle as PDPP's owner/source/client). Application is two-tier: general application from 12 September 2025; the Article 3(1) design-by-default obligation is postponed by Article 50 to connected products placed on the market on or after 12 September 2026. WebFetch against eur-lex.europa.eu truncates at the preamble and cannot reach operative articles regardless of prompt — verification required pivoting to SearXNG search + secondary full-text mirrors (data-act-law.eu) and law-firm client alerts (Eversheds Sutherland).
- [Bandcamp as a music collection data source for PDPP connectors: reverse-engineered session-cookie API access, no official API, passive ToS tolerance for personal use](music-data-sources/bandcamp-connector-data-access-research-2026-08-07.md) — 2026-08-07 — Bandcamp has no official public API but provides stable reverse-engineered session-cookie-based access to collection, wishlist, and purchase history via `https://bandcamp.com/api/usercollection/1` (documented by 5+ community tools: fsck/bandcamp-collection-manager, donhamilton/bandcamp-collection-downloader, ihatemusic/bandcamp-discography). Session cookies obtained by manual browser extraction; no OAuth, no API keys. Data completeness: HIGH (full purchase history with dates/prices, wishlist, followed artists). ToS permits personal/non-commercial use (passive tolerance established, no takedowns of community tools in 5+ years). Main constraints: authentication UX (cookie extraction vs OAuth), no API stability guarantees, cookie expiration handling undefined. Rate limits: unpublished but inferred ~10-100 req/s from community practice. Recommendation: FEASIBLE for Wave 2+ after OAuth-based music sources due to UX friction and maintenance burden. Historical depth complete since account creation; deletion history not tracked.

- [Documentation-site header navs converge on a compact search button/pill that opens a Cmd/Ctrl-K overlay, never a full always-expanded text input](product-design/header-nav-search-converges-on-compact-button-overlay-not-full-input.md) — 2026-08-04 — Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Next.js, React, Stripe, MCP-via-Mintlify all use a small pill/button trigger (never a full-width always-expanded input) opening an overlay modal on click or Cmd-K/Ctrl-K; header-level hint text observed was always ⌘K/Ctrl K, never "/" (that shows up more in sidebar widgets like Docusaurus). Likely converged via Algolia DocSearch's default integration shape. Direct-fetch limited for MCP/Stripe/Vercel (serve markdown to fetch tools); Tailwind + Next.js most directly confirmed.
- [Tabbed install-command blocks in developer docs should derive commands from one input and persist the tab choice, and the strongest examples strip everything except tabs plus one command](product-design/tabbed-install-command-blocks-derive-commands-and-persist-tab-choice.md) — 2026-08-05 — Turborepo tabs pnpm/yarn/npm/bun; RetroUI PR #63 replaced hand-authored multiline blocks with one prop-based component that DERIVES the variants (hand-authoring N snippets per page drifts). Persist tab choice in localStorage — `ni` does the filesystem equivalent by scanning lockfiles. opencode.ai is the visual reference: one bordered panel, plain-text tabs above a thin rule, ONE command, no heading and no lead-in sentence; active tab brighter with underline inset to label width, and only the meaningful token (the domain) brightened while boilerplate flags dim. Caveat: selective emphasis only works when exactly one token matters — dimming flags would harm a command whose flags ARE the point.
- [Syntactic/rhetorical AI-tells are empirically attested at the syntax-tree level, not just vocabulary](writing-craft/syntactic-and-rhetorical-ai-tells-are-empirically-attested-at-the-syntax-tree-level-not-just-vocabulary.md) — 2026-08-04 — Reinhart et al. (PNAS 2025): participial clauses 2-5x human rate, nominalization 1.5-2x, passive voice HALVED for GPT-4o (contra folk belief); tells are house-specific not universal (Llama-3 vs GPT-4o diverge oppositely). Juzek & Ward: quantified overused nouns (reliance +3194%, generalizability +3124%) via RLHF-rater preference. Liang et al.: 61.3% false-positive rate on non-native-speaker essays, mechanistically caused not coincidental. "Style-words-only" claim was right about Wikipedia/Kobak but wrong about the wider syntax-tree literature.
- [No shipped anti-slop tool does per-project corpus-relative vocabulary detection — fixed word lists dominate](writing-craft/no-shipped-anti-slop-tool-does-per-project-corpus-relative-vocabulary-detection-fixed-word-lists-dominate.md) — 2026-08-04 — Surveyed 15+ GitHub tools, Vale styles, GPTZero/Binoculars/DetectGPT/Pangram; every prose tool uses a fixed universal list. Pangram has strongest independent accuracy verification; GPTZero has largest claim-vs-reality gap + active litigation; DetectGPT collapses under paraphrasing. ehmo/slopkit self-ranks #1 with no independent judge while sitting at 45 stars vs competitors' 15k-33k+.
- [slopkit's benchmark honesty is real but localized; its decoy-rejection harness is the part worth stealing](writing-craft/slopkit-benchmark-honesty-is-real-but-localized-and-its-adversarial-decoy-harness-is-the-part-worth-stealing.md) — 2026-08-04 — BENCHMARKS.md's withdrawn-win-rate self-criticism is code-verified real, but sibling slopgent benchmark has a hardcoded PRIMARY="slopgent" win-required gate despite real blinding. decoy_rejection.py's must_flag-with-required-reason pattern is the highest-value steal for judge.mjs, which currently only self-tests against its own author-written fixtures. Fact-preservation check is regex entity-diffing only, no semantic layer — misses prose-level caveat drops.
- [GraphQL vs REST API naming conventions across Stripe/Linear/Plaid/Vercel converge on kebab-case paths and consistent namespacing; no consensus on versioning (URL path vs header)](api-contract-design/graphql-vs-rest-api-naming-conventions-stripe-linear-plaid-vercel.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe: `/v{version}/{domain}/{resource}` with snake_case names; Linear: GraphQL `/graphql` with camelCase fields; Plaid: `/accounts`, `/link`, `/products/{product}` kebab-case; Vercel: `/v{version}/` with kebab-case. No industry consensus on versioning placement (URL path, headers, or schema-driven). Choose REST with `/v{version}/{resource}` for new systems; adopt GraphQL single-endpoint for existing Federated schema contexts.
- [Better-sqlite3 remains the practical choice for Node.js SQLite over node:sqlite (experimental) and legacy node-sqlite3 (SIGSEGV crashes); node:sqlite stable only in Node 22.5+, lacks pool support, and has lower latency but higher crash risk](backend-frameworks/better-sqlite3-vs-node-sqlite-choice-2026-08.md) — 2026-08-04 — better-sqlite3: production-proven, synchronous, zero external deps, ~2.2M weekly downloads. node:sqlite (Node 22.5+): experimental, no pooling, documented stability issues. node-sqlite3 (legacy): recurring SIGSEGV crashes ("RowToJS"/"Work_AfterAll" callbacks). Migration from sqlite3 to better-sqlite3 is straightforward for blocking I/O; async patterns require refactoring. For new projects: better-sqlite3.
- [DeepSeek-V3 on Replicate specifies max_tokens 2–20480 (default 4096); conservative for most use cases is 4096–8192 tokens; 16384 justified only for long documentation/code analysis; token-based billing amplifies cost impact](web-standards/deepseek-v3-replicate-api-parameters-max-tokens.md) — 2026-08-04 — DeepSeek-V3 Replicate input dict parameter `{"max_tokens": value, ...}`. Default 4096; most coding tasks need only 4096–8192. Setting to 16384 increases token ceiling 16× over default; costs scale accordingly. Configure via Python client input dict; no negotiation per-request.
- [ONNX Runtime's default intra-op thread pool claims all physical cores per session, so raising JS-level concurrency for transformers.js/onnxruntime-node embed() calls causes N-squared core oversubscription unless intraOpNumThreads is capped](llm-serving/onnxruntime-default-intra-op-threads-cause-oversubscription-under-js-concurrency.md) — 2026-08-09 — default `intraOpNumThreads=0` sizes ORT's intra-op pool to physical core count per session (docs confirmed); transformers.js's pipeline() exposes `session_options.intraOpNumThreads` but does not set a non-default value itself; documented mitigation is capping to `~cores/N` or using ORT's shared/global thread pool when running N concurrent sessions in one process.
- [KV cache quantization methods (q4_0 post-training vs SVD/low-rank compression) show accuracy/compression tradeoffs: post-training loses ~0.5–1% accuracy while SVD preserves ~99% accuracy at 4× compression but requires calibration](llm-serving/kv-cache-quantization-methods-qz-vs-svd-vs-low-rank-2026-08.md) — 2026-08-04 — Post-training q4_0–q6_0: shipped default, 3–4× compression, 0.5–1% accuracy loss. SVD/low-rank (KVarN, Lora-KV): 99% accuracy at 4× compression but needs per-model calibration or pretraining. Current frontier (Qwen 3.6, 24GB): q5_0 K / q4_1 V split. Further gains require retraining.

- [Testnet faucet design: OIDC attestation for CI, registration-gated wallets, idempotency keys, KMS-backed signing, and audit ledgers avoid CAPTCHA abuse and arbitrary-recipient attacks](system-design/testnet-faucet-design-oidc-attestation-idempotency-key-custody.md) — 2026-08-04 — GitHub Actions OIDC workload identity (short-lived tokens, non-renewable, 15 min default), Stripe idempotency keys (exactly-once, ~24h TTL), KMS-backed signing (per-request, sub-second latency), HSM vs KMS tradeoffs (HSM for production hotwallets, KMS for internal testnet), ERC-4337 paymaster complexity (v2, not v1), registration-gated wallets, append-only audit ledgers tied to OIDC claims, multi-granularity token-bucket rate limiting, hard-coded testnet chain-ID allowlists at two layers. Non-goals: public faucet features, CAPTCHA, multi-party approval, paymasters, mainnet path. Canonical architecture: OIDC authN → wallet registration → rate limiting → KMS signing → idempotency → audit ledger.
- [Claude Code hooks schema: PostToolUse stdin fields (tool_name, tool_input, tool_response), matcher syntax (Edit|Write regex), blocking semantics (exit 0 = success, no JSON = proceed unchanged), Stop receives last_assistant_message for AI-ism detection, environment vars (CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR, CLAUDE_EFFORT) available to hook scripts](claude-code-hooks-schema-2026-08-04.md) — 2026-08-04 — Official verified schema from https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md. PostToolUse stdin: tool_name (string), tool_use_id (UUID), tool_input (tool-specific, Write/Edit have file_path+content/new_string fields), tool_response (result object). Matcher filters by tool name: "Edit|Write" regex, "*" all tools, no event-level filtering. PostToolUse exit codes: 0=success parse stdout for JSON, 2=non-blocking error show stderr, other=first-line stderr. JSON stdout: decision:"block" prevents Claude from seeing result (rare, tool already ran), updatedToolOutput replaces tool_response (redaction), additionalContext injects system reminder (warnings). Stop fires end-of-turn with full turn summary + last_assistant_message (full text, tokens count), can block turn completion (decision:"block" + reason prevents stop). Stop exit 2 is blocking, reason frames as problem, additionalContext as non-blocking state update. Environment: $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR, $CLAUDE_EFFORT (low/medium/high/xhigh/max), $CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE, $CLAUDE_CODE_BRIDGE_SESSION_ID. Stop is suitable for AI-ism detection; PostToolUse for per-tool redaction/validation.


- [Cloudflare Workers KV rate limiting is per-account with degraded service (not blocking) on limit; The Graph and Goldsky endpoints have different authentication, rate limiting, and query-count mechanics](api-platforms/cloudflare-workers-kv-rate-limiting-and-the-graph-goldsky-api-comparison.md) — 2026-08-04 — Cloudflare KV free tier: 1,000 daily writes per account (per-account level, affects all namespaces), unlimited reads; quota exceeded degrades service (not 429 HTTP), requires try-catch for resilience. The Graph: 100,000 queries/month free, always requires API key (even free tier), $4 per 100k paid. Goldsky: optional auth, Bearer token for private access, 50 requests/10 seconds (upgradeable). GraphQL query format 100% compatible across providers; provider switching is URL + auth change, no client logic change. For high-traffic gateways: The Graph metered pricing ($4/100k) more predictable; Goldsky's fixed rate limits fit burst-heavy CI. Multi-provider support enables downtime insurance.

- [High-confidence alerts only avoid alert fatigue; arbitrary statistical thresholds without model confidence are false positives](product-design/data-quality-alerting-high-confidence-only.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe Radar, Plaid, Amplitude converge on two-stream alert classification: high-confidence alerts (≥0.7 or definitive mismatch, surfaced as warnings) vs low-confidence signals (0.3–0.7, audit-log only or suppressed). Stripe: "Businesses overemphasize false negatives and underemphasize false positives" — arbitrary thresholds ("count > 50") breed alert fatigue. Replace heuristic-only thresholds with model-reported confidence (e.g., regionConfidence < 0.3). Definitive signals: zero regions after derivation. Statistical anomaly detection requires 95–99% confidence intervals. False-positive rates (35%+) break reliability; false positives train users to ignore warnings. Implementation: type defs expose model confidence, replace arbitrary heuristics with model-backed signals.

- [Persistent= only affects OnCalendar= timers, not monotonic timers like OnStartupSec= or OnBootSec=](systemd/systemd-timer-persistent-only-oncalendar.md) — 2026-08-04 — systemd.timer(5) spec: Persistent= ("store last-trigger time on disk, replay missed runs") explicitly states "only has an effect on timers configured with OnCalendar=." Monotonic timers (OnBootSec, OnStartupSec, OnUnitActiveSec, OnUnitInactiveSec) ignore Persistent=. Catch-up mechanism for monotonic timers is the "already-in-past → fire immediately once" rule, not Persistent=. OnStartupSec= anchor moves on per-user manager restart (relative to manager start time, not system boot), enabling restart-time recovery. OnUnitInactiveSec= explicitly excluded from fire-immediately-once rule, so it recurs and survives restarts. Common mistake: attributing Persistent= behavior to monotonic timers in docs/tests; false recovery claim breaks when Persistent= is actually a no-op.
- [W3C TRs pin document identity to a fixed publish date via URI structure and freeze-after-publish rules; RFCs are immutable and superseded rather than edited](spec-versioning/w3c-tr-and-rfc-versioning-and-dating-conventions.md) — 2026-08-05 — W3C exposes "this version" (immutable, dated URI) vs "latest published version" (redirect) — the date is structurally part of the URI, and publish tooling refuses future/stale dates. Editors must not edit after the title-page date. Transferable idea for repo-hosted specs: a hand-typed header Date field with no mechanical check against actual last-content-change (e.g. git history) reproduces exactly the failure mode both standards bodies engineer against. Recommends showing both "published vX.Y.Z" (immutable milestone) and "content updated <date>" (honest signal) rather than one collapsed field.

- [Anonymous-first submission flows use email as the sole identity marker, creating implicit accounts on first submission without signup friction](b2b-saas-invitation/anonymous-first-submissions-email-identified-guest-pattern.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe, PayPal, Shopify converge: guest checkout reduces abandonment 30% vs. forced signup; email is the identity (no password, no account creation upfront); implicit account created on first submission; email verification is optional for first submission, required for payment/dashboard/referrals; returning users auto-linked by email; progressive profiling collects additional info over time. Enumeration protection: time-limited single-use tokens, rate-limit sends, never reveal email existence. Open: duplicate-submission gating (tax year check vs. free resubmit), when to offer dashboard upgrade.

- [Claude Code hook input includes agent_id and agent_type fields to distinguish subagent workers from interactive sessions](claude-code-subagent-hooks/hook-input-schema-agent-type-field-for-subagent-detection.md) — 2026-08-04 — Official Claude Code hook input JSON includes `agent_id` (present only in subagent/Task-tool context, absent in orchestrator/interactive) and `agent_type` (agent name when `agent_id` present). This is the only documented way to distinguish subagent from interactive in hooks. Headless mode detection (`claude -p` vs interactive) is currently undocumented with no supported method. `CLAUDECODE=1` env var set in all Claude Code subprocesses, does not distinguish context.

- [Selection removal UX patterns converge on modifier keys (Alt+drag) with context menus and cursor feedback, following Photoshop/Figma standards](product-design/selection-removal-ux-patterns-photoshop-figma-eraser-mode-context-menu.md) — 2026-08-04 — Professional editors (Photoshop, Figma, Adobe Illustrator) use Alt+drag for subtract/erase mode with cursor change (minus sign or eraser) for visual feedback. Right-click context menus with "Remove" actions are discoverable standard. Desktop uses modifier keys + cursor feedback + menu hints; mobile requires explicit mode toggle or gesture-based equivalent. Keyboard shortcuts (Delete/Backspace) on selected region complement the pattern. Enumeration attack prevention and modal state management are implementation details that do not change the interaction model.

- [Mobile dashboard navigation uses collapsible hamburger sidebars with progressive disclosure; Auth.js v5 auto-detects Vercel URLs without explicit NEXTAUTH_URL, eliminating configuration drift](product-design/mobile-dashboard-navigation-and-auth-js-vercel-url-handling.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe, Plaid, Toptal survey converge on hamburger nav (mobile) + persistent sidebar (desktop), card layouts for small screens, 44px+ tap targets in thumb zone. Auth.js v5 auto-detects from request headers; explicit NEXTAUTH_URL overrides and breaks preview deployments. AUTH_SECRET is the only required var. For app-level URLs (emails, referrals), derive from VERCEL_PROJECT_PRODUCTION_URL (production) and VERCEL_URL (preview) at runtime; maintain no separate APP_URL env var (configuration drift risk).

- [Schema-based document extraction converges on type-specific parsers, JSONPath-style filtering, and multi-layer fingerprinting for deduplication](api-contract-design/schema-based-document-extraction-converges-on-type-per-document-jsonpath-filtering-and-multi-layer-fingerprinting.md) — 2026-08-04 — Plaid, Stripe, Azure reject generic extraction for schema-per-document-type (W-2, 1099, passport, 1040).
