Output filtering uses JSONPath include/exclude (e.g., `"**.ssn"` redaction). Multi-layer fingerprinting: exact SHA256 (duplicate detection), fuzzy ssdeep (OCR variation), identity hash (same person cross-year), person hash (cross-submission). Filter layer post-extraction so LLM still validates sensitive fields before redacting output.

- [Name effects on professional outcomes: hiring callbacks, career advancement, and pronunciation bias span 30+ years with no improvement trend](hiring-discrimination-and-name-bias/name-effects-on-professional-outcomes-hiring-callbacks-and-career-advancement-meta-analysis.md) — 2026-08-04 — Bertrand-Mullainathan (2004): white-sounding names receive 50% more callbacks (10% vs 6.7%), equivalent to 8 years of experience. Quillian meta-analysis (2023, 97 studies, 1989-2020): 36-40% callback disadvantage for African American-sounding names, **completely unchanged over 30+ years** despite decades of anti-discrimination law. Discrimination is larger at job-offer stage (128% advantage for majority applicants). Laham (2012): attorneys with pronounceable names advance faster; pronunciation difficulty affects social judgment. Core finding: name-based hiring discrimination is robust, replicated, persistent, and empirically among the largest and most stable sources of labor market inequality.

- [Web3.py and viem handle Solidity bytes parameters differently—web3.py expects raw bytes while viem expects hex strings, causing keccak256 hash mismatches in contract invocations](web-standards-protocols/web3-bytes-encoding-web3py-vs-viem-interoperability.md) — 2026-08-04 — viem accepts hex strings for `bytes` parameters and internally converts them to bytes; web3.py expects Python `bytes` objects directly. Attempting to "match" viem's hex encoding by pre-converting in web3.py causes the contract to receive different bytes, producing divergent keccak256 hashes for the same operation ID. Testing both clients together is essential. Practical rule: encode per library's semantics, not per output format.

- [AI logo prompting avoids generic corporate design via reference constraint, negative direction, and design-system anchoring](image-generation-and-design/ai-logo-prompting-avoids-generic-corporate-design-via-reference-constraint-and-negative-direction.md) — 2026-08-04 — Designer reference anchoring (Vignelli, Rand, Bauhaus), negative prompts blocking clichés (gradients, shadows, padlocks), geometric constraints (honeycomb, interlocking circles), and monochromatic palettes suppress algorithmic convergence to median corporate output. Tested across DALL-E, Midjourney, Kittl, stockimg.ai with consistent results. Protocol: primary prompt + negative companion + 3–4 iterations + post-generation refinement (15–20%). Open: optimal reference count, model-family variance, legal threshold for transformation.

- [HeroUI semantic color tokens (background, foreground, content1-4, default, primary, etc.) are referenced as Tailwind CSS variables with semantic prefixes](heroui-theming/heroui-semantic-color-tokens-and-tailwind-class-usage.md) — 2026-08-04 — HeroUI provides pre-defined semantic tokens (background, foreground, content1-4, default, primary, secondary, success, warning, danger) accessed as Tailwind utilities (bg-content1, text-foreground, border-primary). Content hierarchy mirrors nested UI patterns (background → content1 → content4). CSS variables automatically scoped to `:root` (light) and `.dark` (dark mode) enabling theme switching without code changes. Differs from generic Tailwind v4 in providing out-of-the-box tokens optimized for component composition and accessible contrast.

- [CVE-2025-66478 is a CVSS 10.0 RCE in Next.js <15.0.5 via insecure deserialization with near-100% exploit reliability; actively exploited in the wild](web-security/cve-2025-66478-nextjs-rce-via-insecure-deserialization-affects-15-0-4-patched-15-0-5.md) — 2026-08-04 — Next.js 15.0.4 vulnerable; patched in 15.0.5. CVSS 10.0, unauthenticated, no code changes needed. React Server Components deserialization flaw exploited in production. Related to CVE-2025-55182 (React RSC RCE) and Shai-Hulud 2.0 npm worm. Vercel fix tool available (`fix-react2shell-next`). Immediate upgrade required for affected deployments.

- [Bottom sheet mobile UX patterns, document review flows, and wizard steppers converge on sequential task focus — PDF dominates on mobile, controls collapse to tabs/bottom panels, CTA placement depends on context](table-ui-design/bottom-sheet-mobile-ux-document-review-wizard-pattern.md) — 2026-08-04 — Wise, NN/g, Plaid, Material Design converge: mobile document review should separate PDF viewing from form controls via tabs (Step 1: Document, Step 2: Submit) or wizard pattern, NOT stacked modal; bottom sheets are for supplementary content only; desktop/tablet preserve side-by-side modal with collapsible footer; primary CTA at viewport bottom; defensive messaging removed in sequential flows.

- [Queue systems keep request-level rate limiting separate from scheduler-level continuation and ramp-up behavior](queue-systems/queue-rate-ramp-up-ramp-down-keeps-request-retry-separate-from-scheduler-continuation.md) — 2026-08-04 — Cloud Tasks (500 tasks/sec cap, separate max-concurrent/max-dispatches parameters, ramp-up after pause/idle), SQS (per-consumer/producer fairness, DLQ redrive policy as circuit breaker), Temporal (retry policy distinct from Continue-As-New), client-go workqueue (per-item exponential backoff via Forget/AddRateLimited), Stripe (idempotency keys separate from webhook retry schedules). Core finding: all systems isolate request-level concerns (retry, backoff, idempotency) from scheduler-level concerns (ramp, fairness, circuit breaking) — no unified "smart limiter" exists. Numeric limits (Cloud Tasks 500/sec, SQS 12h visibility, Temporal history size) are infrastructure-specific and should not be imported literally.

- [Tailwind v4 @theme and shadcn/ui converge on CSS variables for custom colors and dark mode, with @theme inline approach replacing theme config for semantic tokens](tailwind-theming/tailwind-v4-at-theme-and-shadcn-ui-css-variables-for-custom-colors-and-dark-mode.md) — 2026-08-04 — `@theme inline` with CSS variables in `:root` (light) and `.dark` (dark mode) is the official recommended approach for Tailwind v4 and shadcn/ui; semantic naming (`--brand`, `--status-approved`) replaces color names for single-point-of-change color updates; oklch() color space preferred for perceptual uniformity across light/dark pairs; dark mode implemented by scoping CSS variable values to `.dark` class without Tailwind config changes.

- [Vana SDK ABI fetching defaults to the oldest proxy implementation, missing new role/function definitions deployed on mainnet](smart-contracts/vana-sdk-abi-fetching-defaults-to-old-proxy-implementations.md) — 2026-08-04 — Vana SDK `fetch-abis` uses `implementations[0]`, the oldest (Moksha testnet) not newest (mainnet). DLP_REWARD_DEPLOYER_ROLE, bonus/epoch functions exist only on mainnet. Fix: `implementations[-1]`. Testnet lags mainnet in features — atypical pattern.
- [Vana QueryEngine data-access permissions are standing, repeatable grants for arbitrary SQL against a refiner's schema, not one-time or query-scoped approvals](smart-contracts/vana-queryengine-permission-approval-grants-standing-repeatable-arbitrary-sql-access-until-revoked.md) — 2026-08-06 — `updatePermissionApproval` toggles an open-ended grant: approved permissions let the grantee submit arbitrary SQL repeatedly, indefinitely, with no per-query re-check beyond "is this still approved." TEE execution bounds raw-data exposure but not schema-exposed field exfiltration via repeated queries. No documented expiry/quota — revocation is the only close mechanism.

- [AI ignore files converge on gitignore-compatible syntax but diverge on filenames (.aiignore/.cursorignore/.aiexclude/.geminiignore); Aider docs most complete, JetBrains is UI-only, Copilot has no per-repo file](ai-ignore-files/aiignore-specification-and-cross-tool-formats-converge-on-gitignore-syntax.md) — 2026-08-04 — Aider (.aiderignore), Cursor (.cursorignore), Gemini (.aiexclude/.geminiignore), JetBrains (.aiignore UI toggle), Codeium (.codeiumignore), GitHub Copilot (workspace settings only). All gitignore-compatible patterns where supported. Aider has the most complete documentation. JetBrains' .aiignore is primarily IDE-driven UI, not a canonical gitignore glob engine. Cross-tool portability is fractured; .aiexclude has broadest reach but not universal.

- [Airtable, Stripe, and SaaS data-table toolbars converge on left-filters + right-actions + persistent column toggles in a single horizontal bar](table-ui-design/airtable-and-saas-table-toolbar-layout-patterns-2024.md) — 2026-08-04 — Systematic survey (Airtable, Stripe Apps, Shopify Polaris, Carbon Design System, Retool, Linear): toolbar ABOVE table, search+filters LEFT (visible by default, no hidden menus), column/export/actions RIGHT, all in one horizontal row with 12–16px group spacing. Bulk-action bar overlays only when rows selected. Mature platforms reject nested menus (cognitive load) in favor of grouped visible controls; density tradeoff solved by spacing reduction, not hiding. For new implementations: place toolbar 16px above table, left-align search+filters, right-align column+export+actions, use 12px between groups and 8px within.

- [MCP server best practices converge on tools over resources, content+structuredContent split, opaque pagination cursors, and compact result-card design](mcp-protocol/mcp-server-best-practices-tools-resources-pagination-content-strategy.md) — 2026-08-04 — Systematic authoritative survey (AWS Design Guidelines, AWS Prescriptive Guidance, Anthropic spec, OpenAI Deep Research guidance, GitHub MCP server, Microsoft pagination, arXiv tool-smell study of 856 tools, reference implementations). Canonical pattern: tools primary (reliable LLM invocation), resources secondary (host-optional). content[] is the model-readable channel (concise summary + truncation markers + pagination state); structuredContent is host-dependent envelope. Opaque server-owned pagination cursors with has_more/nextCursor in content[]. Search+fetch is de-facto OpenAI standard; PDPP exceeds reference servers with three-tier content ladder (preview→envelope→field-window→resource link) and no dead-ends. Result cards ~1792 chars, mark truncation explicitly. arXiv study: 56% unclear purpose, 89.3% no usage guidance, widespread opaque truncation and resource-only required paths. Scorecard: PDPP aligned on 7/9 (verify outputSchema coverage + error actionability), recommendations: description-smell lint, tool-count ceiling, three-tier compat tests.

- [Reverse filter via relationship is a constrained alternative to reverse-expansion for cross-stream/cross-table queries; PostgREST !inner demonstrates the pattern](api-contract-design/reverse-filter-via-relationship-is-a-constrained-alternative-to-reverse-expansion.md) — 2026-08-04 — PostgREST `!inner` (inner join) embeds via reverse relationships, filtering primary resources by the existence of related records. A constrained reverse-filter contract (`filter[via.<rel>.<field>]`) enables queries like "get messages where I reacted to them" without full reverse-expansion payloads. Pattern requirements: explicit relationship declaration (never heuristic), single-hop depth (nested `via` rejected), EXISTS-based SQL, primary data unchanged. Reverse filtering is cheaper than reverse expansion (one EXISTS subquery vs N embedded payloads) and orthogonal to forward relationships; single-hop boundary prevents scope creep and bounds testing surface.

- [Data annotation platforms and partner integration metadata converge on source-file tracking and external-reference mapping](data-annotation-and-partner-integration/data-annotation-platforms-and-partner-integration-metadata-converge-on-source-file-tracking-and-external-reference-mapping.md) — 2026-08-04 — Labelbox, Scale AI, Stripe, and Plaid converge on metadata patterns for external-reference tracking: data annotation requires source-file URLs and audit trails (Labelbox/Scale); financial systems use `external_id`/`reference_id` fields for partner mapping (Stripe metadata); ecosystem partners (Plaid) maintain bidirectional reference fidelity. Implication: annotation/collection systems should store metadata in a separate table (not filename), with source URL, upload timestamp, external confirmation code, and partner-canonical-ID fields enabling both user ("what did I submit?") and partner ("which users…") queries.

- [Admin dashboard auto-refresh converges on silent background polling with tab-visibility pause and pause-on-interaction as a lower-priority optimization](product-design/admin-dashboard-auto-refresh-converges-on-silent-background-polling-with-tab-visibility-pause.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe, Plaid, and Sisense all use silent 30s polling that pauses when tab hidden; SWR and React Query make `refreshWhenHidden: false` the universal default; pause-on-interaction is optional. Visual indicators (dropdowns, timestamps, status dots) add cognitive load for something that should be invisible. Implementation: 30s interval, pause-on-hidden built-in, refresh button only.
- [Admin dashboard navigation converges on sidebar-driven progressive disclosure with stable section anchors, not single long pages or tab-based fragmentation](product-design/admin-dashboard-navigation-architecture-sidebar-sections-vs-single-long-page.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe, Vercel, Grafana, GitHub, Databricks, Linear, and Plaid all use left sidebars with collapsible section groups; each section routes to a distinct page, never a single-page scroll. Sidebar + distinct-page routing reduces cognitive load and improves discoverability vs. inline expansion or tab-based fragmentation. Implementation: 200–280px sidebar, group settings into sections, route each to a distinct URL, persist sidebar across pages.
- [Modern layout container patterns converge on fluid clamp() for max-width and CSS variables for semantic sizing, replacing fixed breakpoint-bound utilities](product-design/layout-container-max-width-clamp-patterns-modern-responsive-design-2024.md) — 2026-08-04 — Uniswap, major web3 apps, and Tailwind v4 use `clamp(min, preferred, max)` for smooth scaling without media queries (e.g., `clamp(16px, 4vw, 32px)` for padding, `clamp(100%, <vw>, 800px)` for content width); context-specific sizing (auth 600px, content 800px, dashboard 1200px); CSS custom properties for semantic naming; mobile-first 100% width with max-width constraints ≥768px on desktop. Tailwind v4's `@utility` must be top-level (no nesting in `@media`); custom utilities via `@apply` + conditional classes are preferred over inline.
- [A coined term is legitimate only if it recurs and does real work — otherwise it's ornament](writing-craft/a-coined-term-is-legitimate-only-if-it-recurs-and-does-work-otherwise-its-ornament.md) — 2026-08-04 — tropes.fyi's "Invented Concept Labels" and Wikipedia's Signs-of-AI-writing name the pattern; boscoh.com (scientific writing) and Scott Kubie (product naming) independently converge on the same fix — a term used once or twice should stay unnamed and be described plainly, not minted as a Title Case handle. No industry-codified N-uses threshold exists; treat as strong convergent practitioner consensus, not settled rule.
- [Neutral-standard-plus-network sites (x402, DID Core, OpenID Connect, Matrix, ActivityPub, Solid) keep specs vendor-silent, but neutrality leaks at the ecosystem/directory layer](standards-body-sites/neutral-standard-plus-network-sites-keep-specs-vendor-silent-but-neutrality-leaks-at-the-ecosystem-directory-layer.md) — 2026-08-04 — x402 launched as a Coinbase product and became neutral via a dated LF governance transfer; Matrix's own ecosystem page double-features Element (its founding steward) over comparable clients; Solid never names Inrupt on its homepage/About page. Only DID Core (plurality quantified in spec metadata) and OpenID Connect (procedural certification suite) avoid an editorial human deciding who gets named.
- [Neutral-standard SITES open with a one-line definition of what the thing is, while the spec DOCUMENTS they publish open with status frontmatter](standards-body-sites/neutral-standard-sites-open-with-a-one-line-definition-not-frontmatter-unlike-the-spec-documents-they-publish.md) — 2026-08-04 — Four of four neutral-standard homepages (openid.net, spec.matrix.org, solidproject.org, x402.org) lead with a one-line statement of what the thing is; zero lead with a version/status rail or metadata table. W3C DID Core, a spec DOCUMENT, puts 9 metadata rows between title and abstract. The prior "spec sites skip the hero" finding was drawn from RFC 9110/WHATWG/W3C TR (documents) and wrongly generalized onto sites. Frontmatter density should scale with the reader's certainty about why they arrived; a homepage is the point of minimum certainty. Definition-first and pitch-first are separable: two of the four use no CTA button at all.

- [SendGrid Inbound Parse uses separate subdomain from root domain because MX records are domain-wide and existing email breaks if root MX points to SendGrid](email-services/sendgrid-inbound-parse-domain-vs-mx-record-separation.md) — 2026-08-04 — MX routing is domain-scoped: one MX target per domain. If root domain has existing email (Gmail, Google Workspace), subdomain separation is essential. Webhook URL versioning unnecessary (SendGrid configures URL, no client negotiation). Default to subdomain for any production system with existing email.

- [Referral programs converge on first-touch attribution with Safari ITP/Firefox ETP workarounds; existing-user landing pages avoid rewards or gate eligibility transparently](referral-programs/first-touch-attribution-existing-user-ux-safari-itp.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe, Plaid, Uber, Dropbox, and referral-software platforms (Growsurf, Referral Factory, SaaSQuatch) all use first-touch attribution + URL+cookie fallback. Existing-user flows route separately (Plaid RUX) or gate transparently (Uber "new only"). Payment-state validation (Stripe PaymentIntent) gates eligibility. Landing pages should show ineligibility upfront, not silently consume referrals.

- [B2B SaaS user invitation lifecycle and database schema converges on separate invitations table, pre-assigned roles, 10-day expiration, and token-based acceptance with automatic session creation](b2b-saas-invitation/invitation-lifecycle-and-database-schema-patterns.md) — 2026-08-04 — Systematic prior-art survey (Auth0 Organizations, Stripe Connect Teams, Plaid, WorkOS, NextAuth v5, Clerk magic links). Canonical pattern: separate invitations table with status tracking (pending/accepted/revoked/expired), role immutable at invite time, 10-day expiration, single-click passwordless acceptance that creates account + session together. Role ceiling (inviter must be >= invitee privilege). Resend and revocation are independent operations. Email verification is at send time, not acceptance. Open: whether to hash invitation tokens at rest.

- [Michael Pollan's "Food Rules" (2009) tested against 2025-2026 nutrition science: major reversals on saturated fat and dietary cholesterol, heterogeneous UPF effects, internal contradictions in 2025-2030 guidelines](nutrition/pollan-food-rules-vs-2026-evidence.md) — 2026-08-04 — Primary-source verification of RCTs, meta-analyses, 2025 DGAC Report, 2025-2030 Dietary Guidelines (15+ searches). Yamada et al. (JMA Journal 2025) RCT meta-analysis (9 trials, 13,532 people) finds saturated fat reduction has NO mortality benefit, contradicting AHA/ACC guidelines. 2025 DGAC removed dietary cholesterol limits entirely; elevated eggs. Springer Nature 2025 meta-analysis: 15% all-cause mortality ↑ on highest-UPF diets but effects heterogeneous by subcategory (yogurt/cereals protective; sugary drinks/processed meat harmful). **2025-2030 Guidelines internal contradiction**: saturated fat target <10% while recommended foods exceed it. Vegan diets require supplementation (iodine 90% below RDA without fortification). Pollan's core (plant-heavy, real food) survives; specifics reversed (saturated fat: avoid→RCTs contradict; cholesterol: limit→remove; eggs: risky→recommended; vegan: complete→supplementation needed). **Verdict: 3 major reversals, 4 held up, 3 contested.** Related: [[yamada-saturated-fat-rct-2025]], [[2025-dgac-cholesterol-reversal]], [[2025-guidelines-contradiction]].

- [Orchestrator-centralized synthesis prevents fragmentation; per-worker writes requires strict isolation to avoid duplication and contradiction](agentic-context-design/multi-agent-knowledge-consolidation-orchestrator-vs-per-worker-writes.md) — 2026-08-04 — Systematic web prior-art survey (13 sources: Anthropic Research system, OpenAI Deep Research, Google Co-Scientist, CrewAI, LangGraph, Claude Code, production failure analysis). Canonical pattern: orchestrator holds consolidation logic, workers return structured findings. Anthropic beats baseline 90.2%, cost is 15× tokens (synthesis pass ONCE at end, not distributed). Per-worker writes work only with strict domain partition + Git worktrees. 79% of production failures are state-sync races + inter-agent misalignment + contradiction undetected. Scratchpad deduplication via SHA-256 + metadata aliasing + version control. Open: optimal consolidation frequency, dependency chain ordering, verification of orchestrator itself. Implementation: structured JSON from workers, explicit dedup strategy (hash/similarity/entity-resolution), conflict escalation rule (which source authoritative, or escalate human). Related: [[prompted-reminders...]], [[auditing-the-orchestrator...]], [[per-worker-mcp-isolation...]], [[agent-memory-write-guarantees...]].

- [Automatic knowledge extraction from conversation logs via post-hoc background consolidation, not in-session agent decisions](agentic-context-design/background-post-hoc-memory-extraction-without-agent-decisions.md) — 2026-08-04 — Systematic web prior-art survey (15+ papers + production systems): canonical pattern is online/offline decoupling (user-facing inference fast, consolidation deferred to background job). Letta sleep-time agents, Auto-Dreamer, SCM (90.9% noise reduction), MOOM (600-turn ultra-long dialogues), TRUSTMEM (hallucination detection), SAGE (3.4× cost reduction via novelty gating), Generative Agents reflection (validated by ablation). Mem0 async achieves p50 0.148s latency. Cost/precision tradeoffs: high-throughput heuristics (SAGE) vs high-precision verification (TRUSTMEM) vs domain-specific schemas (MOOM). Episodic→semantic consolidation (100→3 summaries) is the template. Open: optimal consolidation frequency, unbounded dialogue handling, retrieval modality interaction. Implementation: defer extraction to 6-hourly batch jobs, set memory capacity limits, validate quality experimentally (replay with/without consolidation).

- [Agent-memory write guarantees differ by mechanism: forced function calls (Letta), automatic tool invocation (Windsurf), LLM extraction (Mem0), or manual writes (Claude Code, Cline)](agentic-memory/agent-memory-write-guarantees-across-systems.md) — 2026-08-04 — comprehensive prior-art survey of Letta/MemGPT, Mem0, Claude Code, Windsurf, Cursor, Cline, ChatGPT, Zep, Aider, Continue.dev. Key findings: Letta guarantees via forced function calls; Windsurf via automatic tool invocation (security risk documented); Mem0 has no guarantee (silent DELETE on text-similarity conflicts); Claude Code dual-layer (manual + auto); ChatGPT explicit-action + background update; Zep auto event logging. Mem0's conflict resolver cannot distinguish context-dependent preferences → unwarned data loss. Best practice: forced function calls for auditability; automatic event logging for durability; avoid LLM-driven extraction without human override.

- [Instruction-following compliance in LLM agents: measured rates, timing/placement effects, hook vs prompt enforcement, multi-turn degradation](instruction-compliance-and-enforcement/README.md) — 2026-08-04 — systematic web research on instruction-following compliance: AGENTIF 27.2% ISR, HANDBOOK.md 36.2% best, process compliance 0–97% reward-dependent, prompt-only 70–90% baseline, hooks 100%, multi-turn degrades 39–112% worse, position effects real but secondary, no published data on hook compliance drift. Consolidated findings: compliance is architecturally determined (reward signal alignment), timing matters within prompt regime, hooks are the architectural tier above prompts. Open: no empirical measurement of hook-compliance degradation over long sessions.

- [Agent completion gates: forcing required actions before finishing — three patterns (model-level forcing, state-machine gating, session-level blocking) with Claude Code Stop hook contract, Claude/OpenAI tool_choice forcing, and LangGraph/Guardrails architectures](agent-completion-gates/README.md) — 2026-08-04 — established patterns for completion gates across Anthropic Claude, OpenAI, LangGraph, Guardrails AI, NeMo Guardrails, and constrained decoding. Claude Code Stop hook blocks session termination via `{"decision": "block"}` or exit code 2 with 8-block-per-turn loop cap; Claude API `tool_choice="any"` forces one tool, OpenAI `tool_choice="required"` guarantees tool invocation; LangGraph `interrupt_before/after` for workflow gates; Guardrails AI three-stage validation (input→output→action). No cross-provider standard; extended thinking incompatible with forced tools on Claude.

- [No major secret-scanning/hygiene tool embeds its private denylist inside the scanned repo — gitleaks/trufflehog externalize config via env var or --config, detect-secrets hashes rather than storing plaintext, GitHub keeps custom patterns in settings UI outside the tree, and none of them document or endorse a scanner exempting its own definition file from its own scan](repo-hygiene-and-secret-scanning/no-tool-embeds-its-private-denylist-in-the-scanned-repo-they-externalize-hash-or-config-it-via-settings.md) — 2026-08-03 — gitleaks' config precedence (--config → GITLEAKS_CONFIG env var → GITLEAKS_CONFIG_TOML env var → in-tree fallback → built-in default) and git-secrets' `.git/config`-backed pattern store are the cleanest proof "public engine, ruleset supplied out-of-band" is mature load-bearing practice. "The denylist is itself sensitive" and "self-exempting scanner is an anti-pattern" are NOT documented anywhere in the primary tool literature — both are this agent's synthesis, not a stated tool-maintainer position. Government open-source-release policy (SEC, GSA) is a better procedural match than any secret-scanner for PDPP's actual threat model (pre-publication squash-to-public scrubbing of non-credential residue like paths/hostnames/codenames), assigning scrubbing as a human-owned release-gate responsibility rather than a per-commit hook.

- [GitHub push protection is free and default-on for public repos but $19/active-committer/month on private ones, and is self-service bypassable unless delegated bypass is configured](repo-hygiene-and-secret-scanning/github-push-protection-is-free-on-public-repos-but-19-per-committer-on-private-and-bypassable-by-default.md) — 2026-08-07 — GHAS unbundled 2025-04-01 into Secret Protection ($19) / Code Security ($30), purchasable by Team-plan orgs for the first time; "Purchased: Unlimited licenses" in the org panel means unlimited *available*, not free — the per-committer meter (90-day rolling window, bots excluded, org-deduplicated) starts on enablement for private repos only. Security configurations are the current org-wide mechanism (subset targeting by visibility, enforcement blocks repo owners from disabling, applies to archived repos); per-repo `security_and_analysis` PATCH is the legacy path. Docs' phased order is assessment → alerts → blocking → remediate, because first enablement scans full git history on all branches and produces a backlog *before* anyone is blocked. Critical: default push protection lets anyone with write access click through with a reason (logged + emails admins), so a policy claiming "blocked at push" is only true with delegated bypass configured. Free Secret Risk Assessment answers backlog volume with real numbers — no authoritative source estimates it.

- [The current MCP specification (2026-07-28, unchanged from 2025-11-25) mandates both client-side RFC 8707 `resource` parameter usage AND server-side audience validation on token receipt — RFC 8707 itself only mandates the former](oauth-mcp-auth/mcp-spec-mandates-rfc-8707-resource-parameter-and-server-side-audience-validation.md) — 2026-08-02 — RFC 8707's only AS-side requirement is a SHOULD to audience-restrict tokens, and it imposes zero normative requirement on the resource server to verify audience on receipt. MCP closes that gap independently: MCP servers "MUST validate that access tokens were issued specifically for them as the intended audience" and MUST reject/401 non-matching tokens — identical wording across both revisions. Parsing/requiring `resource` at the AS's authorization endpoint (issuance-side plumbing) is NOT equivalent to implementing RFC 8707 for MCP; the resource-server-side enforcement is where the actual confused-deputy protection lives and neither spec prescribes the storage mechanism.

- [Leading self-host and MCP-client tools converge on auto-generated first-boot secrets, doctor-style preflight commands, API-enforced capability gating, and a hard public-HTTPS requirement for cloud-hosted MCP clients that Claude Code's local transport does not share](self-hosting/self-host-onboarding-prior-art-2026-08-02.md) — 2026-08-02 — Anthropic's claude.ai connectors do a pre-flight DNS/IP-range rejection of private/CGNAT/self-signed servers (Free/Pro/Max can self-add; Team/Enterprise gated to Owners), so no config can make a LAN-only PDPP work there — Claude Code's local `stdio`/`--callback-port` transport has no such requirement. ChatGPT Developer Mode excludes the Free plan entirely. Cloudflare Quick Tunnel needs no account but caps at 200 in-flight requests, no SSE, no SLA, new URL every restart. Stripe Connect's `requirements`/`disabled_reason` capability object is the strongest prior art for gating a flow before it starts, not after it fails.

- [GPT-5.6 Luna at max reasoning effort is a defensible cost/quality pick for bounded Codex coding tasks, but is not a broadly-consensus "best Codex setting" and is not the default Codex tier](model-routing/gpt-5-6-luna-at-max-effort-is-a-defensible-not-dominant-codex-value-pick.md) — 2026-08-02 — Official Codex config reference lists no `max` value for `model_reasoning_effort` (only minimal..xhigh); `max` exists as a separate opt-in UI toggle and Codex's default tier is Sol@medium, not Luna. On the one grade-B (ARC Prize) effort-stratified series, Luna@max is beaten on BOTH accuracy and cost by Sol@medium — Luna's own best score/$ points are medium/low, not max. The one supportive coding-benchmark reading (DeepSWE leaderboard, mini-swe-agent harness, 113 tasks) shows Luna@max at 67%±4% pass@1 / $0.61/task vs Sol@max 73%±3% / $8.39/task — a real value point but single-source with overlapping CIs, not a broad HN/Reddit consensus.

- [Durable-workflow, job-queue, actor, and terminal-automation systems all put engineering into the boundary Waspflow is missing](agent-harnesses/durable-wakeup-patterns-from-workflow-queue-actor-and-terminal-systems.md) — 2026-07-30 — Cross-system survey (Temporal, Step Functions, Erlang/OTP, GitHub Actions, BullMQ, VS Code shell integration, tmux, systemd, plus Gas Town/FirstMate) answering whether any system achieves native-like injection without owning the parent event loop (no — Step Functions' token pattern is the closest non-owning exception) and whether terminal automation is a legitimate adapter or only a fallback (only a fallback — no ordering/atomicity/ack guarantee at the tmux layer, no cross-tool readiness signal except Codex's narrow OSC 9 turn-complete). Recommends a durable idempotent receipt plus per-harness cooperative signal where documented, heuristic pane classification as an explicit fallback elsewhere.

- [Waspflow completion adapters require evidence for delivery order, turn ownership, and recovery](agent-harnesses/waspflow-native-like-completion-adapters-should-use-a-tiered-contract.md) — 2026-07-30 — Draft implementation gate for eight products: classify each path as documented pre-next-decision, documented next/new-turn only, or not documented; controller acceptance never substitutes for model-context delivery; every row carries setup ownership and unrun active/busy/permission, restart, and duplicate-ack probes.

- [Eight coding-agent products have different documented async-delivery boundaries](agent-harnesses/official-async-completion-contracts-for-seven-coding-harnesses.md) — 2026-07-30 — Draft official-source map separates actual release-tag facts from post-release `main` and live-documentation observations for Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, OpenCode, Pi, Qwen, legacy Kimi, and Kimi Code; it explicitly distinguishes notification, transport acceptance, model delivery, new-turn invocation, and recovery.

- [Kernel stack unwinding reliability: DWARF vs ORC vs frame pointers, corrupted frame detection in x86-64, ARM64, and BPF programs](kernel-stack-unwinding-reliability/stack-unwinding-reliability-x86-arm64-bpf.md) - 2026-07-26 - Comprehensive analysis of stack unwinding methods (ORC fastest and most reliable for kernel; DWARF slow but complete; frame pointers fast but interrupt-unsafe), corruption detection (CFI, KASAN, ECC, stack canaries), BPF stack trace hash collision flaw causing data loss vs corruption trade-off, ARM64's arch_stack_walk_reliable() API, and diagnostic patterns for distinguishing hardware corruption from software bugs in production stack traces.

- [DrKonqi pickup's intentional runtime bound produces a benign failed-unit timeout](systemd/drkonqi-pickups-intentional-runtime-bound-produces-a-benign-failed-unit-timeout.md) - 2026-07-24 - KDE intentionally gives login-time crash pickup a 30-minute window, whose normal expiration systemd records as failure. Existing practice is to ignore this exact timeout or mask DrKonqi when crash handling is unwanted; the custom timeout wrapper was removed.

- [AdGuard Home must conditionally forward Active Directory DNS zones](self-hosting/adguard-home-must-conditionally-forward-active-directory-dns-zones.md) - 2026-07-24 - Static A/PTR rewrites cannot replace AD's dynamic LDAP/Kerberos SRV records; use AdGuard Home's per-domain upstream syntax to forward `reef.vivid.fish` to the domain controller.

- [Timeshift does not support network snapshot targets; use a separate system-backup repository](self-hosting/timeshift-does-not-support-network-snapshot-targets-use-a-separate-system-backup-repository.md) - 2026-07-24 - Current upstream and user reports agree that Timeshift rejects NFS/SMB targets; retain a small local Timeshift window and use a dedicated, per-host NAS repository with a backup tool designed for network storage.

- [Balance authorizations serialize on a durable row, and accepted obligations preserve their authorization identity](distributed-systems/balance-authorizations-serialize-on-a-durable-row-and-accepted-obligations-preserve-their-authorization-identity.md) — 2026-07-23 — PostgreSQL `FOR UPDATE` serializes competing lockers until transaction end, while Stripe keeps authorization state and capturable amount on one durable PaymentIntent. For Data Gateway, lock the payer+asset balance row before reading balance and accepted obligations, then persist the exact authorizing grant id/version on the obligation.
- [Ultracite 7.9 / Biome 2.5 official-doc facts for a uniform monorepo upgrade](lfdt-labs-prior-art/ultracite-79-biome-25-scope-and-migration-facts-2026-07-23.md) — 2026-07-23 — Ultracite treats JavaScript and TypeScript as first-class inputs, merges project scope through `files.includes`, and distinguishes indexed ignores (`!`) from full scanner exclusions (`!!`). For a uniform monorepo upgrade, migrate legacy JavaScript, exclude only real artifacts, and stage the rollout per workspace.
- [Antigravity is a separate CLI and quota provider, not a Gemini CLI authentication mode](ai-quota-monitoring/antigravity-is-a-separate-cli-and-quota-provider-not-a-gemini-auth-mode.md) — 2026-07-23 — Google retired Gemini CLI consumer OAuth on June 18 while retaining enterprise/Cloud/API access; consumer users move to the separate Go-based `agy` CLI, whose `/usage` surface reports per-model quotas and resets. Clawmeter should add Antigravity as a separate provider and initially read its local status service rather than relabeling Gemini or owning another OAuth flow.
- [FIDO and TOTP authentication do not supply the password KWallet PAM needs](self-hosting/fido-and-totp-authentication-do-not-supply-the-password-kwallet-pam-needs.md) — 2026-07-23 — FIDO/U2F, smart-card, and TOTP prove possession or a one-time code; they do not hand PAM the stable login/KWallet password. `pam_kwallet5` therefore cannot auto-unlock an existing password-encrypted wallet from those factors alone; password login can, while public-key/FIDO-key SSH should intentionally leave the wallet locked.
- [SSH hardening norm is key-only, while key plus password is a separate two-factor choice](self-hosting/ssh-hardening-norm-is-key-only-while-key-plus-password-is-a-separate-two-factor-choice.md) — 2026-07-23 — Ubuntu recommends key-based SSH and disabling passwords when key recovery is reliable; OpenSSH’s `AuthenticationMethods publickey,password` explicitly requires both factors, so it is a deliberate MFA policy, not the usual key-auth baseline. Personal workstations may retain password SSH for an intentional use case, but should decide based on reachability and recovery rather than an unnoticed default.
- [KWallet auto-unlocks at PAM desktop login, not an SSH key login](self-hosting/kwallet-auto-unlocks-at-pam-desktop-login-not-an-ssh-key-login.md) — 2026-07-23 — KWallet is unlocked with the login password only through configured `kwallet_pam`; non-graphical SSH/TTY sessions need `force_run`, and modern KF6 state must be checked through the `ksecretd` backend rather than the `kwalletd6` compatibility frontend.
- [Infisical CLI offline secret access is a CLI-managed encrypted cache, not a plaintext shell export](self-hosting/infisical-cli-offline-secret-cache-is-a-cli-managed-encrypted-cache-not-a-plaintext-shell-export.md) — 2026-07-23 — `export` was locally proven offline with an exact 29-entry digest match and no legacy file vault, but `auto` is not fail-closed: its source falls back to an encrypted file vault after a system-keyring write failure. The desired state is ciphertext cache on disk plus its key/login in KWallet; strict KWallet-only behavior needs upstream support.
- [Infisical CLI user sessions never refresh because the refresh-token call site was implemented then reverted upstream](self-hosting/infisical-cli-user-session-does-not-refresh-because-the-refresh-token-call-site-was-implemented-then-reverted.md) — 2026-08-02 — `// TODO: add refresh token` in credentials.go is live in the latest release (v0.43.116, binary confirmed): PR #3797/ENG-2690 implemented it 2025-06-14 and `f167ba0` reverted it 2025-06-19 with an empty commit body and no public issue, so workstation sessions hard-expire ~14d regardless of use. Compounding it, only the user-login branch writes the encrypted offline cache — a machine-identity-only host is fast and never expires but accumulates no outage cache. Hence the split design: MI primary, periodic manual `infisical login` purely to keep the cache warm, automation rejected because the only non-interactive user login takes an account password.

- [Docker Sandboxes install preflight must validate the real microVM runtime, not just the CLI](docker-sandboxes/install-preflight-must-validate-the-real-microvm-runtime-not-just-the-cli.md) — 2026-07-21 — Docker's documented Ubuntu prerequisites and diagnostics show an sbx version check is insufficient: readiness needs the apt-backed install, KVM, daemon, initialized policy, and login in the profile that will run the sandbox.

- [No major OSS project frames its AI policy as quality rather than provenance; Artsy's `Assisted-by` RFC is the strongest vendor-neutral trailer precedent](lfdt-labs-prior-art/no-major-oss-project-frames-ai-policy-as-quality-not-provenance-artsy-assisted-by-is-the-vendor-neutral-trailer-precedent.md) — 2026-07-21 — Major projects disclose or restrict AI provenance even when humans remain accountable. PDP-Connect can make quality the acceptance gate, treat provenance as context, and use an automated `Assisted-by: AI` trailer separate from the human DCO sign-off.
- [Standards that won on craft (semver, OAuth, Sigstore, OpenTelemetry) as references for PDP-Connect](lfdt-labs-prior-art/standards-that-won-on-craft-semver-oauth-sigstore-otel-as-references-for-pdp-connect.md) — 2026-07-21 — ALL four keep the SPEC in its own repo SEPARATE from implementations (semver/semver = spec-only; sigstore/architecture-docs [IETF i-d format] + sigstore/protobuf-specs separate from cosign/fulcio/rekor; opentelemetry-specification separate from SDKs). VERDICT for PDP-Connect: split the reference implementation OUT of pdpp, leave pdpp spec-only. Sigstore = closest structural analog (formal spec + LF/TSC charter + multi-lang RI ecosystem + modern DX) BUT invert its tool-first docs IA to spec-first. Adopt: semver one-page RFC-2119 prose + zero-permission adoption; OAuth core+registry extension model (build the oauth.net-style curated "spec map" from day one, its RFC-sprawl is the anti-pattern); OTel "cross-impl source of truth" README framing + per-impl version independence + 2-approvals-from-2-companies anti-capture merge bar.
- [High-craft contribution flow (Rust, Astro, Biome, Deno, K8s) for PDP-Connect](lfdt-labs-prior-art/high-craft-contribution-flow-rust-astro-biome-deno-k8s-for-pdp-connect.md) — 2026-07-21 — KEY: Biome + Deno have explicit AI-DISCLOSURE norms (Biome: disclose "written primarily by Claude Code" + ban AI-written PR *descriptions* even when AI wrote code + AgentScan CI bot; Deno: "PRs rejected if suspicion of undisclosed AI usage", 2x in PR template) — the direct answer to the AI-slop-optics worry: disclose openly + hold PR *communication* to a human bar, NOT hide in a private repo. Right-sized recc: CONTRIBUTING <1pg Rust-style router (+1 AI-disclosure line); PR template 3-5 "explain if skipped" sections (Astro) + AI-disclosure banner (Deno); one YAML bug form; small label set (good-first-issue/help-wanted/3-4 priority tiers) defer K8s OWNERS + Rust E-* ladder; WIP = plain draft PRs; keep DCO not CLA.
- [The org .github repo is fallback-only; repo-specific files always win; CLOWarden never touches content files](lfdt-labs-prior-art/the-github-org-dot-github-repo-is-a-fallback-only-repo-specific-files-always-win-and-clowarden-never-touches-content-files.md) — 2026-07-21 — LFDT/CLOWarden owns ONLY team membership + repo ACLs/visibility (governance/config.yaml); NO field for CONTRIBUTING/CoC/templates/CODEOWNERS/branch-protection/CI/labels/Discussions — so the lab owns the entire contribution surface per-repo, zero collision risk. Org .github = pure fallback (repo's own file always wins; a custom issue-template opts the repo out of the whole org-default set). CODEOWNERS references CLOWarden-managed @org/team-slugs (no conflict). GAP: PDP-Connect's 3 product repos NOT in config.yaml yet — ask Ry (also: DCO app scope; whether to populate our own .github).
- [How LF maintainers develop: direct branches not forks; WIP-optics solved by squash-merge not privacy](lfdt-labs-prior-art/how-lf-maintainers-develop-fork-vs-direct-branch-and-wip-optics.md) — 2026-07-21 — real merged-PR data: maintainers with the commit bit push branches DIRECTLY to upstream (fabric, backstage, LFDT labs); forks are the OUTSIDE-contributor pattern, no company keeps a standing branded fork as its dev env (not even IBM on fabric). Public-WIP-optics solved by squash-merge + draft PRs, NOT repo privacy. Recc for PDP-Connect (a maintainer): direct branches + draft PRs + squash-merge on PDP-Connect/*; public fork buys nothing on optics; private dev repo reads worse (vendor-capture) for a neutral steward.
- [Company-originated LF projects neutralize at graduation, not at Lab entry](lfdt-labs-prior-art/company-originated-lf-projects-neutralize-at-graduation-not-at-lab-entry.md) — 2026-07-17 — LFDT TOC lifecycle attaches "be vendor neutral" to the Proposal→Incubation gate, NOT Labs (Labs sits outside the formal pipeline); bundled company infra at Labs entry is normal, proven by AIFAQ (argues neutrality AT Labs→Incubation), CNCF Backstage (entered Sandbox as Spotify's tool, still sells Backstage SaaS), and Hiero/Hedera (donated whole production codebase, still runs mainnet on it, cleanup ongoing post-donation). Only Labs-entry guardrail = branding: call it "an LFDT Lab," not "an LFDT/Hyperledger project." For PDP-Connect: transfer data-connect WITH Vana infra intact; neutralize post-entry.
- [AnonCreds (spec + reference impl) is the closest structural precedent for a company-originated LFDT standard](lfdt-labs-prior-art/anoncreds-spec-plus-impl-is-the-closest-structural-precedent-for-a-company-originated-lfdt-standard.md) — 2026-07-17 — neutralize-AFTER-entry is the proven pattern (AnonCreds decoupled from Indy's ledger as first post-entry deliverable, done publicly, smaller than expected); project-prefixed repo naming (anoncreds-spec/anoncreds-rs, not company/foundation); repo-scoped license split (Community Spec License for spec + Apache-2.0 for code = exactly pdpp's setup); DCO-style foundation-generic contributor byline, no copyright assignment; one shared governance repo all MAINTAINERS.md point to. Caveat: AnonCreds skipped Labs → entered at Incubation because it had 5+yr adoption (earned shortcut, not general).
- [LFDT labs bless squash-to-one-DCO-signed-commit only when reusing a non-compliant existing repo; most labs start fresh](lfdt-labs-prior-art/lfdt-labs-blesses-squash-to-one-dco-signed-commit-only-when-reusing-a-non-compliant-existing-repo-most-labs-start-fresh.md) — 2026-07-17 — observed norm across real labs (chaindeploy/gitmesh/Naryo) = steward seeds blank repo w/ LICENSE-only "Initial commit," code lands as a SEPARATE "Initial contribution of X" commit (cleaner audit than one squashed blob). Company-keeps-copyright/project-gets-neutral-name = standard CNCF/Kubernetes split. De-branding is a long tail (Hedera→Hiero unfinished 12+mo later, dual-publish window needed). HARD RISK: npm Trusted Publishing OIDC binding breaks SILENTLY on org move (pinned to old repo+workflow path; 404s at publish time not build time) — re-point + re-verify is a required post-transfer step. Incubation entry FLIPS the rule: history preserved, not squashed.
- [Standards with reference impls keep the spec neutral and push vendor behavior into extensions/plugins](lfdt-labs-prior-art/standards-with-reference-impls-keep-the-spec-neutral-and-push-vendor-behavior-into-extensions-and-plugins.md) — 2026-07-17 — authorship organizationally separated from shipping (HL7 writes FHIR / HAPI ships it as separate org; ToIP WGs don't ship products); extension mechanisms are namespace-ENFORCED not review-judgment (OAuth→IANA-registered extension RFCs; SMART-on-FHIR bare-strings-for-core vs full-URIs-for-vendor, mechanically checkable); trademark stewardship divorced from vendors (OWF requires trademark/domain/repo transfer to foundation). OPEN CHOICE for PDP-Connect: should the neutral entity OWN the reference impl? OWF yes, HL7 no (HAPI separate) — since data-connect is positioned as THE reference client, OWF model (neutral-owned RI, no favoritism to Vana's network) is closer fit. Decide deliberately.
- [LFDT lab governance runs on CLOWarden config.yaml + DCO Bot with lab-scoped trust](lfdt-labs-prior-art/lfdt-lab-governance-runs-on-clowarden-configyaml-plus-dco-bot-with-lab-scoped-trust.md) — 2026-07-17 — ACLs via CLOWarden (CNCF tool) peer-reviewed config.yaml in governance repo: teams(maintainers/members)+repos(role+visibility); lab maintainer teams get `maintain` NOT `admin` (matches PDP-Connect/governance). DCO enforced by cncf/dco2 GitHub App (required branch-protection check; non-destructive remediation-commit option). New committers can't be added unilaterally (steward action required). Baseline files: LICENSE, MAINTAINERS.md, CODEOWNERS, CONTRIBUTING. npm trusted-publishing on transfer = UNSOLVED gap (no LF playbook; OIDC doesn't auto-update) — budget explicit engineering.
- [The remaining credible Qwen3.6 engine gains are MTP depth tuning or a DFlash challenger, not non-P2P dual-GPU serving](llm-serving/remaining-qwen36-engine-upside-is-mtp-depth-or-dflash-not-dual-gpu.md) — 2026-07-16 — the local sweep keeps MTP depth 3 and ubatch 512, promotes target-verified q5/q4 draft KV at 205,824 context, excludes DFlash by operator choice, and rejects non-P2P dual-GPU serving.

- [Claude Code subagent hooks](claude-code-subagent-hooks/README.md) — SubagentStart/Stop events, hook propagation into subagents, session_id semantics, whether parent sees subagent hooks

One line per entry, **newest first**. Agents read this first to decide what to open

**[Recovery backlog](RECOVERY-BACKLOG.md)** — 91 KEEP leads from the 2026-08-01 census of uncaptured research; recover on demand.
(see `README.md` for the convention; the standing "check here before web-researching"
rule is in `ai/AGENTS.md`). Add a line here whenever you create an entry.

- [Mature remote-display systems (RFB, RDP, SPICE, Guacamole, n.eko, CDP, cloud gaming) converge on media, input, clipboard, cursor, and session/floor-control as separable planes — but disagree on how many wire channels to spend on that separation](remote-browser/mature-remote-display-systems-converge-on-media-input-clipboard-cursor-and-session-control-as-separate-planes.md) — 2026-07-17 — for @opendatalabs/remote-surface's plane decomposition. RFB spends ONE wire channel on a conceptual taxonomy (typed messages incl. dedicated cursor pseudo-encoding on one TCP stream); SPICE spends the most (11 named channels incl. separate CURSOR, PLAYBACK, RECORD, auth scoped only to MAIN); RDP's core (video+input) plus open-ended virtual-channel extensibility gives clipboard (CLIPRDR) and audio (RDPSND/DVC) each a dedicated named+stateful channel; Guacamole gets SPICE-grade taxonomy richness (first-class audio/clipboard/file/pipe/filesystem instructions) on an RFB-grade single instruction-multiplexed stream, chosen explicitly for incremental-parse-ability over JSON/XML. X11-vs-Wayland reframes input/render separation as a SECURITY boundary (Wayland: no cross-client input/render access without an explicit broker; XWayland doesn't inherit this). n.eko's own v3 design treats control as transport-PLUGGABLE by design (input can ride either the WebSocket or a WebRTC data channel, not hard-wired) — unusual vs. every fixed-channel system surveyed; unverified against n.eko's actually-shipped build, issue #371 is a design proposal not confirmed behavior. Session/floor-control (who has input authority in a shared session) is real, shipped prior art (RealVNC `FloorControlEnable`+view-only toggles), not a speculative feature — relevant to remote-surface's multi-viewer case. CDP separates concerns into domains (Page/Emulation/Input/DOM) but has no first-class cursor, clipboard, or audio concept at all — an SDK on CDP must manufacture those planes that n.eko's WebRTC+native-X11-input backend gets closer to for free.

- [Visual session replay masks at capture time with no server-side net, and a timestamped-screenshot+action log is one-way evidence not deterministic replay](product-design/visual-session-replay-masks-at-capture-time-with-no-server-net-and-screenshots-are-one-way-evidence-not-replay.md) — 2026-07-16 — for Pramana/BUI-739 visual replay: rrweb/Sentry/Playwright all mask CLIENT-SIDE before upload (Sentry default-on maskAllText/blockAllMedia; canvas has ZERO PII scrubbing; srcdoc/cross-origin-iframe/global-unmask are documented bypasses) so a missed selector is a permanent unrecoverable leak with no server net; a screenshot+action log records outputs at discrete instants and fails Fowler's event-sourcing rebuild test (vs rr/rrweb re-derivation) — it is evidence, not replay; GH Actions rejects `:` in artifact paths and hosted macOS can't screen-record. Verdict: visual capture is a separate higher-risk sensitivity class, NOT an extension of the existing safe-metadata textual ledger.

- [Komodo copies tracked env-file contents plaintext into every stack's Mongo doc; track:false (its documented 'externally managed file' path) stops it, and the stored blobs clear on the next filtered rebuild](self-hosting/komodo-persists-tracked-env-files-plaintext-in-mongo-track-false-is-the-fix.md) — 2026-07-16 — Komodo `additional_env_files` with track:true (default) copies the WHOLE env file plaintext into StackInfo.deployed_contents/remote_contents in Mongo (verified in Rust source: tracked_env_files() filters on `f.track`, contents rebuilt+`$set` overwritten each deploy/refresh). Fix = track:false (still `--env-file`-injected, never stored — literally documented for "sops decrypted files"); flip THEN refresh/redeploy or scrub (scrub-while-tracked re-populates). Komodo native Variables also plaintext-in-Mongo (is_secret = UI mask + ACL, not at-rest enc); docs say "not enterprise secret management." Prior-art: Coolify Laravel-encrypted cast, Swarm secrets Raft+tmpfs, Flux+SOPS/ESO reference-at-runtime win. For an Infisical-backed box: track:false + per-stack least-priv env + LUKS mongo-data.

- [Long-press in a CDP browser stream is a held native touch sequence, not a right-click](remote-browser/long-press-in-a-cdp-browser-stream-is-a-held-native-touch-sequence-not-a-right-click.md) — 2026-07-16 — CDP's ordered raw touch lifecycle preserves the remote page's selection/context behavior; remote-desktop long-press-to-right-click/pan policies are not browser-semantic substitutes.

- [Qwen dynamic-resolution vision needs at least 1024 image tokens for reliable OCR and grounding](llm-serving/qwen-vision-grounding-needs-at-least-1024-image-tokens.md) — 2026-07-16 — Qwen recommends 1024–2048 image tokens for OCR/grounding; locally, a 1024-token floor cost about 767 additional context tokens on the same icon and passed exact screenshot OCR plus relative-position tests.

- [For Qwen3.6 on a 24 GiB GPU, q5_0 K plus q4_1 V is the practical default while q8_0 is a validation profile with a large context cost](llm-serving/qwen36-q5-k-q4-v-is-the-default-profile-while-q8-is-a-validation-profile.md) — 2026-07-16 — Bee's precision preset and KV ladder call q5_0 K / q4_1 V the VRAM-constrained default and q8/q8 a validation mode; locally, official llama.cpp passed at 202,752 q5/q4 tokens versus 145,408 q8 tokens, a 57,344-token cost for making q8 the default.

- [Derived projections self-heal by reconciling authoritative state and expose unavailable as distinct from empty](distributed-systems/derived-projections-self-heal-by-reconciling-authoritative-state-and-expose-unavailable-as-distinct-from-empty.md) — 2026-07-16 — Kubernetes controller loops, PostgreSQL materialized-view freshness, and Kafka Streams state restoration converge on an idempotent reconciliation boundary: compare canonical/source checkpoints to observed projection rows, repair missing or stale rows, and expose failed/unobserved evidence instead of collapsing it to zero or green.

- [Immutable SHAs preserve reviewable GitHub Action v4 behavior while mutable major tags can move](github-actions/immutable-sha-pins-preserve-reviewable-action-v4-behavior.md) — 2026-07-16 — reviewed v4 action commits are checkout v4.3.1 `34e114…`, pnpm setup v4.3.0 `b906af…`, setup-node v4.4.0 `49933e…`, and upload-artifact v4.6.2 `ea165f…`; static tests should require exactly those SHAs.

- [Coolify CLI's official installer supports a user-scoped cross-platform binary](self-hosting/coolify-cli-official-installer-supports-a-user-scoped-cross-platform-binary.md) — 2026-07-16 — Coolify's official Linux/macOS installer supports `--user`, which installs the CLI in `~/.local/bin` without sudo; bootstrap the binary there and leave contexts/tokens as per-machine runtime state.

- [Deterministic presentation replay should derive serializable state from explicit inputs while traces remain one-way evidence](product-design/deterministic-presentation-replay-should-derive-serializable-state-from-explicit-inputs-while-traces-remain-one-way-evidence.md) — 2026-07-16 — for My Little Psychosis: build a fixed-tick, pure PresentationState reducer over immutable tape plus ordered user actions; make checkpoints verified caches and trace/debug captures redacted one-way observers; keep Pixi/Audio/React as adapters, use Playwright only as a test/evidence tool, and do not adopt Temporal, Redux, rrweb, or an event-store backend.

- [CodexBar can raise parser confidence without accounts but cannot validate undocumented producer contracts](ai-quota-monitoring/codexbar-can-raise-parser-confidence-without-accounts-but-cannot-validate-undocumented-producer-contracts.md) — 2026-07-16 — a five-worker provider audit and independent cross-review found strong account-free gains for parser/safety correctness, but live-user confidence remains capped by undocumented producer contracts; prioritize OpenRouter, Synthetic, Copilot, and z.ai semantic repairs, and defer browser-session-heavy integrations.

- [Qwen3.6 large-prefill checkpoint churn is disabled by zero context checkpoints while preserving flat q8 flash-attention throughput](llm-serving/qwen36-large-prefill-checkpoint-churn-is-disabled-by-zero-context-checkpoints.md) — 2026-07-15 — on official llama.cpp b10034, changing only `--ctx-checkpoints 0` made fresh 2k/10k/20k/60k prompts complete at 0.79-1.00 ms/token and a fresh 60,015-token OpenAI chat prompt pass, while retaining cache RAM, mmproj, mmap/mlock, MTP, and unbounded launcher reasoning.

- [Remote-surface clipboard shortcuts require a bidirectional clipboard channel separate from key forwarding](remote-browser/remote-surface-clipboard-shortcuts-require-a-bidirectional-clipboard-channel-separate-from-key-forwarding.md) — 2026-07-15 — noVNC and Guacamole synchronize clipboard state through protocol-level clipboard channels rather than treating C/V/X as ordinary keys; A/Z/Y remain remote commands, while Clipboard API permissions require explicit fallback UX.

- [This llama-bee.service config uses none of Bee's DFlash/TurboQuant differentiators, so it can switch to official llama.cpp with no feature loss, only a lost loop-guard safety net](llm-serving/bee-vs-official-llamacpp-2026-07.md) — 2026-07-15 — every flag in the wrapper (incl. `--spec-type mtp`, upstream since PR #22673 merged 2026-05-16) exists in official llama.cpp; the fork's 9 custom MTP commits patch Bee-only `server_loop_guard`/`server_mtp_replay` code (404 upstream) fixing bugs in Bee's own loop-guard layer, not upstream MTP; upstream issue #23577 (open) is a similar-symptom but different-root-cause bug (a shared, unmodified CUDA `fattn-tile.cuh` kernel overflow) that neither fork currently fixes. Recommend migrating, adding a client-side degenerate-loop guard to replace the removed safety net.

- [CDP screencasts omit native select popups, while Chromium customizable selects provide a constrained opt-in page-painted alternative](remote-browser/native-select-popups-in-cdp-screencast.md) — 2026-07-15 — `Page.startScreencast` has no picker-rendering control; desktop/WebRTC systems such as n.eko avoid the page-surface boundary and automation libraries select values directly. Chromium `appearance: base-select` + `::picker(select)` makes an ordinary single-select menu visible in the same CDP frame, but is support-limited, presentation-changing, CSP-sensitive, and unsuitable as a default.

- [CDP native touch sequences produce Chromium fling momentum, while remote-desktop products may instead expose wheel emulation](remote-browser/cdp-native-touch-sequences-produce-chromium-fling-momentum.md) — 2026-07-15 — CDP touch start/move/end gave a local mobile-emulated Chromium 149 a real post-release fling (485 px immediately after release → 2,161 px by 800 ms); use native mapped touch for CDP targets, retain wheel mode for wheel-only backends, and do not add a multiplier or synthetic tail unless a target-specific test disproves native fling.

- [Remote input surfaces forward mouse buttons, preserve backend click-count semantics, and treat touch fling as a separate policy from drag distance](remote-browser/remote-input-surfaces-forward-buttons-preserve-click-count-and-treat-touch-fling-as-a-separate-policy.md) — 2026-07-15 — noVNC and Guacamole forward remote button state while canceling the local context menu; Hyperbeam exposes button 2 and wheel events; CDP has explicit click-count and touch-sequence primitives, so right-click suppression, double-click count, and touch inertia should remain distinct mechanisms.

- [JavaScript consumers should receive a pinned package while source JSON and immutable release artifacts remain authoritative](api-contract-design/javascript-contracts-should-ship-as-pinned-packages-while-source-json-and-release-artifacts-remain-authoritative.md) — 2026-07-15 — Octokit distributes an OpenAPI spec as a Node package while Stripe keeps JSON/YAML specs public; for TypeScript consumers, use an exact npm package generated from authoritative JSON, retain immutable GitHub release assets for inspection/non-JS consumers, and use trusted publishing plus provenance rather than persistent tokens.
