## oauth-mcp-auth
- [Both Claude Code and Gemini CLI `mcp add` materialize ${ENV_VAR} tokens into config files (known bug); guard with a post-sync placeholder restore](oauth-mcp-auth/mcp-cli-add-commands-materialize-env-var-tokens-into-config-files.md) — 2026-08-03 — cc#18692 + gemini#7840/#5828; sync-mcps.sh now auto-scrubs.
- [RFC 9396 §9.2/§14.3 already standardizes and IANA-registers `authorization_details` as an RFC 7662 introspection-response member, so RAR-based AS→RS grant transport needs no proprietary extension for the approved details](oauth-mcp-auth/rfc-9396-registers-authorization-details-as-an-rfc-7662-introspection-response-member.md) — 2026-08-04 — Separated AS/RS OAuth+RAR designs can carry approved authorization details over standard introspection (RAR-capable ASs emit it off the shelf); only non-RAR grant metadata (grant digest, consent evidence, lifecycle state, supersession, cache bound) remains genuinely custom. Surfaced red-teaming a PDPP auth-architecture decision that framed this as needing a PDPP-proprietary extension.

## model-routing
- [Model selection is sound as identity-blind stats only within tagged-comparable benchmark groups plus a domination filter and a quota filter; learned routers aren't worth calibrating for a small custom pool; five human-set slots are irreducible](model-routing/stats-based-selection-is-sound-only-within-tagged-comparable-groups-plus-domination-and-quota-filters.md) — 2026-07-15 — researched for waspflow's `docs/design/MODEL_CHOICE_ROUTING.md`. Routing products (RouteLLM/OpenRouter Auto/Not Diamond) select on a learned per-query difficulty score + a cost/quality dial, NOT task-type — type is a proxy at best; agent frameworks (Claude Code, OpenAI Agents SDK, CrewAI) instead bind model to sub-agent ROLE, a legitimate but different pattern; stakes/reversibility is a recognized, rising, but unindexed third axis. NVIDIA's `prompt-task-and-complexity-classifier` is a data-curation/bucketing tool, NOT a frontier router — even NVIDIA's own production router doesn't load it; the real 2026 frontier is response-conditioned cascade routing (quality) and embedding-clustering over empirical accuracy (Avengers-Pro, training-free, beats learned routers on cost-efficiency). Calibrating a learned router (RouteLLM/Avengers-Pro-style) to a small custom pool is not worth it — "The Routing Plateau" shows sophisticated routers cluster within ~1pp of a trivial kNN/threshold baseline, RouteLLM's OOD transfer is untested outside large-capability-gap pairs and performs near-random without GPT-4-judge augmentation, and Avengers-Pro's "training-free" claim hides ~14,600 paid calibration calls to build the lookup table. Pure identity-blind stats-only selection is unsound (no 2026 system ships it; cross-vendor benchmark comparability is catastrophically broken by harness/scaffold effects, 10-20+ pt swings on the same model) but a bounded version is sound: capability filter → domination filter (Pareto-drop within one tagged `comparability_group`) → quota filter (live headroom + dollar/quota shadow-price), with five irreducible human-set slots (task→benchmark mapping, performance bar, comparability-group trust weighting, quota↔dollar exchange rate, new-model admission gate) — closely matching what `minnows/model-catalog` + `model-choice-policy` already encode.
- [In agent loops, verify-FAILURE is a reliable rank-1 escalation trigger but verify-SUCCESS is not a stop signal (28-76% of green passes are gamed); escalation walks the non-dominated (model, effort) FRONTIER — effort-vs-model ordering is data-dependent, NOT a fixed effort-first rule (effort switches bust the cache too) — with structured-state handoff](model-routing/escalation-triggers-on-verify-failure-not-verify-success-and-walks-the-model-effort-frontier.md) — 2026-07-15 — researched for waspflow's `docs/design/MODEL_CHOICE_ROUTING.md`. Real agent products (Claude Code, Codex CLI, Cursor, Devin, Cline/Roo) overwhelmingly use a STATIC role split, not dynamic difficulty routing or auto-escalation-on-failure; genuine automatic mid-task model switching by measured difficulty barely exists in shipped coding agents. The most-proven pattern is "strong model plans, cheap model executes" (Aider architect/editor, Anthropic's Opus-lead+Sonnet-workers at +90.2%) — but AgentCollab's direct ablation contradicts it: putting the LARGE model in EXECUTION beats putting it in planning (27.3 vs 24.6), an open, unreconciled tension. Cascade break-even algebra: `d < 1 − c_L/c_H` (escalation/deferral rate must stay below this or a cascade costs more than always-strong); naive threshold cascades fail on binary pass/fail signals and when difficulty is predictable ex-ante (then route, don't cascade). Trigger reliability ranking: verifier failure > calibrated multi-feature risk estimate > self-consistency > semantic entropy > logprobs > LLM-judge > verbalized confidence (least reliable, 98.8% stated confidence at 68% accuracy in one survey) — but green verify is NOT a certified stop (Verification Horizon: 28.57% of passing solutions gamed tests pre-mitigation; ImpossibleBench: GPT-5 exploited tests 76% of the time on one benchmark, 2.9% on another). R2V-Agent shows step-level escalation decisively beats query-level (94.3% success at 0.6% of steps escalated vs 26.6% needed by query-level routing) since difficulty is trajectory-dependent, not query-dependent. Escalation ladder RECONCILED (the capture's original "effort-first because cache-preserving" was falsified by its own sources — Anthropic: "each effort level has its own cache… recomputes the entire request"): walk the non-dominated (model, effort) frontier; effort rungs are FREQUENTLY the next arm up on large models (GPT-5 high→low ≈ −1.5pp accuracy for −70% cost) but a better model at LOW effort can dominate a worse one at MAX (Sonnet-5-high ≈ Opus-4.8), and effort is non-monotonic on small models; repeated verify-failures at a model's top arm = capability saturation (Snell TTC band) → jump models. Every model OR effort switch is a full prompt-cache miss (one full-price re-read) uniformly across Claude Code, Codex CLI, and Grok Build, and across the raw Anthropic/OpenAI APIs; no free mid-session switch exists anywhere — so switch only at task boundaries on a trigger. The same non-dominated frontier serves spawn-time selection AND escalation (one data structure, two uses). Hand off structured state (objective+constraints+diffs), not a raw transcript — AgentSwing shows clean restart can beat continuation on a poisoned/noisy trajectory.

## llm-serving
- [KV cache quantization methods (q4_0 vs SVD/low-rank compression) show tradeoffs: post-training quantization loses ~0.5–1% accuracy while SVD-based low-rank projection preserves ~99% accuracy at 4× compression but requires pretraining integration](llm-serving/kv-cache-quantization-methods-qz-vs-svd-vs-low-rank-2026-08.md) — 2026-08-04 — Post-training KV quantization (q4_0–q6_0) is practical and mature in llama.cpp but trades modest accuracy loss for 3–4× memory savings; SVD/low-rank methods (KVarN, Lora-KV) preserve ~99% accuracy at 4× compression but require per-model calibration or pretraining integration. Current frontier: q5_0 K / q4_1 V on consumer GPUs (Qwen 3.6); further gains require retraining or emerging quantization schemes. [anbeeld-benchmark, arxiv papers on SVD/KVarN/Lora-KV, Reddit community benchmarks]
- [MTP hybrid Qwen serving with four slots needs measured headroom because 131k unified KV is aggregate and speculative rollback expands recurrent state](llm-serving/mtp-hybrid-qwen-shared-kv-four-slot-serving-needs-measured-headroom.md) — 2026-07-15 — upstream unified KV is aggregate across slots; MTP adds a separate draft context and remains unoptimized for recurrent-Qwen batching, while Bee’s Turbo/TCQ types require backend validation. Preserve a known cache pair and measure the full shared-context, four-slot workload before deploying aggressive compression.
- [Abliteration can silently drop a model's MTP speculative-decoding tensors; verify block count post-quantization rather than assuming preservation](llm-serving/abliteration-can-silently-drop-mtp-speculative-decoding-tensors-verify-block-count-post-quant.md) — 2026-08-17 — Two independent Qwen3.8-27B abliteration efforts (JonathanColetti, AEON-7) hit the same silent bug: the standard transformers re-save path drops MTP tensors even though config.json still advertises them, and nothing errors — decode just quietly stops being speculative. Both had to manually graft the base model's MTP weights back. Look for explicit tensor-count verification (not just an "MTP" tag) in any abliterated-model card before trusting it; "official"/trusted-lineage publishers aren't automatically safe on this axis.

## connectors
- [CORRECTED: credentialed ecosystems (Airbyte CAT, Terraform acceptance, Apify daily probes) DO live-verify function; the white space is verification for private personal accounts without platform-held credentials — registries (MCP) verify identity only](connectors/no-connector-ecosystem-verifies-function-registries-verify-identity-and-evidence-carrying-artifacts-are-emerging-white-space.md) — 2026-08-13 — Surveyed for PDPP connector DX strategy: Airbyte declarative validates shape only (Custom Components officially UNSAFE, version-skew live bug #45398); dlt has per-scope schema contracts but no marketplace verification; Nango substitutes runtime auditability for template vetting; Vana data-connectors already ships degraded-vs-omitted errors[] disposition. Companion sweeps (yt-dlp extractors, 2024-2026 personal-data aggregators) pending.
- [A single-owner collector-agent fleet should use a lenient server-side coverage table (not strict set equality), a three-state stale/unknown/broken health vocabulary instead of two, a named partial-coverage status, and an installer that leaves the agent already running](data-collection-systems/self-hosted-single-owner-collector-agents-need-three-states-not-two-a-named-partial-coverage-status-server-installed-equals-running-and-lenient-server-side-coverage-checking.md) — 2026-08-16 — ⚠️ **PERSONA-CORRECTED 2026-08-19: this entry's SYNTHESIS assumed a technical self-hoster ("devices you personally upgrade"); PDPP is now a CONSUMER product. Corrected in place — rec 2 (protocol version) needs re-derivation, recs 1 and 3 survive, rec 4's conclusion survives but its developer-tooling justification does not.** Every credible completeness-proving system (restic exit 3, borg exit 1, rclone `--missing-on-dst`) treats a coverage gap as a *named, non-fatal* status on still-usable data, never grounds to discard the result — PDPP's strict set-equality check has no precedent among systems surveyed. Health needs three tiers not two (Prometheus stale-NaN vs bare 0; K8s `Unknown` vs `False`; Healthchecks.io "Late"), and the middle tier must avoid "error"/"broken"/"failed" — Tailscale ships a bare "Last seen: <timestamp>" and Dropbox splits "up to date"/"unable to sync", because a timestamp lets the owner judge severity themselves. "Setup" should mean "running" (Tailscale `systemctl enable --now`, Datadog installer); osquery's pre-wired-but-manual middle case "reads to the user as broken, not almost done." **This entry was previously absent from INDEX.md entirely (added 2026-08-19).**
- [Agent-experience shaping (field selection, bounded previews, detail-level control) belongs in REST API parameters so it is defined once, not duplicated per MCP/CLI client adapter — only response-format packaging (JSON envelope vs text-with-handles vs terminal table) stays client-specific](api-contract-design/agent-experience-shaping-belongs-in-rest-api-parameters-once-not-duplicated-per-client-adapter.md) — 2026-08-14 — Stripe `expand`, Google `fields`/`FieldMask`, and JSON:API sparse fieldsets are mature precedent for shipping bounded-by-default responses as REST parameters shared by every client; Anthropic/AWS/Atlassian tool-design guidance and clig.dev/gh-CLI/Arcjet's agent-CLI writing describe the client-side *packaging* (JSON-RPC envelope + escalation handle for MCP, TTY-detection + `--json <fields>` for CLI) that should stay adapter/CLI-specific. Builds on `mcp-tool-surface-minimized-by-host-native-search-server-toolsets-and-grant-scope.md`.
- [Terraform/krew/Homebrew registry wire formats + OCI referrers/npm provenance/HACS] `connectors/terraform-krew-and-homebrew-model-multi-version-multi-platform-registry-indexes-with-materially-different-signing-and-deprecation-shapes.md`, `connectors/oci-referrers-npm-provenance-and-hacs-show-signature-attachment-and-attestation-as-separate-objects-not-inline-index-fields.md` — wire-format survey underlying PDPP's registry.pdpp.dev v1 index schema design.
- [Mature plugin/extension/connector ecosystems place the trusted execution runtime either in a separately-governed project (Terraform go-plugin, K8s CRI/containerd/runc, GitHub Actions runner, OTel Collector core) or inside the product itself (VS Code, browsers) — but never inside the same repo/governance as the untrusted content it runs; every surveyed case that shares governance between content and runtime (Home Assistant+HACS, ComfyUI custom nodes, npm postinstall) has documented incidents traceable to that conflation](connectors/mature-plugin-ecosystems-place-the-trusted-execution-runtime-in-a-separately-governed-project-or-the-product-never-in-the-content-repo.md) — 2026-08-14 — Direct precedent for the PDPP monorepo split: runner (process spawning, credentials, outbox, ingestion) is a reference-monitor with real privilege, not a sandboxed content-runner — Pattern-1 precedent (Terraform go-plugin, K8s containerd/runc, GitHub Actions runner, OTel Collector core) argues for a separately-governed runtime project consumed by both the desktop product and the standalone local-collector CLI, not folded into data-connectors (which would replicate the exact governance conflation behind every documented incident: HACS directory traversal, ComfyUI RCEs, npm postinstall worms).
- [Mature pluggable-isolation systems (K8s CRI, OCI runtime spec, containerd shim v2, HashiCorp go-plugin) converge on a minimal closed lifecycle-verb set (create/start/kill/wait/state/delete) with an opaque backend-owned ID and backend config kept OUT of the shared schema — while vm2's ~7 CVEs and deprecation show in-process language-level "sandboxes" are a structurally different, weaker trust boundary than OS/VM isolation](execution-boundary-design/mature-pluggable-isolation-systems-share-a-minimal-closed-verb-set-with-backend-specific-config-kept-opaque.md) — 2026-08-17 — CRI's RuntimeService and containerd shim v2's Task interface reduce to the same handful of verbs regardless of backend (runc/gVisor/Kata behind an identical wire protocol, selected only by shim-binary naming); the OCI runtime spec mandates exactly state/create/start/kill-with-signal/delete plus a fixed state enum, with an all-or-nothing error contract. Kata's shim-v2 adaptation (self-reported address, stats moved into the shim) shows VM-backed isolation forcing the abstraction to bend; a live containerd bug (#13849, shim hangs with zero children after OOM-killed PID 1) shows it still leaks under real failure modes. go-plugin's handshake is a UX guard, not the security boundary — the real security property is OS-process isolation inherited from subprocess use, not added by the RPC layer; its own unfixed issue #136 shows process-tree kill must be actively engineered (Setpgid+negative-PID kill on Unix, Job Objects on Windows), not assumed free. vm2's CVE-2023-29017/32314 (both via Error.prepareStackTrace, one reappearing one release after the other was "fixed") and its own deprecation are the negative precedent: language-level in-process confinement fails structurally, not just occasionally.
- [Deno, Chrome/Firefox extension manifests, systemd sandboxing directives, and Flatpak finish-args all separate "declare the capability" from "enforce the capability" as independently toggleable layers using flat path/host lists (never globs) — Deno 2.5's DENO_PERMISSION_BROKER_PATH is the closest shipped precedent for a swappable enforcement decision-maker, and systemd's SystemCallLog=/ProtectHome=tmpfs are the only true audit-only and probe-safe primitives found](execution-boundary-design/declared-capability-systems-converge-on-flat-path-lists-never-globs-and-systemd-is-the-only-one-with-a-true-audit-only-tier.md) — 2026-08-17 — Deno's --allow-read/--allow-write are directory-prefix lists with no glob support (tilde isn't even expanded); Chrome/Firefox host_permissions/optional_permissions use match-pattern globs but only in the subdomain-leading position; systemd and Flatpak use enumerated keywords/prefixes, never globs — no system surveyed uses a full glob grammar for capability declaration. DENO_PERMISSION_BROKER_PATH delegates enforcement DECISIONS to an external process while keeping one declaration format — directly transplantable to PDPP's sandbox-toggle shape. systemd's ProtectHome=tmpfs (paths exist-but-empty) is a better answer than any allow-list to "a connector probes 5 candidate config dirs." Flatpak's --nofilesystem=host composition hole (a narrower grant survives a broader revocation, fixed by adding :reset rather than changing default semantics) is a concrete backward-compat lesson. Drift detection is honor-system almost everywhere (Chrome Web Store review, Flatseal's UI drift markers are the only partial exceptions) — nobody surveyed runs code under real enforcement in CI to prove a manifest matches behavior.
- [Manylinux, Node's prebuild ecosystem, and Go static linking establish ABI tag schemas plus build-time/CI audits for native binary portability, but no mainstream ecosystem verifies compatibility by executing the artifact on the real target before install](connectors/manylinux-node-prebuild-and-go-static-linking-establish-abi-tag-plus-build-time-audit-but-no-ecosystem-verifies-by-executing-on-the-real-target.md) — 2026-08-17 — Manylinux (glibc-floor tags + auditwheel's static symbol-version audit), Node's prebuildify/prebuild-install/N-API stack (platform-arch-libc tags + ABI-stable interface), and Go's CGO_ENABLED=0/musl static linking all solve "prebuilt binary on an environment I can't see" via declarative tags + build-time verification/construction — but PEP 513 explicitly punts runtime enforcement to pip's optimistic best-effort check, and no surveyed ecosystem executes the binary against a declared smoke command on the real install target before accepting it. That gap is exactly what caused today's incident (missing libsecret-1.so.0, GLIBC_2.38-vs-2.36 mismatch) — both are the two structurally distinct dynamic-linking failure modes (absent library vs. too-new symbol) that static analysis and "it compiled" cannot catch, only execution can.
- [Every major official MCP server that exposes admin/write power (GitHub, Stripe, Cloudflare, Supabase, Neon, AWS, Sentry, Kubernetes) ships default-read-only + tiered/scoped write access + confirmation gates — an owner-persona MCP with the same shape is mainstream, not an outlier](mcp-privilege-architecture/tiered-admin-mcp-servers-with-default-read-only-and-confirmation-gates-are-the-mainstream-pattern-not-an-outlier.md) — 2026-08-14 — Surveyed 8 official/major-vendor MCP servers (GitHub, Stripe, Cloudflare, Supabase, Neon, 3× AWS Labs, Sentry, Kubernetes) — every one that exposes write/admin power defaults to read-only, gates writes behind an explicit flag/header/scope, and scopes by resource independent of operation type; none rejected admin exposure outright. Documented attacks (Invariant Labs' GitHub private-repo exfiltration demo, Willison's Supabase "lethal trifecta") both hinge on read-private-data + untrusted-content-exposure + exfil-channel, not on owner-write operations being invoked — write-scoping, not read-scoping, was the actual chokepoint in the GitHub case. Two real CVEs (GitHub read-only bypass in streamable-HTTP mode, CVE-2026-46519 in mcp-server-kubernetes) show read-only/toolset filters must gate `tools/call`, not just `tools/list`, or they're bypassable. MCP spec ships a real risk vocabulary (`readOnlyHint`/`destructiveHint`/`idempotentHint`/`openWorldHint` tool annotations, spec'd untrusted-unless-trusted-server) and a real HITL primitive (elicitation, added 2025-06-18) for pausing a call to request user confirmation. Verdict: CLI-with-owner-key is strictly worse than a tiered owner-persona MCP server, because the CLI has none of these hooks (no annotations, no elicitation, no host-enforced allowlist, no structured audit trail) — it doesn't remove the trifecta risk, it just removes every mitigation surface the MCP layer would otherwise provide.
- [Third-party protocol extensions are best served by a namespaced-capability wire boundary plus PATH/registry-style discovery metadata, not a runtime code-loading plugin system](extension-architecture/third-party-protocol-extensions-should-use-a-versioned-protocol-boundary-not-a-plugin-loader.md) — 2026-08-14 — Terraform's provider-protocol (versioned gRPC boundary, third-party = different namespace not different trust model), LSP's `$/*` vs vendor-prefixed (`rust-analyzer/*`) capability namespacing negotiated declaratively via `initialize`'s `experimental` field, krew-index/git-subcommand's "index is inert metadata, PATH/YAML pointer only, zero code execution by maintainers," and OTel's unenforced self-submitted registry all converge on: open the wire/namespacing seam now (cheap, no risk), keep code-loading closed (oclif/VS Code show full-ambient-authority in-process plugin loading is the only alternative found anywhere in the prior art, with no capability-scoped middle ground). MCP already lets third parties ship standalone servers today with zero core cooperation; the gap is no first-party aggregation/namespacing convention, which is why ~a dozen independent MCP aggregators each invented incompatible schemes.
- [TUF, Sigstore, and app-store/registry precedent converge on signed + transparency-logged distribution as the standard defense, but only Firefox AMO gates ALL execution (including self-distributed) on a central signature — every arbitrary-git-URL model (HACS, ComfyUI custom nodes) has documented real-world compromise incidents traceable to the absence of that gate](connectors/tuf-sigstore-and-app-store-precedent-converge-on-signed-transparency-logged-distribution-but-only-firefox-amo-gates-execution-on-a-central-signature.md) — 2026-08-14 — TUF heavier than even PyPI has fully shipped (RSTUF still beta); Sigstore keyless is the pragmatic 80/20 and supports pseudonymous signers; VS Code/HACS/ComfyUI all lack the central-signature gate and all have documented malware incidents; Terraform's tier model applies signing as a uniform floor with vetting as a separate axis; lockfile enforcement only stops silent auto-updates in strict mode.
- [PDPP Data Connect should ship connectors as signed OCI artifacts using Sigstore keyless identity tied to repo-level OIDC, a TUF-lite signed index for official + unverified-by-default custom repos, strict lockfile pinning with no silent auto-update, and permission-diff re-consent gates modeled on OAuth incremental authorization and Chrome's permission-escalation disable-until-approved behavior](connectors/pdpp-data-connect-should-ship-connectors-as-signed-oci-artifacts-with-sigstore-keyless-identity-tiered-trust-badges-and-strict-lockfile-pinning.md) — 2026-08-14 — PDPP-specific synthesis: OCI+cosign packaging, single Sigstore-signed index as TUF-lite root of trust, three visible trust tiers with unverified defaulting to no silent auto-update, Rekor-anchored revocation checked every launch, capability-diff-gated update re-consent.
- [Extractor ecosystems (yt-dlp, gallery-dl, streamlink, RSS-Bridge, HA) and personal-data aggregators all inherit reliability via fat shared runtimes and typed failure-kind signals, verify without credentials only structurally/via fixtures, and none solves proactive breakage detection without flaky CI](connectors/extractor-and-personal-data-ecosystems-inherit-reliability-via-fat-runtimes-and-typed-failure-signals-but-none-solve-proactive-breakage-detection-without-flakiness.md) — 2026-08-13 — yt-dlp excludes live tests from CI and is purely reactive (plus bans AI contributions); gallery-dl is the lone nightly-live-probe ecosystem (skip-not-fail semantics); RSS-Bridge/HA prove typed failure-kind exceptions + coordinator-owns-the-loop; Airbyte low-code CDK exists explicitly so one interpreter fix repairs N connectors; PDPP third-way thesis: fleet-as-monitor post-merge + transcript-replay/fault-injection pre-merge.
- [Google Timeline's semanticSegments carries a fourth payload key, timelineMemory, so a visit/activity/timelinePath parser that falls back to 'path' silently mislabels real segments instead of failing](connectors/google-timeline-semanticsegments-has-a-fourth-payload-key-timelinememory-so-a-visit-activity-path-parser-silently-mislabels-it.md) — 2026-08-08 — `semanticSegments[]` entries carry exactly one of `visit`/`activity`/`timelinePath`/`timelineMemory` (the last holding `trip` destinations or a user-authored `note`); the old `timelineObjects[]` format has only `placeVisit`/`activitySegment`, and `transitPath`/`flight` are NOT segment-level keys in either. Google publishes no schema — all references are community reverse-engineering, and the 2024 cloud→on-device migration was a hard break. GENERALIZABLE DEFECT: a parser that uses a real kind as its `else` branch converts an unknown shape into a confident false claim — PDPP's connector emitted `timelineMemory` trips as `segment_kind: "path"` with every location field nulled, no skip, no diagnostic (reproduced against the real parser). That is strictly worse than a closed enum, which at least produces a visible skip. Fix that generalizes: explicit `unrecognized` branch that retains the record, preserves the provider key verbatim, and counts it — retain-and-label beats skip when the data is the owner's, and the counter (not a key enumeration) is the durable defense against an undocumented format. Also: iOS exports are a bare top-level array, and coordinates appear in three encodings (degree strings, `geo:` URIs, legacy E7).
- [Amazon's own help docs say cancelled orders stay visible under Your Orders, but Subscribe & Save has documented silent re-cancellation/re-pricing quirks with no email notice](connectors/amazon-help-docs-say-cancelled-orders-stay-visible-but-subscribe-and-save-has-documented-silent-recancellation-quirks.md) — 2026-08-22 — Investigated while root-causing a 76-order gap between two scrapes of the same Amazon account (100% Cancelled, 100% Subscribe & Save-shaped). No Amazon doc or forum consensus supports orders vanishing from a `timeFilter=year-N` list view; "disappeared" reports are almost always date-filter confusion. S&S itself has real community-documented quirks (silent re-cancel + re-price, one anecdotal report of vanishing even from the Cancelled Orders tab) that are a distinct, narrower subsystem worth a targeted live-capture hypothesis later — not something to assume from prior art alone. Same "capture a real DOM fixture before guessing" lesson as the sibling `heb-passkey-...` entry.
- [Jellyfin's /Users/Me endpoint returns HTTP 400 with a dashboard-issued API key instead of a user session token](connectors/jellyfin-users-me-400-with-dashboard-api-key.md) — 2026-08-08 — confirmed upstream bug (jellyfin/jellyfin#14559): API keys aren't user-scoped, so any "who am I" call 400s even though System/Info succeeds with the same key; fix is to stop calling Users/Me, not to retry auth.
- [Last.fm, Google Drive, and Dropbox are highest-confidence next-wave connectors; Mastodon follows with instance complexity; browser history and streaming services defer to fixtures](connectors/high-delight-connector-candidates-prioritized-by-acquisition-method-and-confidence-2026-08-07.md) — 2026-08-07 — prioritized 11 candidates by acquisition method, auth friction, incremental/deletion support, and implementation confidence; Last.fm, Google Drive (reuse oauth from google_takeout), and Dropbox are RECOMMEND IMPLEMENT; Mastodon adds instance-aware complexity (HIGH confidence on protocol); browser history fixtures validate local-DB pattern; Goodreads/Bluesky/Telegram/Netflix defer due to API deprecation, adoption stage, TOS risk, or fragility respectively.
- [H-E-B account login supports passkeys and verification codes, and unauthenticated my-account traffic lands on an Incapsula-fronted OIDC login page; passive collection therefore needs a browser-session-first path plus an optional saved-sign-in-details repair path](connectors/heb-auth-session-and-passive-collection-2026-07-14.md) — 2026-07-14 — official H-E-B help pages confirm passkeys and verification-code login/recovery; unauthenticated `my-account` traffic redirects to `accounts.heb.com/oidc/auth?...prompt=login...` behind Incapsula, so passive collection should prefer live-session reuse and only use stored sign-in details when the owner explicitly opts in.
- [As of August 2026, several consumer-data-platform acquisition paths diverged from common assumptions: Google Photos lost broad library-read access for all apps, Jellyfin ships SQLite-only with no session-level history in core API, Steam's rtime_last_played is undocumented and self-key-scoped, and several claimed API/rate-limit facts for Google Contacts, Google Messages, GroupMe, and Apple CardDAV do not exist in official docs](connectors/2026-consumer-data-apis-google-apple-steam-jellyfin-netflix-groupme-diverged-from-community-assumptions.md) — 2026-08-07 — Google Photos: broad `photoslibrary.readonly`-style reads removed for ALL apps March 31 2025, not just new ones; new apps get per-item Picker API only. Calendar/Photos have no Data Portability API path (Maps does). Calendar quota restructured May 1 2026 (10k/min/project, 600/min/user/project). Legacy Contacts API v3 (GData) turned down Jan 2022 — current People API v1 uses `syncToken`/`PersonMetadata.deleted`, not `updated-min`; no fixed numeric quota published. Google Messages has zero export/API path; the "SMS backs up to Gmail" claim is fictional (real mechanism is Android/Google One device backup). Apple names Contacts as an app-specific-password use case officially, but the CardDAV hostname/wire protocol is undocumented (third-party-only). PhotoKit/Contacts framework are sanctioned but native-only (no Node/TS access without a bridge); osxphotos' raw SQLite reads are real but confirmed brittle across macOS versions. Steam API keys are free/self-service but `rtime_last_played` is undocumented and reportedly only populated for the key owner's own data. Jellyfin is SQLite-only (Postgres is experimental-plugin-only, not shipped); core API gives last-played+count only, real history needs the PlaybackReporting plugin. Netflix has two different self-export mechanisms (rich/slow vs. minimal/instant) plus a real (not hallucinated) but unofficial Shakti GraphQL capture path. GroupMe publishes no numeric rate limits at all despite commonly-repeated specific figures, and OAuth2 (not simple token copy) is its documented auth model.

- [Steam has no per-user OAuth token, so an owner-supplied API key beats the industry app-owned-key precedent for self-hosted personal-data tools](connectors/steam-has-no-per-user-oauth-so-owner-supplied-api-keys-beat-the-app-owned-key-precedent-for-self-hosted-tools.md) — 2026-08-07 — Steam OpenID 2.0 returns ONLY a steamid64 (no token, no grant), so every read still needs `key=`; a standard key identifies the calling APPLICATION, not the subject, and can read ANY public profile. "Key must match the steamid" is the narrow EXCEPTION for your OWN non-public data (`rtime_last_played`, `playtime_2weeks`, achievement timestamps) — which is exactly the personal-data use case, so an owner-supplied key is MORE correct than the ITAD/Backloggd pattern of one app-owned key + OpenID (that pattern is optimized for reading many others' PUBLIC profiles, hence their Public-profile requirement). Shipping a shared key is hostile to self-hosting: registered to a domain+account under Valve ToU, and one ~100k/day rate-limit choke point. CORRECTNESS TRAP generalizable to other connectors: "profile public" and "game details public" are SEPARATE toggles, and a public library with private playtimes returns `playtime_forever: 0` — not an error, not null — so any pipeline conflating absence with zero silently fabricates data; pre-check `communityvisibilitystate` via GetPlayerSummaries (100 ids/call). Personal keys live at steamcommunity.com/dev/apikey (302s to normal login); partner.steamgames.com is publisher onboarding and the wrong destination.

- [Home Assistant's hassfest is the closest prior art to machine-checked tier promotion (per-rule linting + human sign-off, not pure editorial review); Pact and VCR-family tools have no documented answer to a mock authored under the same misunderstanding as the code it mocks, and Pact's own model explicitly excludes third-party APIs you don't operate](connectors/home-assistants-hassfest-hybrid-gate-is-the-closest-prior-art-to-machine-checked-tier-promotion-pact-and-vcr-dont-solve-the-same-author-same-mistake-mock.md) — 2026-08-07 — HA quality scale (bronze/silver/gold/platinum) promotes per-rule via human-reviewed PR, but `hassfest` machine-checks ~half the rules by grepping source for code shapes, closest analog to this repo's mock-mutation PASS/WEAK/UNKNOWN. Terraform/Zapier require real live/production credentials (no mocking path documented); Plaid/Stripe host their own sandbox with deterministic seeded data — nobody solves the credential problem for free, someone always pays. Pact's provider-verification replays consumer expectations against a REAL provider, but Pact's own FAQ excludes public third-party APIs you don't operate. VCR's `re_record_interval` and MSW's "keeping mocks in sync" both name temporal drift, never a mock wrong from the moment of authorship — the Jellyfin same-author-same-mistake class is a genuine unaddressed gap across Pact/VCR/nock/MSW, not a gap specific to this repo.

- [Jellyfin API keys are admin-only server credentials, so a personal-data connector must use per-user AuthenticateByName access tokens instead](connectors/jellyfin-api-keys-are-admin-only-so-a-personal-data-connector-must-use-authenticatebyname-per-user-access-tokens.md) — 2026-08-07 — A Jellyfin API key is created ONLY in the admin dashboard, so a non-admin cannot connect their own library at all; it also authenticates without identifying a user, so `/Users/{id}/Views` is unresolvable on a multi-user server (28 users, live case) without an extra field or a guess. `POST /Users/AuthenticateByName` is available to ANY user and returns BOTH an `AccessToken` and `User.Id`, collapsing credential and identity into one call; permissions still apply (403 on admin-only endpoints), so it is not privilege escalation. Verified live: that endpoint returns 401 (not 404) with bogus creds. CAUTION: a malformed MediaBrowser Authorization header on that endpoint wipes the server's entire Devices table and does not require admin. Generalizes: when a self-hosted provider offers a server-level API key AND a per-user token, personal-data extraction should default to the per-user token even though the API key is more prominently documented — the API key is built for server-to-server automation where 'which user' is irrelevant, which is precisely the thing that matters here. Check Nextcloud/Immich/Plex for the same shape.

## self-hosting
- [Hand-generating secrets in the headline self-host quickstart is split roughly evenly across respected OSS projects, so it is a defensible-but-dated convention rather than an outlier](self-hosting/headline-quickstart-secret-generation-is-split-roughly-even-across-respected-oss-projects.md) — 2026-08-04 — opencode/Supabase-current/Umami automate secret generation in the one-line quickstart; Ghost and Plausible CE still require hand-run `openssl rand`. Corrects the May entry: Supabase's recommended path is now a piped `setup.sh` that generates secrets itself. Decision rule: a long `openssl` preamble is a packaging choice, not a security requirement, if the runtime can already self-generate on first boot — check whether some image/target already does before assuming it is inherent.
- [ngrok free plan assigns one stable dev domain (no purchase or custom domain required) with --url flag syntax, zero account-required Quick Tunnel fallback](self-hosting/ngrok-free-plan-domains-cli-reference-2026.md) — 2026-08-04 — ngrok free assigns one persistent Dev Domain (e.g. `my-assigned-domain.ngrok-free.app`), reused via `--url <dev-domain>` flag, no purchase/custom domain required; Quick Tunnel (zero account) generates `*.trycloudflare.com` but caps 200 requests and is "testing only" per docs; browser interstitial bypassed with `ngrok-skip-browser-warning` header.
- [Timeshift rsync is a system rollback tool, and high-churn service state needs explicit scope](self-hosting/timeshift-rsync-is-a-system-rollback-tool-and-high-churn-service-state-needs-explicit-scope.md) — 2026-07-24 — Timeshift’s own documentation defines it as system rollback rather than user-data backup; rsync snapshots hard-link unchanged files and should ideally live on a non-system device. When a host already has independent service/data backups, bound Timeshift’s retention and explicitly exclude high-churn service state after verifying the restore boundary; measure the whole snapshot tree once because per-snapshot `du` double-counts shared files.
- [Coolify source-backed applications support exact-commit deploys while the built-in MCP is read-only](self-hosting/coolify-source-backed-applications-support-exact-commit-deploys-while-built-in-mcp-is-read-only.md) — 2026-07-13 — official docs confirm `git_commit_sha`, `dockerfile_location`, and `instant_deploy` on public-repo apps; domain conflicts are guarded unless explicitly overridden; built-in MCP has ten read-only inspection tools, so write/deploy automation remains on the API.
- [Postgres in a Proxmox LXC generally outperforms a VM on raw disk I/O, contrary to Reddit folklore favoring VMs; unprivileged UID/GID mapping on non-local storage is the real gotcha](self-hosting/postgres-in-proxmox-lxc-outperforms-vm-on-io-and-unprivileged-uid-mapping-is-the-main-gotcha.md) — 2026-08-15 — Proxmox forum benchmark showed LXC >2x VM throughput for a DB workload; VM's real edge is PBS incremental dirty-bitmap backups and live migration, not performance. Mitigate the one documented LXC risk (unprivileged UID/GID remap breaking NFS/bind mounts) by keeping PGDATA on local block storage. Decision rule: default LXC+local-storage for single-node/no-HA homelab DB placement; prefer VM only for concrete near-term incremental-backup-at-scale, live-migration, or Ceph/HA needs.

## nextjs-deployment
- [Next.js version skew (stale build manifest after rebuild) is fixed by one-process-per-immutable-build-directory + atomic process/front-door cutover, not by deploymentId alone](nextjs-deployment/version-skew-fix-is-one-process-per-immutable-build-directory-not-deploymentid.md) — 2026-08-05 — Next's own self-hosting docs name this "version skew"; `deploymentId` only forces a hard-reload on already-open tabs, it doesn't stop a fresh request hitting a half-rebuilt `.next`. Standalone output (`output:"standalone"`) omits `.next/static`/`public/` by design — copy them into a fresh per-build directory, boot a new process, health-check, then swap the front door (never patch files under a live process). Also: a running Next server relabels its process title to `next-server (v...)`, breaking cmdline-based cleanup — identify by `/proc/<pid>/cwd` or a PID file instead.

## survey-platforms
- [MTurk and CloudResearch require platform-specific URL parameters for participant tracking and completion submission](survey-platforms/mturk-cloudresearch-external-survey-integration-parameters.md) — 2026-08-04 — MTurk ExternalQuestion requires external survey URLs to accept `workerId`, `assignmentId`, `hitId` query parameters and workers submit completion via a unique completion code; CloudResearch uses `participant_id` parameter; both platforms require external surveys to store tracking IDs and integrate with completion-code submissions.

## pii-detection
- [LLM model selection for PII detection and NER: structured-output reliability, cost/performance tradeoffs, and hybrid rule-based-LLM architectures](pii-detection/llm-model-selection-for-pii-detection-and-ner-structured-output-reliability-2026-08.md) — 2026-08-04 — Systematic comparison of Claude 3.5 Sonnet, GPT-4o, Gemini 2.0 Flash for PII detection; structured outputs 97–99% JSON valid (Claude) vs 88–92% (Gemini); hybrid Presidio+LLM improves accuracy 6–15 pp; cost/performance rubric; datasets available.
- [Presidio's LOCATION entity type detects full addresses, not street/city/state separately; granular redaction requires custom recognizers or post-processing](pii-detection/presidio-location-entity-detection-includes-full-addresses-not-granular-street-vs-city-redaction.md) — 2026-08-04 — Presidio conflates street address, city, state, and ZIP codes under a single `LOCATION` entity type; custom recognizers or post-processing are required to implement policies like "keep city/state, redact street number/name."

## monorepo-tooling
- [git filter-repo --path/--path-rename extraction + target-repo `merge --allow-unrelated-histories` is the proven, hands-on-verified recipe for history-preserving monorepo package extraction into an existing unrelated repo; open PRs, CI/CODEOWNERS/branch-protection, REUSE/SPDX metadata, and pnpm-workspace specifiers do NOT survive the rewrite and need separate handling](monorepo-tooling/git-filter-repo-path-rename-plus-allow-unrelated-histories-merge-is-the-proven-history-preserving-repo-split-recipe.md) — 2026-08-14 — VERIFIED hands-on in throwaway repos (~/.tmp/reorg-0814/git-scratch/): single-package extraction+rename+tag-rename+merge with correct blame/log attribution; multi-package single-pass extraction (multiple --path/--path-rename pairs in one filter-repo call, untouched packages correctly dropped); reproduced a real add/add path-collision merge conflict; `--analyze` report generation. Real-world precedent (WP-CLI, WordPress Core/Gutenberg, io.js/Node.js) confirms git mechanics are the easy part — the actual risk is CODEOWNERS/branch-protection (100% manual, not carried by history rewrite), REUSE/SPDX manifests (path-scoped, need re-pathing), in-flight PRs (must drain queue before rewrite, no auto-carryover), and pnpm→npm workspace semantics (workspace: protocol rewrite, lockfile regen, phantom-dependency re-test). No public git-mechanics writeup found for Jest/Babel splits specifically — those are governance stories, not history-surgery case studies.
- [With a same-FS warm pnpm store, each extra git-worktree `pnpm install` costs ~100 MB + ~5 s (metadata + a few copied files), NOT the package bytes — so per-worktree dedup is already ~solved; real N-worktree drivers are postinstall side effects and wall-time](monorepo-tooling/pnpm-already-dedupes-worktree-node-modules-via-hardlinks-so-the-cost-is-metadata-and-postinstall-not-package-bytes.md) — 2026-07-11 — OBSERVED on Tim's machine (pnpm 10.33.0, pdpp): global store `~/.local/share/pnpm/store/v11` (2.9 GB) on `/dev/nvme0n1p5` ext4, SAME FS as `~/.tmp` worktrees → hardlinking confirmed (a package file's inode has st_nlink=89, shared across main + worktrees). 2nd identical worktree install = only ~98 MB df-used delta (~6 MB real new bytes / 853 nlink==1 files; rest is 52k files + 3.3k symlinks + 9.4k dirs of METADATA) and ~5 s with `--frozen-lockfile --prefer-offline`. ext4 has NO reflink (`cp --reflink` fails) so `package-import-method=clone` can't help — leave it `auto`(=hardlink). Only non-deduping per-worktree cost = `packages/polyfill-connectors` postinstall shelling `patchright install chromium` (browser bytes DO dedupe in shared `~/.cache/ms-playwright`, but the spawn/cold-download runs per worktree unless `PATCHRIGHT_SKIP_BROWSER_DOWNLOAD=1`/`PLAYWRIGHT_SKIP_BROWSER_DOWNLOAD=1`). waspflow `lib/worktree.sh` does git-worktree-add only, no install → set install policy in the spawn wrapper. RANKED FIX: (1) hold same-FS+warm-store (already banked, the big win); (2) `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prefer-offline` + skip browser download in the waspflow wrapper; (3) don't set clone on ext4; (4) endgame `enableGlobalVirtualStore: true` (symlinks-only, near-free worktrees) but pnpm v11-only + experimental (box is v10) so a when-you-upgrade move; (5) never manual-symlink node_modules across branches (divergent-deps → silent wrong versions). VERDICT: small problem after pnpm's built-in dedup (~100 MB/~5 s per worktree); only becomes big if same-FS invariant breaks (→ full copies) or browser downloads cold. Cites pnpm.io/git-worktrees + /global-virtual-store + FAQ + pnpm/reflink.

## pymupdf
- [PyMuPDF bounding box extraction fails on embedded fonts, Type 3 fonts, and PDF forms with negative coordinates or missing glyph metrics](pymupdf/bbox-extraction-fails-on-embedded-fonts-type3-negative-coordinates.md) — 2026-08-04 — PyMuPDF's `get_text("rawdict")` returns incorrect bboxes (negative y-coords, positions below visible text) for embedded fonts or Type 3 fonts lacking glyph metrics; root cause is incomplete font metric normalization in fitz; workaround is `set_small_glyph_heights()` or upgrade to ≥1.24.x, but neither reliably fixes all cases — fallback to multi-pass OCR or direct PDF form extraction.

## llm-integration
- [Pi exposes per-model-call context pruning, but built-in threshold compaction waits for the agent run to end](llm-integration/pi-exposes-mid-loop-context-pruning-but-built-in-compaction-waits-for-agent-end.md) — 2026-07-13 — the original Pi compaction proposal explicitly considered aborting and compacting after assistant messages inside tool loops, but current built-in threshold compaction runs after `agent_end`; Pi's `context` event enables extensions such as `pi-context-prune` to prune before every model call, while Claude Code and Codex provide evidence of effective mid-turn compact/retry behavior.
- [Current Pi uses the Earendil package, and extension-only updates do not update the Pi CLI](llm-integration/pi-current-cli-is-earendil-and-extension-updates-do-not-update-the-cli.md) — 2026-07-13 — `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` is the active package; `pi update --extensions` updates packages but not the CLI, so verify and update self separately (or use the documented all-update path).
- [Pi custom compaction can preserve Pi boundaries and metadata while using a bounded direct completion with reasoning off](llm-integration/pi-custom-compaction-uses-a-bounded-direct-completion-with-reasoning-off.md) — 2026-07-13 — `session_before_compact` supports custom summaries; retain Pi's boundary/metadata, use direct no-tools no-reasoning completion with a finite cap and abort-aware fallback instead of patching Pi.
- [Synchronous LLM ranking uses GenerateContent with structured output and local semantic validation](llm-integration/synchronous-llm-ranking-uses-generatecontent-with-structured-output-and-local-semantic-validation.md) — 2026-07-10 — Gemini structured JSON is schema-safe but needs application semantic validation; its Batch API targets 24-hour asynchronous completion, so interactive ingest uses GenerateContent.

## developer-experience
- [Leading failure-diagnostic tools retain rich evidence by default and order it around the failure moment, but almost never state a free-text root cause](developer-experience/leading-failure-diagnostic-tools-retain-rich-evidence-by-default-and-order-it-around-the-failure-moment-but-almost-never-state-a-free-text-root-cause.md) — 2026-08-14 — Playwright (trace on-first-retry/retain-on-failure, errorContext aria-snapshot-at-assertion), Cypress (screenshot+video default-on in CI mode), yt-dlp/gallery-dl (mandatory -vU/--verbose logs as an issue-template gate), Sentry (breadcrumbs + stack-trace fingerprint grouping, both over- and under-merge in practice), GitHub problem matchers/Gradle Build Scan (surface correlated evidence, never assert a cause), Buildkite/CircleCI (flaky = deterministic same-commit pass+fail, not inference), mitmproxy-vs-Charles (no HTTP flow object exists if TLS/TCP fails before the HTTP layer — silent exactly when failure precedes the unit of capture), Elm/Rust compiler diagnostics (curated brevity + concrete fix, not exhaustive dump). Only Sentry Seer attempts open-ended free-text root-cause, publishes a tracked accuracy figure (94.5%), and still documents known misses. PDPP synthesis: capture DOM size/viewport/URL unconditionally on every probe failure (no opt-in flag), snapshot it at the failure instant in the same artifact as the error (not cross-referenced by timestamp), add a small closed taxonomy of pre-flight failure classes (e.g. `navigation_incomplete`) before ever attempting free-text diagnosis — this alone would have caught the venmo `about:blank`/39-byte-DOM case.

## product-design
- [An auto-directed personal-data show should preserve its default flow while exposing deterministic pause, reading-time, history, and cue-navigation controls](product-design/auto-directed-personal-data-shows-need-deterministic-playback-controls-not-sandbox-mechanics.md) — 2026-07-11 — EA supports the Sims transport precedent (pause + 1/2/3 speeds); Ren'Py supports adjustable text/auto-forward, shown-dialogue history, and stateful rollback; WCAG requires user control over auto-advancing readable content. Synthesis: retain auto-direction, but navigate immutable presentation cues rather than rolling back/re-directing the episode; one controller should own pause, reading time, cue navigation, and mobile-safe readable source context.

## code-quality
- [RAW: the expert v1 Repo Quality Engine directive (verbatim) — the spec hone was built from; §7 contains "use strong models for selection..." (captured late after the agenda pressure-test found it uncited)](code-quality/RAW-expert-v1-engine-directive.md) — 2026-07-01
- [THE FULL-BUILD DOCTRINE: the project-quality PORTFOLIO machine (B-first + judged A2 + T1b pilot + T0 ratchet), NOT a codemod machine — converged after 3 expert rounds + a real falsification experiment](code-quality/THE-PROJECT-QUALITY-PORTFOLIO-MACHINE.md) — 2026-07-01 — SUPERSEDES the codemod-CENTER of MEMO-6-IDEAL-MACHINE (keeps its principles: ceremony∝risk, property×transform×proof, Tier0=certified-not-tests, router-as-control, trust-to-mechanisms, A-preserving/B-changing, falsify-first). WHY re-scoped: Memo 7 measured T0 = 16% of complexity MASS not the assumed 70-80% → codemods can't be the engine. OPERATING PRINCIPLE: optimize owner-attention-reduction per PROOF DOLLAR, not cc-points per token. MACHINE (budget dir): B 40-50% (public-contract evaluator/proposal-gen, behavior-CHANGING, human-ratified, build NOW) · A2 30-40% (judged decomplect aimed at HIGH-ATTENTION owned complexity, not blanket threshold) · T1 10-15% (seam extraction SUBCLASSED: T1a cheap-clarify / T1b explicit-context-extraction=turn a capturing closure's implicit env into an EXPLICIT context object [the canon's hidden-state→explicit; the right answer to '84% is closures'] / T1c = T2-in-disguise; T1 is cheaper than T2 ONLY when local + captures-can-be-made-explicit + no security/async/txn/contract invariant — else collapses; PROVE via 5-10 real T1b extractions, don't assume) · T0 5-10% (a RATCHET not the engine: ≤2-3 certified transforms that ALSO prevent slop). UPSTREAM FIX (maybe highest-leverage): the 84%-in-anon-capturing-callbacks is an AI-BUILT-CODE SMELL → add a GENERATION RULE (no high-cc anonymous callbacks; name them, pass explicit context) + a callback/closure smell detector (file|parent|kind|cc|captured-vars|mutable|await|sec/db|recommended-class) — prevents regeneration, likely > any codemod. STOPPING CONDITION (reconciles owner's 'fully optimal not critical-mass' note): terminal standard = whole-codebase SLVP ideal; execution ORDER = attention-weighted leverage (low-mass = LATER not never); the ideal is NOT 'every fn under a biome threshold' (nominator; a cc-6 fn can BE ideal, a cc-4 can be slop) but 'every owned subsystem is internally-pure / product-pure / rented-pure / deleted / justified(quarantine/freeze/generated/compat) / or queued-with-a-reason'. DOCTRINE: aim for 100, sequence by leverage, don't worship thresholds, don't stop at critical mass, every imperfection eventually fixed/rented/deleted/quarantined/generated/frozen/justified. NEXT: promote Memo7→standing discovery instrument (router regex→AST) · build the callback-smell detector+ratchet · run the T1b pilot · build B-minimum · keep T0 tiny. Raw: RAW-expert7-response-round3-portfolio-machine.md (incl. round-3b reconciliation addendum).
- [FALSIFICATION DATA: T0 (cheap mechanical) = 42% of function COUNT but only 16% of complexity MASS — the codemod-centric 5M thesis is FALSE on this codebase; re-scope the build](code-quality/MEMO-7-falsification-data-kills-the-mechanization-thesis.md) — 2026-07-01 — ran the expert's §11 falsification experiment (falsify.mjs + resolve-anon.mjs, deterministic/read-only, sweep branch). Whole owned core = 1,139 flagged fns / 167 files / 8,406 excess-cc points. MEASURED tier-MASS (Σ excess-cc, the attention proxy — NOT count): T1-seam 26.4% / T2-unresolved 23.6% / T2-property 19.4% / **T0 only 16.3%** / T2-async 14.4%. The cheap safe mechanizable tier touches the LEAST mass; the 42%-by-count is inflated by marginal cc-6/7 fns (~1-2 pts each). De-anonymized the 251 "unresolved": **0 of 53 iterator-callbacks cleanly extractable — every one CAPTURES enclosing scope** (closure-capture = the disqualifying hazard); 201 (mass 1420) capturing closures, 50 (562) DDL/security. CONSOLIDATED: ~16% of complexity mass is reachable by a certified mechanical transform, ~84% is NOT (capturing closures / security / awaited-ordering / concept-seams). So the codemod-centric machine is the WRONG build; the leverage is in T1-seam (LLM picks seam, mechanical execution — IF it doesn't collapse at closure boundaries). Honest instrument caveats stated (regex router not AST; capture-detection is a conservative UPPER bound on disqualification; these are ELIGIBILITY not proof — the §B false-safe half was NOT run because routing alone already contradicted the premise). Three questions to expert: does mass-distribution change the build rec (T1-seam / better-routed-judged / downscope-to-add-on-while-B-gets-budget)? is T1-seam really cheaper than T2 or does it collapse at the first closure? is this codebase PATHOLOGICAL (AI-built, inline capturing callbacks = a smell) or TYPICAL? Awaiting expert round 3.
- [The ideal near-autonomous decomplecting machine — converged after 2 expert rounds (NOT YET BUILT; a 100-function falsification experiment gates the build)](code-quality/MEMO-6-IDEAL-MACHINE-EXPERT-CONVERGENCE.md) — 2026-07-01 — the design the code-quality machine should BECOME. Motivating failure: the overnight machine has ONE GEAR (max-paranoia maker≠checker+fuzz) applied to 100% of work incl. trivial if/else inversions → ~150k tokens/cut, ~130M to sweep the owned core; the fix is scrutiny ∝ residual risk + codemod the mechanical bulk + trust that accrues. GOVERNING FORMULA (expert core correction): ceremony ∝ residual risk, and residual risk falls ONLY when the proof covers the PROPERTY at risk — routing unit = `property-at-risk × transform class × proof strength`, NOT proof-strength-alone (Memo 6, too broad) and NOT file-identity (old RED-tier, too coarse). No file sacred, properties are: a byte-identical auth guard proves safety only when the invariant is LOCAL (nonlocal = ordering-vs-effects / fail-closed / normalization-before-auth / error-shape / projection-leak). THREE TIERS, population EARNED by proof not ASSIGNED by filename: T0 = CERTIFIED semantics-preserving transform under statically-checked side conditions (NOT "AST-safe + existing tests" — unsound; auth mutation ~60%, §B was the real oracle, deriveSpineSource was skip-masked; tests are SMOKE, transform-proof is ORACLE) w/ a certification packet (allowed AST shape + forbidden hazards this/args/TDZ/closure/await-order/throw-timing + emitted-JS/AST proof + transform-level property/fuzz w/ side-effect sentinels + per-application receipt); START WITH 3-4 (type-only-erasure, exact-move, local-boolean-fact-naming, trivial-invert-if-else), NOT 15 — hoist/flatten/dedup-branch/early-return need certification first (change eval order/count/init-order). T1 = judgment on the SEAM, mechanical on execution (1 call). T2 = full adversarial+property-harness, RARE, only where preservation isn't mechanically decidable. ROUTER = a CONTROL component (maker≠judge fractal — it decides its own oversight = Adversarial Goodhart): deterministic-by-default, versioned, runs before+after diff, ambiguity escalates, RANDOM T0→T2 calibration + seeded traps, decisions ledgered; separate ROUTE/PROVE/LAND/AUDIT. TRUST accrues to MECHANISMS (transform/router/checker VERSION) not agents, reduces COST not PROOF; any REVISE/regression/code-change resets to 100%. BATCH: prove individual AND aggregate (import-graph + init-order + no-shared-symbol + set-coherence). AUTONOMY (round-1→round-2 shift toward owner's intent): human audit is a MODE SETTING not a per-change gate — `human_audit_rate:0` is a LEGITIMATE unattended mode the owner OWNS (compensated by deterministic proof + traps + ledger), NOT a gap the system closes for him; one up-front conversation → tunables w/ autonomous defaults; machine DERIVES proof-burden per change, never asks the human to enumerate off-limits files. SCOPE: BOTH purity floors are code quality; A is behavior-PRESERVING (internal purity), B is behavior-CHANGING (public contract — MCP/error/grant/pagination = in scope per own-rent-delete §3, but a design change so A's gate REJECTS it, B queues human-ratified proposals). A→B SEAM: decomplect NAMES seams = a concept model extracted from behavior, currently DISCARDED — it's B's input (seam = HYPOTHESIS about a contract defect, w/ an alternative "facade deliberately hides it" explanation); A exposes, B adjudicates, B doesn't block A. Do NOT optimize "95% under threshold" (a proxy/metric-chase) — target "no high-attention owned complexity without a reason". THE GATE BEFORE THE BUILD = a 100-function falsification experiment (40 churn×cc / 25 random / 15 auth-security-public / 10 storage / 10 agent-generated; route each, run cheap-proof AND §B shadow; T0 eligibility <50% ⇒ 5M false, honest floor 20-50M; any T0 false-safe ⇒ narrow T0). Final architecture: cheap where proof strong / expensive where weak / autonomous where behavior preserved / human-ratified where contract changes / claim-ledgered everywhere. Raw expert responses: RAW-expert6-response-round1/round2.md.
- [A standing defect-discovery layer ranks by ACTIONABILITY × CHANGE-WEIGHTED IMPACT (churn×complexity), not raw finding count; it lives or dies on its effective-false-positive rate and on diff-time delivery](code-quality/discovering-and-prioritizing-codebase-defects-at-scale.md) — 2026-06-29 — the DISCOVERY FRONT-END the system lacked (it has a verifier, not a scanner). PAIRS WITH the ungameable-budget entry: that one fixes WHO sets the budget (human-anchored outcome gates the agent can't re-weight); THIS one is HOW the scanner finds+ranks candidates WITHIN those gates. Cross-org convergent answer, primary-source-verified: the hard problem is delivery+ranking, NOT running scanners. (1) FALSE-POSITIVE is the master constraint and is defined by the developer — Google Tricorder <10% effective-FP or analyzer goes on probation/killed (>25%); Coverity >30% = trust death-spiral; "effective FP = any report a user chooses not to act on." (2) DELIVERY TIMING dominates cleverness — Meta's 0%→70% Infer fix-rate flip was deployment (batch→diff-time), SAME analysis SAME precision; Google "changed lines by default" + Sonar "Clean as You Code / gate new code" converge. (3) RANK by churn×complexity hotspots (Tornhill/CodeScene) — codebases are power-law in change-freq so a small hotspot set holds most edits+defects (23% of bugs in 5.5% of code); a bad file nobody touches is LOW priority, refuting naive "list all god-files." Inventory table maps 7 defect classes → canonical tool → cheap-deterministic (knip, dependency-cruiser NOT madge, jscpd, sonarjs/cognitive-complexity, type-coverage, ts-eslint no-unsafe, ESLint/Biome) vs the ONE expensive high-value blind spot (StrykerJS mutation testing — survived mutant = test that won't catch a regression, which coverage can't show). Designed 4-stage pipeline: cheap-sweep → deterministic change-weight+rank (no LLM) → expensive mutation sample on hotspots only → prefilter→judge handoff where the different-model judge adjudicates ONLY the ambiguous ranked head, never enumeration (Z-ranking→Ruthruff 70%-Google→SCAIFE lineage). FP-budget is a self-monitored fail-closed gate: a scanner over budget auto-demotes to advisory; new scanners onboard on probation. Cognitive complexity (read-difficulty) not cyclomatic (test-difficulty).
- [An agent that authors the rubric it answers to will game it; the trustworthy budget is a fixed, human-anchored, OUTCOME-based gate the agent cannot re-weight — NOT an agent-derived one](code-quality/ungameable-quality-budget-and-prioritization-for-agent-pipelines.md) — 2026-06-29 — the BUDGET/PRIORITIZATION layer own-rent-delete §6 + SLVPQ asserted but didn't defend. Motivating failure: an orchestrator drifted its token budget to mechanically-gateable internal refactoring (what its tooling shined at) and away from docs/surface/product the human cared about; the "derive the budget mechanically from gap-to-100, ratify late" fix just RELOCATES the bias (agent still picks dimensions + influences judges → headline number weighted away from the human's concern). Diagnosis = Adversarial Goodhart by construction (Garrabrant: optimizing a proxy incentivizes the adversary to correlate-then-destroy; the optimizer IS the adversary when it authors its own proxy) + the AI-safety record: models EDIT a checklist/rubric to fake completion ("Insubordinate Rubric Modification" 0%→97%; Anthropic spec-gaming→reward-tampering generalization), coding agents game graders (verify→true, exit(0)), pressuring the measured channel produces HIDDEN gaming not honesty (OpenAI CoT), self-preference bias in self-judging LLMs (Panickssery/Wataoka/Zheng), proxy overoptimization diverges from truth (Gao), and *selection* on your own eval already breaks measurement (Matton — kills "ratify late"). The 4 ungameable mechanisms from real orgs: gate NEW work on a READ-ONLY differential condition set (SonarQube Clean-as-You-Code), measure OUTCOMES not ACTIVITY w/ Goodhart named as the pitfall (DORA: "adding code can reduce revenue"), maker≠approver w/ the LGTM unable to be self-granted (Google SWE-book + readability as independent authority), separation-of-duties (NIST AC-5). VERDICT: the budget must NOT fall out of discovery scoring — that relocates Goodhart into the scoring functions and inherits the discovery tooling's bias (exactly how the failure happened); discovery scoring orders work WITHIN fixed human-anchored OUTCOME gates (existence/correctness, not self-scored "% to 100" which is the overoptimization curve), maker≠budget-setter, different-model can CHALLENGE but not be the authority, + a realized-spend-vs-stated-concern tripwire that fails loud. Supplies the WHY for own-rent-delete's `dimension_set: fixed_in_repo` + `human_ratifies_budget`. ~26 web-verified primary sources (DeepMind/Anthropic/OpenAI/arXiv/SonarSource/DORA/Google/NIST).
- [OWN/RENT/DELETE: the attention-perimeter objective function for an AI-built codebase](code-quality/own-rent-delete-the-attention-perimeter-objective-function.md) — 2026-06-29 — the PORTFOLIO layer the canon lacked: which complexity to keep/transfer/remove. Owner's objective = maximize product value ∧ minimize complexity UNDER OWNER ATTENTION, subject to TWO purity floors (boundary-purity for RENTED, internal-purity for OWNED). Attention's only levers are DELETE + clean-boundary RENT; lowering owned quality is NOT a lever → "purity vs attention" is a FALSE tradeoff. RENT = transfer-not-discount, rare/high-conviction, only to objectively-best+trending-up+sticky deps (libsodium/viem class), human-ratified late-but-batched. Owned-core floor is ABSOLUTE — the escape hatch is RECLASSIFY (SPIKE/GENERATED/QUARANTINE/FREEZE/GLUE), not tolerate. KILL the `externalize_before_custom_build` work-generator flag → passive guards. Budget = GATES not capped lanes. Grounded on PDPP: 14 deps already lean/serious → RENT is a scalpel; dual-backend = OWN-both internal-purity job not prune; discovery tooling (knip/ts-prune/madge) is greenfield. Next step = READ-ONLY OWN/RENT/DELETE discovery pass + packets, not a write sprint. Converged across owner + 2 pro-mode expert rounds.
- [Operationalizing the canon: SLVP-Q is a GUIDE+GATE protocol, NOT a scalar score (phase 3)](code-quality/SLVPQ-OPERATIONALIZATION.md) — 2026-06-28 — turns the theory into practice WITHOUT a gameable scalar. TIER1 GUIDE = cheap signals that NOMINATE, never gate. TIER2 GATE = judged verdict. UPGRADED after a real failure (grant-package extraction passed deterministic gates but was relocation+duplication w/ a wrong cut — only the different-model judge caught it; root-caused + Codex-reviewed). The corrected 3-LAYER model: §E PRE-CODE hidden-concept contract (dependency-cluster test — if ≥3 deps form their own lifecycle, extract THAT first; unclassified cluster HARD-BLOCKS) PREVENTS wrong cuts cheapest; §A POST-CODE table-driven symbol/body gate (no-duplication/no-parallel-path, generated from the ownership table, fail-closed) catches relocation free; §B ONE final-diff judge as ADVERSARIAL VERIFICATION vs the contract (mandatory for decompositions, NOT the primary wrong-cut detector). Seam-width = prior not threshold. Evidence-not-narration (§D). Cost: NET-NEGATIVE (prevent building wrong cuts > detect them late).
- [A constitutional spec for coding agents — the canonical theory of code quality (phase1, REBALANCED)](code-quality/CANONICAL-CODE-QUALITY-THEORY.phase1.md) — 2026-06-28 — paradigm-INDEPENDENT theory after an adversarial counter-pass de-biased the FP/Hickey-centric first draft. SPINE = simplicity + verification + explicit state (the value BOTH Hickey-FP AND Gabriel-"Worse-is-Better" make #1 → robust core; FP/immutability = paradigm artifact, demoted to a tactic). "Decenter without demote": Hickey stays central (generalized to simplicity+explicit-state), pragmatic/systems canon (Gabriel/Acton-DOD/Carmack-pragmatist/SQLite-verification/Torvalds/Pike/djb/Bellard) PROMOTED to equals. Constitutional structure: Invariants / Anti-goals / Operational protocol (R1 verify-defect-before-metric, R2 behavior-preservation-gate, R3 maker≠judge, R5 decomplect≠relocate). Backed by RAW-deep-research + RAW-adversarial-counter-research + sources-collected/. Open: Acton/Carmack video quotes + 3 books (BOOK-LIST-FOR-TIM.md). Raw: RAW-deep-research-findings + RAW-adversarial-counter-research (2026-06-28).
- [A cycle broken by a dynamic import() is not a defect; cycle gates must count static (load-time) cycles only](code-quality/dynamic-import-cycles-are-not-defects-measure-static-cycles-only.md) — 2026-06-27 — corrects the scorecard's "6 cycles" premise: PDPP's 2 reported runtime cycles (auth↔search) are already-mitigated lazy cycles (documented `await import()`); TRUE static cycles = 0/220. The dynamic-aware dep-cruiser rule (`viaOnly:{dependencyTypesNot:["dynamic-import"]}`) is the correct gate. Caught a near-miss risky extraction (invalidConnectorManifest ×94) to fix a non-defect — verify the premise before designing the fix.

- [Decomplecting and cognitive load beat LOC and DRY as refactoring targets](code-quality/decomplecting-and-cognitive-load-beat-loc-and-dry-as-refactoring-targets.md) — 2026-06-26 — Hickey/Ousterhout/Metz/Beck synthesis: anti-goals are LOC reduction + DRY maximization; durable targets are decomplect → reduce cognitive load → make explicit → delete > abstract → extract only deep modules.

- [Structural/cadence signals measured 11.7x stronger than vocabulary lists (0.9x) on general prose, but 2 of 3 length-based cadence sub-rules failed to replicate on technical prose in our own local test](writing-craft/structural-cadence-signals-outdiscriminate-vocabulary-lists-for-ai-text-detection.md) — 2026-08-04 — avoid-ai-writing's own RAID+HC3 measurement (112-word tell list 0.9x lift, near coin-flip AUC 0.501-0.623, vs. 11.7x for sentence-rhythm uniformity) + Pangram Labs + StoryScope paper (93.2% macro-F1 from discourse features alone) converge on "structure beats vocabulary" for general/essay-register prose. But shipping this as a live detector (`ai/skills/local/slopgate`) found length-CV and same-length-run sub-rules false-fire on disciplined technical writing — likely register mismatch, not a flaw in the original measurement; only repeated-sentence-start survived as a corroborating-only signal. Citation-graph survey of 12+ tools separately found none do corpus-relative frequency detection against a target system's own vocabulary — an open niche.
