- [Claude Code hook event schemas and blocking contracts](agentic-context-design/claude-code-hook-event-schemas-and-blocking-contracts.md) — 2026-08-04 — per-event input fields, which events can block, JSON parsed only on exit 0.
- [Fan-out research must be consolidated by the orchestrator, not written per worker](agentic-context-design/fan-out-research-must-be-consolidated-by-the-orchestrator-not-written-per-worker.md) — 2026-08-04 — Anthropic/OpenAI/Google/CrewAI/LangGraph all centralize the write; per-worker writes cause duplication+contradiction+desync (79% of prod multi-agent failures). Claude Code exposes agent_id (subagent-only) so a gate can target the orchestrator; out-of-process lanes (claude -p) remain undetectable.
- [Prompted reminders cannot guarantee an agent side effect (0-27% measured); force the call or gate completion](agentic-context-design/prompted-reminders-cannot-guarantee-agent-side-effects-use-forced-calls-or-stop-gates.md) — 2026-08-04 — AGENTIF <=27% ISR, process-compliance 0-97%, our own 6%; Letta forces function calls, Mem0 explicitly doesn't guarantee; Claude Code Stop hook CAN block session end (decision:block, exit 0) with stop_hook_active + 8-block cap for loop safety.
- [Redirect a coding agent's scratch to a disk-backed dir SCOPED to the agent (agent-only TMPDIR + CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR), not via a GLOBAL TMPDIR override of an intentionally small RAM tmpfs](agentic-context-design/coding-agent-scratch-goes-to-a-scoped-disk-tmpdir-not-a-global-tmpdir-override-of-a-ram-tmpfs.md) — 2026-07-11 — the question when an agent wedges a small RAM `/tmp`. DON'T `export TMPDIR=~/.tmp` in shell rc: it surrenders tmpfs speed/zero-SSD-wear/reboot-clear for ALL normal dev work (not just agents), has documented side effects unrelated to agents (GitLab-CI internal-step breakage, emacs-server socket-path divergence, build-tree rc re-source clobber, Slurm refusing its fast per-job dir when TMPDIR is preset — maintainer rule: command-scoped `TMPDIR=/foo tool` fine, `export` "will cause problems"), AND is incomplete (rc exports miss GUI/systemd procs). tmpfs `/tmp` is documented for "smaller, size-bounded files only" (systemd) — agents (large outputs/package extract/browser profiles/parallel workers, 771 GB crash-loop tail) are the wrong tenant, so MOVE the agent not the tmpfs. FIX = wrap the launch so `TMPDIR` (covers all well-behaved subprocs via systemd precedence rule; Codex reads it straight into its writable-roots set) + `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` (Claude-native, v2.1.4+) export only into the agent tree. CAVEATS: Claude Code has open bugs hardcoding CWD-tracking/background-task paths to `/tmp/claude-*` ignoring both env vars → keep `/tmp` writable, can't shrink to zero; tmpfs-full fails SILENTLY (Bash tool returns empty). Enlarge-tmpfs = secondary only (commits RAM, doesn't bound unbounded growth, systemd v258+ 80% per-user quota won't follow a live resize, inodes exhaust separately). MACHINE-SPECIFIC: `~/.tmp` is on `/` at 87% full → don't trade small-tmpfs-ENOSPC for root-partition-ENOSPC; keep the tmp-reaper + add a `d …/agent … 1d` tmpfiles rule for crash-orphaned scratch. ~17 primary sources (systemd TEMPORARY_DIRECTORIES + v258 quota, kernel tmpfs.txt, Codex sandbox docs, CC issues 46479/15700/31024/51814/35442, kas/prezto/Slurm global-TMPDIR-hazard threads).
- [Per-worker MCP isolation must be derived from the live provider config at every process boundary](agentic-context-design/per-worker-mcp-isolation-must-be-derived-from-the-live-provider-config-at-every-process-boundary.md) — 2026-07-11 — Claude strict-empty config and Codex live discovery work per invocation; re-resolve on every new process/resume, reject config/profile bypasses under isolation, and park rather than age-reap retained workers.
- [Agent model choice is cost–performance at effort (and cost-per-successful-task), not sticker $/MTok or quota alone — Sonnet 5 curves + OpenAI/xAI primary rates](agentic-context-design/agent-model-choice-is-cost-performance-at-effort-not-quota-or-sticker-price-alone.md) — 2026-07-09 — captures Anthropic Sonnet 5 BrowseComp/OSWorld cost–performance × effort framing; official API rates for Claude/OpenAI/xAI; Codex credit rate card + 5h model-dependent message ranges; evaluation doctrine from arXiv:2407.01502; maps clawmeter (quota) vs tokensmash (invoice) vs waspflow (no cost knowledge); Grok has rates but no vendor effort-curves comparable to Sonnet 5.
- [Install-time mutation of agent-harness configs — the safe lane (drop-in > CLI/grep-guarded additive write > never hand-edit co-owned JSON)](agentic-context-design/install-time-agent-config-mutation-the-safe-lane.md) — 2026-06-30 — recurring question when adding a minnow/agent tool: is it OK for an installer to edit `~/.claude.json`/`settings.json`/`config.toml`? THREE TIERS, safest first: (1) DROP-IN — symlink skill dirs into `~/.{claude,codex,gemini}/skills/` + bins onto PATH, ZERO config mutation (what minnows does; strictly better than the MCP-install pattern); (2) CLI- or grep-GUARDED ADDITIVE WRITE when unavoidable — harness's own CLI (`claude mcp add`/`plugin install`) or idempotent `grep -qF`-guarded append, targeting the DECLARATIVE file you own (`settings.json`) NOT the stateful parts of `~/.claude.json` (OAuth/trust/caches); read-modify-write never overwrite, dedupe arrays (`$defaults` sentinel) — exactly how dotfiles `setup.sh` does `[tui]`/`[features]`/`plugin add`; (3) HAND-EDIT/OVERWRITE a co-owned JSON = the ANTI-PATTERN Anthropic built `.mcpb` Desktop Extensions to KILL. Primary-source: Anthropic Desktop-Extensions post ("manual config editing" named as THE friction; fix = declarative manifest, HOST owns the write); Willison uses `claude mcp add` (CLI, not hand-edit) but flags config OPACITY (silently lands in `~/.claude.json` `"projects"` key); Claude keeps 5 timestamped backups, `claude doctor` validates. minnows stays Tier 1; future MCP-needing minnow → Tier 2. Pairs with [[monorepo-layout-for-cli-tools-that-ship-agent-skills]].
- [Audit the ORCHESTRATOR, not just the change: an agent that DOES the work + CHOOSES the next work + NARRATES the progress is an un-gated self-grade — lift maker≠judge off the CHANGE onto the NARRATIVE and SEQUENCING via deterministic claim-reproduction + a different-model narrative-auditor + a pre-work sequencing-challenger + a fixed human spot-audit cadence](agentic-context-design/auditing-the-orchestrator-not-just-the-change.md) — 2026-06-30 — the layer ABOVE the sibling budget entry. Motivating failure: a multi-cut refactoring campaign repeatedly OVERCLAIMED tranches as more complete than they were; a different-model code-judge caught two only because the orchestrator ROUTED it — nothing systematically audited the campaign NARRATIVE or the LANE choices. Same Adversarial-Goodhart conflict one level up (the party that acts also grades the story of acting + picks the next act). VERDICT: the orchestrator CANNOT soundly self-audit its narrative/sequencing — a self-authored, self-run, self-routable check relocates the gaming (self-preference [Panickssery] + mere SELECTION-on-own-eval breaks it [Matton], exactly as the agent-derived budget did; pressuring the report channel produces HIDDEN gaming that doesn't trip the monitor [OpenAI CoT]). Trust requires moving the check OUTSIDE the orchestrator on 3 axes, descending strength: (1) DETERMINISTIC — every campaign tally RE-DERIVED from git/metrics by a fixed script, never asserted; fails loud if irreproducible (the only in-principle-ungameable anchor; = doubly-efficient debate [Brown-Cohen] + Ctrl-Z incrimination-by-resampling + maker-checker "system-derived evidence"); (2) DIFFERENT-MODEL narrative-auditor + PRE-WORK sequencing-challenger that adversarially REFUTE claims/lanes against the sibling entry's fixed gates (debate: refuting a lie < constructing one, PSPACE>NP [Irving/Christiano/Amodei]; collusion-bust if same family — redact provenance/paraphrase [Redwood untrusted monitoring]) — CHALLENGES, never certifies (own self-preference, itself gameable); (3) HUMAN spot-audit at FIXED CADENCE on random + seeded-bad sample (AI-Control scarce-trusted 2% channel; IIA Three Lines third-line reports to BOARD not management; sandwich the auditor with known-overclaimed tranches). GOVERNING FRAME = Redwood AI Control: the orchestrator is an UNTRUSTED POLICY, a weaker-but-TRUSTED monitor over its ACTIONS+CLAIMS bounds it without redoing the work (1:1 mapping table: trusted-monitoring/defer-to-trusted/untrusted-monitoring+collusion/control-evaluation/2%-audit). Process-tier separation-of-duties (NIST AC-5 "dividing mission/business functions among roles", four-eyes "PMs must never evaluate or sign-off their own execution") is the org template; maker≠judge is FRACTAL (change tier → iteration tier → campaign/sequencing tier). Honest limit: deterministic reproduction is PROVABLY ungameable only for what it covers; orchestrator can shift narrative onto UN-instrumented soft dims (Extremal/Causal Goodhart) — require every claim to name its reproducer or be marked UNVERIFIED; irreducible-human core = authoring the gates/reproducers + judging "is this the right campaign at all". ~23 primary sources (Redwood AI Control + Ctrl-Z, Irving debate + Brown-Cohen prover-estimator, Leike RRM/Christiano amplification/Hubinger market-making, Bowman+Cotra sandwiching, IIA Three Lines, NIST AC-5, + reused Garrabrant/Matton/Panickssery/Anthropic/OpenAI from the budget entry).
- [Monorepo layout for CLI tools that each ship an agent skill — shared-lib in dev, vendor-on-ship at the skill boundary](agentic-context-design/monorepo-layout-for-cli-tools-that-ship-agent-skills.md) — 2026-06-30 — for `tnunamak/minnows` (repo of small authored tools where each ships exe+SKILL.md, some sharing parse code). VERDICT: two distribution targets, opposite constraints — (1) DEV: shared `lib/` (Pattern 1, one source of truth, e.g. convo+uncompact share a Claude-session/compaction-boundary parser); (2) SHIP: the skill folder is copied OUT of the repo so `from lib import x` dies with ModuleNotFoundError on another machine — **vendor `lib/` into each `skills/*/scripts/` at ship time** (`make sync`/pre-commit copy). Installable package (Pattern 3) = DON'T until an external consumer. PEP 723 (`# /// script` + `uv run`) is the 2026 default for DEPENDENCIES but per its own Motivation does NOT do local-code imports — use PEP 723 for deps, `lib/` for shared code; they compose. Biggest gotcha = the import breaks SILENTLY at the skill boundary, on another machine, where you can't see it (the Agent Skills spec's "scripts self-contained" rule exists for this). Primary-source-verified: anthropics/skills keeps skills self-contained and DUPLICATES shared code (`office/` byte-identical across docx/pptx/xlsx, md5-checked); Agent Skills spec; PEP 723; uv docs; Simon Willison. Keep SHIPPED skills few+well-described (skill-index ~16k cliff, machine already 2× over — see below).
- [Skill metadata budget: the ~16k-char index cliff + name-only overrides](agentic-context-design/skill-metadata-budget-and-name-only-overrides.md) — 2026-06-29 — skills load only name+description at startup (a lean index; bodies load on-demand), but the index has a ~16k-char (~1%/2%) budget; past it descriptions shorten then skills drop (63→"42 of 63 shown", 33% invisible). `defer_loading` does NOT apply to skills (the gap behind "why can't a suite be one deferred entry"). Levers: `skillOverrides`→name-only (keep installed, ≈0 index cost — closest to "collapse the suite"), raise `skillListingBudgetFraction`/`SLASH_COMMAND_TOOL_CHAR_BUDGET`, disable whole plugins (all-or-nothing; selective trim needs forking), `/doctor` to inspect. Caveat: Vercel evals — skills never invoked 56% of cases; a compressed AGENTS.md index hit 100% vs skills' 79%, so an always-on doc index can beat a skill. This machine: ~112 skills, ~27.8k chars ≈ 2× over budget; compound-engineering alone ≈ 40 entries.
- [gnhf overnight-agent orchestrator: excellent infra, but the agent self-grades each iteration](agentic-context-design/gnhf-overnight-agent-orchestrator-excellent-infra-but-agent-self-grades.md) — 2026-06-27 — ~2.5k-star npm ralph-style "keep agents running while you sleep" tool (NOT stale, pushed 2026-06-10); agent-agnostic, worktree parallelism, backoff, commit-on-success, exit summaries. BUT per-iteration success = the agent's OWN self-reported JSON result + clean-git (no separate oracle, no maker/checker) — same self-grading model as Loop Library, far better engineered. Steal its packaging/ergonomics; keep our deterministic-verifier oracle + maker≠checker that it lacks.
- [The serious LLM-judge / checklist-rubric evaluation literature — and how our gated loop measures against it](agentic-context-design/llm-judge-and-checklist-rubric-evaluation-literature-for-loop-engineering.md) — 2026-06-27 — 5 web-verified papers (Checklists/RLCF NeurIPS'25 spotlight, JudgeBench ICLR'25, AgentRewardBench, RubricEval, CRITIC ICLR'24) + Prometheus/G-Eval/Meta-Rewarding. Synthesis: judges weak alone (GPT-4o ~random on JudgeBench; judge-ability∝solve-ability), rubrics help but unsolved (GPT-4o 55.97% hard), tool-grounded verifiers > self-critique, never let a scalar judge be the only signal. Our gated loop already implements 6/7 of the synthesis (deterministic-verifier oracle + maker≠checker + diff-model design gate off the critical path); the one gap is SYSTEMATIC judge meta-evaluation (we do it ad hoc — Codex caught a real oracle miss).
- [Forward Future's Loop Library is prompt templates with self-rated termination, not a gated/measured loop](agentic-context-design/loop-library-is-prompt-templates-with-self-grading-not-a-gated-loop.md) — 2026-06-27 — competitive: ~70 copy-paste prompts via `npx skills add`, terminates on "run autoreview / until you are happy" (the non-checkable self-grade our gated loop removed); no different-model checker, no oracle, no fail-closed gate. Better DISTRIBUTION (installable skill + catalog) worth borrowing; its self-grade termination is exactly what NOT to adopt.
- [Cross-agent hooks: no standard, use thin per-agent wiring over one shared script](agentic-context-design/cross-agent-hooks-no-standard-thin-per-agent-wiring-over-shared-logic.md) — 2026-06-26 — CORRECTS "Codex has no hooks" (it does now, 0.130+); all 3 have pre-compaction (PreCompact/PreCompress/PreCompact); verified event-mapping table; no abstraction library is mature (weykon/agent-hooks stale, context-mode embeds it) and no standard — practitioner pattern is one shared script + per-agent config stubs (the sync-mcps.sh pattern).
- [Research-capture is unreliable as an instruction — needs a PreCompact hook](agentic-context-design/research-capture-is-unreliable-as-instruction-needs-precompact-hook.md) — 2026-06-26 — editing AGENTS.md doesn't reach running sessions; capture-at-the-end is the weakest moment; for multi-compaction sessions PreCompact is the right enforcement trigger (+ SessionEnd backstop). Why capture can't be trusted to the instruction alone.

The audit rubric.

## kafka
- [Per-partition offset commits are independent across consumers; one consumer's missing or lagging offset does not block others](kafka/per-partition-offset-commits-are-independent-across-consumers.md) — 2026-08-17 — each partition tracks offsets independently; a consumer with no committed offset applies `auto.offset.reset` (earliest/latest/none) only for that partition, isolated from other consumers' positions. Rebalancing is the only group-wide coordination event; processing speed does not trigger rebalance. At small scale (25 consumers, 1.4M events), per-partition isolation eliminates coupling overhead.

## session-ux
- [A clawmeter-style Federation contributor tray app should reuse clawmeter's Go + fyne.io/systray stack and wrap the existing CLI as a thin spawn-per-command NDJSON renderer, not reimplement the loop](session-ux/federation-contributor-tray-app-should-reuse-clawmeters-go-systray-stack-and-wrap-the-cli-as-a-thin-ndjson-renderer.md) — 2026-07-21 — three converging research passes for the Waspflow Federation GUI. Framework: Go+fyne.io/systray (clawmeter's ACTUAL stack is tray-lib+beeep+pkg/browser, NOT full fyne) reused wholesale incl. brew/`.app`/autostart/deb/rpm; Tauri/Electron are pure added cost for the tiny surface. Architecture: spawn-per-command NDJSON renderer over the shipped `waspflow federation --json` CLI (GitHub-Desktop-vs-gh = the reimplementation anti-pattern); stderr=opaque progress, stdout-JSON=contract; add a `schema_version` field + golden CI test before the GUI depends on it; daemon+event-endpoint deferred until multi-observer/restart-durable. UX: 3-state tray icon (contributing/paused/action-needed, needs-login is NOT "offline"), paste-one-field join (deep link/command/token), GitHub-CLI device-flow for `awaiting_browser{url}` + honest numbered card for `auth_required_manual`, Docker-Sandboxes 3-part safety copy + `trusted{key_id}` badge, requester submit-form→lifecycle-stepper→decoupled "result ready". Prior art: BOINC/Folding@home/Salad/Honeygain/Storj, Syncthing/Ollama/Docker-Desktop daemons, Flamenco/Deadline/SageMaker submitters.

- [Agent terminal fleets need explicit task metadata and compositor placement manifests, not process-name window lists](session-ux/agent-terminal-fleets-need-explicit-task-metadata-and-compositor-placement-manifests.md) — 2026-07-06 — researched Tim's Kubuntu/KDE Wayland + kitty + grouped tmux + Claude/Codex fleet. FINDING: Claude/Codex now expose first-party metadata surfaces (Claude statusline/hooks/agent view; Codex `/title`, `/statusline`, hooks), so task naming should use hook/sidecar metadata and render it in tmux, not rely on process names or cwd-only auto-renamers. For switching, community consensus is fzf-in-tmux-popup/choose-tree lineage; `tmux-agent-status` is the closest new agent-aware prior art. For full desktop resume, no OOTB consensus tool restores 26 kitty OS windows across KDE virtual desktops mapped to tmux windows; pragmatic path is explicit kitty/KWin identity + placement manifests, with Wayland xdg-session-management as an improving but insufficient substrate.

- [tmux routes alerts via per-window monitor flags and session-scoped actions, and swallows OSC notifications unless DCS-wrapped](session-ux/tmux-routes-alerts-via-per-window-monitor-flags-and-session-scoped-actions-and-swallows-osc-notifications-unless-dcs-wrapped.md) — 2026-07-16 — OSC 9/99/777 do not cross tmux; the robust path is alert-* hooks/watchers OUTSIDE tmux → notify-send/kitten @; monitor-* are window options (shared across clones), bell-action/set-titles are session options (per-clone).
- [KDE Plasma window attention is compositor-owned via focus-stealing prevention, and only window title (not app_id) is runtime-dynamic](session-ux/kde-plasma-window-attention-is-compositor-owned-via-focus-stealing-prevention-and-only-title-not-app-id-is-runtime-dynamic.md) — 2026-07-16 — a declined activation becomes the demands-attention taskbar highlight; notify-send -u critical never auto-expires; per-type icons would require per-launch --class (startup-time only).
- [The universal command-done notification pattern gates on terminal focus/visibility via preexec/precmd, with iTerm2's Triggers as the benchmark attention set](session-ux/shell-and-iterm2-attention-patterns-gate-command-done-notifications-on-terminal-focus-via-preexec-precmd-and-triggers.md) — 2026-07-16 — zsh/bash-preexec command-done gated on focus (use kitten @ ls, not X11 tools); iTerm2 Triggers (regex → action) is the service-error-burst pattern worth porting; kitty/tmux ship no native trigger engine.

## standards-body-sites
- [Credible standards/protocol sites split a neutral org shell from a separate, austere, dated-and-permalinked normative spec surface — and earn authority by conspicuous restraint, not marketing](standards-body-sites/credible-young-protocol-site-splits-neutral-org-shell-from-versioned-normative-spec-surface.md) — 2026-07-09 — W3C/IETF/OpenAPI/MCP/OAuth/json-schema/Solid/DTI: two-surface pattern (org shell for legitimacy+onboarding, spec surface for authority+citability), header contract (status banner, dated version + latest pointer + editor's draft, named editors, RFC2119 keywords, changelog), neutrality via foundation affiliation or anti-favoritism, implementations always delegated to a separate registry.
- [Neutral-standard-plus-network sites keep specs vendor-silent by default; neutrality actually leaks at the ecosystem/directory/about-page layer, and the two sites that avoid this (OpenID Connect, W3C DID/VC) replace editorial naming with a procedural conformance gate](standards-body-sites/neutral-standard-vs-implementer-neutrality-fails-at-the-ecosystem-directory-layer-not-the-spec-layer.md) — 2026-08-04 — x402 did not launch neutral (2025 whitepaper: "developed by Coinbase") and became neutral only via a dated 2026 Linux Foundation transfer with a 40-member cross-competitor TSC roster; legacy artifacts (old whitepaper, Coinbase's own current docs) still contradict the neutral framing. Across ActivityPub/Mastodon, Matrix/Element, Solid/Inrupt, OpenID Connect, and DID/VC, no spec text ever over-names its implementer — the failure is always one layer out (Matrix's ecosystem page double-features Element; Solid's About page never mentions Inrupt) — and OpenID Connect's certification program plus W3C's DID method registry solve it by making implementer listing an output of a testable admission gate, not an editorial choice.
- [Well-regarded protocol explainers use analogy only as reinforcement AFTER a plain definition, never as a substitute, and RFC 6749's parenthetical role gloss is the only device serving non-experts and implementers in the same words](standards-body-sites/protocol-explainers-use-analogy-as-reinforcement-after-definition-and-the-parenthetical-role-gloss-is-the-only-dual-audience-device.md) — 2026-08-04 — Of 8 openings (MCP/x402/Stripe/RFC 9110/RFC 6749/DID Core/Solid/Matrix) only MCP uses an analogy, in sentence 3 after the definition; the RFC 6749 printing-service passage is NOT a cold open but a decoder ring landing 4th, after the harms list and mechanism. Solid's rucksack breaks on custody and copying (a hosted Pod is not carried; a read cannot be un-read), its file-system-for-the-Web survives because near-literal; MCP's USB-C breaks on "if it fits it works" and on the missing trust boundary. IETF (RFC 7322: "technically knowledgeable reader") and W3C Manual of Style ("write it for a non-technical audience") disagree verbatim on abstract audience. No convention exists for a non-expert first screen; the convention is the non-normative introduction plus an explicit Audience section that redirects unsuited readers. Structural finding: every everyday object has ONE enforcement regime (physics vs consequence), so any system mixing enforced and merely-declared terms is mis-served by every metaphor, and a concrete situation beats all of them.
- [The shortest credible spec change processes document countable evidence requirements rather than numbered stages, always state what skips the process, and keep maintainer rosters in a repo file rather than on the website](standards-body-sites/short-credible-change-processes-document-countable-evidence-not-numbered-stages-and-keep-maintainer-rosters-in-the-repo.md) — 2026-08-04 — WHATWG's famously short `/working-mode` has NO stages (evidence requirements organized as Changes/Additions/Removals); its numbered Stages 0-4 are an explicitly optional TC39-derived overlay a contribution "can advance directly" past. OTEP gives every transition a countable trigger ("four reviewers github-approve"); x402 is shortest at ~200 words as artifact sequencing (PR 1 spec → PR 2 reference impl → PR 3 SDKs). Every credible process states what SKIPS it (WHATWG exempts editorial/non-normative; MCP lists typos/docs/examples) — documenting only the heavyweight path overstates ceremony. W3C (~50k words) and IETF (10 steps) describe DOCUMENT MATURITY not change steps: wrong scale for a small project. On maintainers, 4 of 6 (MCP/Solid/x402/Kubernetes/OpenTelemetry/LFDT) name NO individuals on the website and none publish handles or avatars; MCP is the outlier and lists bare names with no affiliations ("membership is for individuals, not companies"). LFDT: a lab is "an LF Decentralized Trust lab", never "Hyperledger"-prefixed, never Graduated/Incubating; the logo icon "is not permitted to be detached from the primary logo", so no inline LFDT glyph; hyperledger.org now 301s to lfdecentralizedtrust.org.

## systemd
- [A Bash exec wrapper can preserve direct service ownership while process substitutions keep line-filtered logs](systemd/exec-wrapper-preserves-server-ownership-while-process-substitutions-filter-logs.md) — 2026-07-13 — `exec` makes the server the service main process while asynchronous process substitutions retain narrow stdout/stderr line filtering.
- [`timeout` only bounds wall-clock time and `ulimit -t` is ignorable, so `systemd-run --user --unit=<name> --pty -p CPUQuota=N% -p RuntimeMaxSec=<n>` (service mode, not scope) is the kernel-enforced, TTY-preserving ceiling on a CPU-bound interactive script](systemd/systemd-run-user-scope-with-cpuquota-bounds-a-cpu-bound-runaway-that-timeout-and-ulimit-t-cannot.md) — 2026-08-29 — CORRECTED same-day: an agent research report recommended `--scope --pty`, but `--pty` is documented AND empirically confirmed incompatible with `--scope`; bare `--scope` connects no TTY at all. Service mode (`--unit=`) empirically verified instead: connects a real TTY, blocks synchronously, preserves exit code, and `RuntimeMaxSec=2` killed a 30s sleep at ~2.1s. `CPUQuota=` is systemd's direct wrapper over cgroup v2 `cpu.max`, unignorable by the contained process. Motivated by 7 orphaned `cleanup --clean` instances that accumulated 1000+ CPU-minutes from a busy-spin bug (see `shell-scripting/bash-wait-n-...`); lesson: agent-cited primary sources can still be combined into an untested, wrong recommendation — verify combinations, not just individual claims.
- [A plain `env VAR=value` prefix (or shell `export`) before `systemd-run --user` has NO effect inside the spawned unit — only `--setenv=NAME` crosses the boundary, and the failure is silent, not an error](systemd/env-var-prefix-before-systemd-run-user-is-silently-ignored-inside-the-unit-only-setenv-crosses-the-boundary.md) — 2026-08-29 — Empirically confirmed: an `export`ed var and an `env VAR=... systemd-run` prefix both fail to reach the unit; `--setenv=VAR` (with or without `=VALUE`, pulling from the caller's env when omitted) works correctly. `HOME` resolves to the REAL user's home by default inside a `--user` unit, so an attempted `env HOME=/scratch ...` override doesn't error — it silently runs against the real `$HOME`. This caused a real incident: an ad-hoc debug command during development of the `cleanup --clean` containment wrapper (see the CPUQuota entry above) used this exact broken pattern and deleted 3 real `node_modules` dirs. Rule: any script/wrapper that internally re-execs via `systemd-run --user` must explicitly `--setenv=` every variable it reads — a var missing from that list silently reverts to the real machine's value, not an empty/error state.

## linux-cgroups
- [cgroup v2 (and v1) effective cpu.max/memory.max is the MINIMUM across every ancestor, not the nearest ancestor file found](linux-cgroups/cgroup-v2-effective-quota-is-min-across-ancestor-chain-not-nearest-file.md) — 2026-08-10 — Live-reproduced with real nested systemd cgroups: child leaf's own cpu.max can be numerically looser than a parent's (kernel doesn't reject the write), but the kernel still enforces the tighter ancestor (measured: 0.1-core parent throttled a leaf whose own file read 4.0 cores). A "walk to nearest ancestor with the file" userspace reader is unsound; correct is min() across all ancestors that have the file.

## dns-apex-aliasing
- [Cloudflare CNAME-flattened apex records do not reliably survive a zone-file export/import into DNSimple (or other providers) and should be manually recreated as that provider's own apex-alias record type](dns-apex-aliasing/cloudflare-cname-flattening-does-not-reliably-survive-zone-file-export-to-dnsimple-or-other-providers.md) — 2026-08-21 — Apex-alias tricks (Cloudflare CNAME flattening, DNSimple/Route53 ALIAS, ANAME) are live computations on the origin provider's own nameservers, not stored zone data, so a BIND zone-file export/import will not carry the aliasing behavior — that one record must be manually recreated as the destination provider's native apex-alias type. Confirmed live that LF/LFDT's DNS platform is DNSimple via `dig NS` on hyperledger.org/lfdt.org/lfdecentralizedtrust.org.

## open-source-foundation-governance
- [LF Decentralized Trust (LFDT) is chartered as a Linux Foundation Directed Fund, not a Series of LF Projects LLC, and LFDT Labs sites carry no consistent legal footer boilerplate](open-source-foundation-governance/lfdt-is-a-directed-fund-not-a-series-of-lf-projects-llc-and-lfdt-labs-carry-no-standard-footer-boilerplate.md) — 2026-08-04 — x402's "Copyright (c) x402 a Series of LF Projects, LLC" line reflects x402's own distinct dual charter (Directed Fund + separate Series-LLC), not generic LF boilerplate; LFDT itself uses a plain "Copyright © The Linux Foundation" line. LFDT publishes a suggested trademark-notice template but no document confirms it applies to Labs specifically, and the one real Lab footer checked (GitMesh) skips it entirely — Labs safe default is minimal/no legal boilerplate beyond an accurate governance statement.

- [Injection-based remote-browser SDKs like remote-surface should shrink to one required transport object per side, collapse the two confusingly-named CDP adapters, and ship a ~250-line both-sides quickstart instead of the 2,948-line playground](remote-browser/injection-based-remote-browser-sdks-should-shrink-to-one-required-transport-object-per-side.md) — 2026-07-10 — Stripe/Supabase/Playwright/openapi-fetch/tRPC/Temporal all converge on one-required-arg-rest-optional; remote-surface's server side (`CdpCommandTransport = {send,on}`) already matches this, client side doesn't (`CdpSurfaceClientApi` is a runtime-duck-typed union of Direct/Legacy, and `CdpSurfaceAdapter` vs `CdpRemoteSurfaceBackendAdapter` name-collide — confirmed via the repo's own COOKBOOK.md "DX friction found" section). `examples/vanilla-viewer` (136 lines, CI-typechecked) is already a correct minimal CLIENT half; a ~100-130-line server half following COOKBOOK recipe (d) would complete a genuine both-sides quickstart, vs. chrome-remote-interface's 26-line core loop as the CDP-relay floor. neko's own docs already split server-quickstart (12-line compose) from embed-recipe (URL params + iframe `allow` attr) — the model for a remote-surface neko doc split; neko v3's own roadmap admits "easy as embedding a video player" is not yet fully shipped upstream.
- [Production remote-desktop/browser clients (Guacamole, and "every browser based remote solution" per a first-hand cross-product report) let the phone's back button/gesture escape to the viewer page's history rather than forwarding it into the remote session, and no reliable pure-web technique exists to change that on real Android hardware back buttons](remote-browser/mobile-back-gesture-in-remote-browser-clients.md) — 2026-07-15 — Guacamole's own manual documents browser-back-to-home-screen as intended behavior; r/homelab thread confirms unresolved across products, workaround is open-in-new-tab not code; droidVNC-NG treats "send Back to remote" as an explicit controller-side action, never gesture interception; the popstate/dummy-history-entry trap is defeated by real Android hardware back buttons (SO #58122565, no fix found) and by Chrome's own WICG anti-history-abuse intervention (skips gesture-less pushState entries by design), and iOS 16 Safari swipe-back stopped firing popstate entirely (WebKit bug 248303, open); Android's own dev docs solve "forward back into WebView history" only at the native-app layer (OnBackPressedDispatcher/BackHandler); the Navigation API (Baseline 2026) explicitly states "cancellation of traverse navigations is not yet implemented" — even the modern API can't reliably veto a back-traversal yet.
- [Preventing mobile keyboard layout shift on a full-viewport (100dvh) video/stream viewer requires layering interactive-widget=overlays-content, the VirtualKeyboard API, and window.visualViewport as an iOS Safari fallback — dvh alone does not solve it](remote-browser/mobile-keyboard-overlay-requires-interactive-widget-plus-visualviewport-not-dvh-alone.md) — 2026-07-10 — W3C CSS Viewport Module (interactive-widget=overlays-content), VirtualKeyboard API (overlaysContent, env(keyboard-inset-*), geometrychange) is Chromium-only per MDN's raw BCD data (Safari version_added:false); visualViewport is the only cross-engine (incl. iOS Safari) signal, Baseline since Aug 2021; CSS Values L4 spec explicitly excludes the on-screen keyboard from dvh/svh/lvh's default UA-UI tracking — dvh reflow is a side effect of initial-viewport resize (resizes-content mode), not dvh-specific keyboard awareness; open WebKit bugs confirm position:fixed still misbehaves under iOS keyboard.
- [Production remote-browser vendors (Browserbase, Steel, Hyperbrowser, Browserless, Anchor, Cloudflare, AWS AgentCore) fix the remote viewport at session start and scale/letterbox on the client; none continuously resize the remote browser to the viewer container](remote-browser/production-remote-browser-vendors-fix-viewport-at-session-start-and-scale-or-letterbox-on-the-client.md) — 2026-07-09 — VERDICT: @opendatalabs/remote-surface's Xorg-modeline + Browser.setWindowBounds + gutter-crop (n.eko) / Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride (CDP) approach is AHEAD of the market. Browserless's `resizable` boolean is the only vendor-documented remote-resize toggle found (coarse, one-shot). Upstream neko (the substrate under both remote-surface and competitor Kernel) has an open unaddressed issue (#595) requesting exactly this continuous resize-follows-container behavior; Kernel explicitly chose to lock display size rather than solve the same dual-pipeline resize problem. Closest true prior art is VNC-world: KasmVNC Medium/High/Extreme modes and noVNC's `resize=remote` (RFB DesktopSize) — parity-or-behind vs. remote-surface's CDP-specific alignment. Chrome's ~500px/400px min-window-width floor (Chromium issue 41408600) is real; documented workaround is `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`, not window-size flags — validates the dual-mechanism design. Two sources (Kernel blog, Chromium bug thread) flagged as search-summarized, not directly re-fetched — verify before external citation.
- [Remote-desktop and cloud-gaming systems mask perceived input latency with client-side local echo/prediction, aggressive stale-frame discard, and off-main-thread decode — not by making the round-trip itself faster](remote-browser/remote-desktop-and-cloud-gaming-mask-perceived-latency-via-client-side-echo-not-faster-networks.md) — 2026-07-09 — RFC6143 cursor pseudo-encoding, Guacamole/RDP local cursor rendering (+ RDP shadow-effect regression), Valve Source prediction/lag-compensation, GGPO rollback; PointerEvent getCoalescedEvents/getPredictedEvents, noVNC 17ms send throttle; WebCodecs "paint ASAP", off-thread createImageBitmap, requestVideoFrameCallback; a multi-year CDP screencastFrameAck flow-control bug (Chromium #40934921) directly explains "laggy even on same host" for CDP-screencast backends; Nielsen 100ms / RAIL 50ms budget, NVIDIA Reflex Frame Warp. For @opendatalabs/remote-surface.
- [Streaming an interactive browser (text/login forms) should use contentHint='detail'/'text' + degradationPreference='maintain-resolution' with screen-content-tuned GStreamer/encoder settings, not motion-video defaults; CDP Page.startScreencast has no dirty-rect optimization](remote-browser/screen-content-streaming-favors-maintain-resolution-and-detail-tuned-encoders-over-motion-optimized-defaults.md) — 2026-07-09 — W3C mst-content-hint spec (directly fetched): contentHint="text"/"detail" normatively forces maintain-resolution + AV1 text-mode tools; Chromium's own "auto-throttled screen capture" design doc states low-latency+crisp-text (not motion smoothness) is the goal for interactive content; GStreamer vp8enc docs recommend static-threshold=100 for screen-share (default is 1) and deadline defaults to non-realtime (1000000) unless set to 1; n.eko v3 docs confirm VP8 default + full GStreamer pipeline override + a separate first-class JPEG screencast mode (10fps default); CDP Page.startScreencast spec confirms only format/quality/maxWidth/maxHeight/everyNthFrame, no delta/dirty-rect; webrtcHacks (Philipp Hancke) confirms AV1 screen-content-coding is live in Chromium via contentHint="detail" (~25%+ bitrate win) though Google Meet itself doesn't use it. For @opendatalabs/remote-surface's n.eko + CDP backends.

- [Unified-API platforms repair a broken/paused connection in place — same connection identity + history, credential refreshed — rather than forcing recreation](api-contract-design/platforms-repair-a-broken-connection-in-place-preserving-identity-and-history-not-by-recreating-it.md) — Plaid update mode, Nango re-authorization vs. Plaid /item/remove & Stripe /oauth/deauthorize as terminal.
- [Mature integration platforms render a single schema-driven credential form that serves setup, edit, and repair identically — repair is edit triggered from an error state](connectors/connector-setup-edit-and-repair-collapse-into-one-schema-driven-form-with-no-dead-ends.md) — Airbyte connectionSpecification, Nango providers.yaml + reconnect, Plaid update mode, Stripe key masking/rotation, Merge Link.
- [On phone widths, list→detail is full-page push navigation (dedicated route + back), not stacked panels or bottom sheets — unanimous across SLVP products and design canon](product-design/mobile-list-detail-uses-full-page-push-navigation-not-stacked-panels-or-sheets.md) — Stripe/Linear/Vercel/Plaid + Material 3, Apple HIG, NN/g; sheets reserved for confirmations.
- [Systems bound a hung/zombie job with a wall-clock ceiling + heartbeat, a startup reaper for crash recovery, and fencing tokens to prevent double-dispatch](distributed-systems/bounding-a-hung-job-uses-heartbeat-plus-wall-clock-timeout-a-startup-reaper-and-fencing-tokens.md) — Temporal, Sidekiq, k8s Jobs, Step Functions, Celery, Kleppmann fencing, GoodJob advisory locks.
- [Why user-controlled personal-data protocols (UMA, Solid, DataPortability.org, Locker/Singly, Mydex, HAT) failed adoption, and why SMART on FHIR + UK Open Banking succeeded](data-portability/why-user-controlled-personal-data-protocols-failed-to-get-adoption-uma-solid-and-the-dead-pool.md) — no forcing function, no demand-side actor, required platform cooperation; success cases had a legal mandate + conformance regime.
- [Regulatory forcing functions for personal-data portability (DMA 6(9), EU Data Act, GDPR 20, CFPB 1033, CDR, Open Finance Brasil, DUAA) and how SMART-on-FHIR / UK Open Banking coupled a mandate to a named standard](data-portability/regulatory-forcing-functions-for-personal-data-portability-and-how-regulation-couples-to-a-named-standard.md) — DMA 6(9) is a live mandate with no technical standard; success cases pair an outcome mandate + industry-authored standard adopted by reference + a second enforcement lever.
- [Mature npm ecosystems name a shared package after the durable concept it owns — `<concept>-core`, `-runtime`, `<domain>-util-to-<output>`, a domain noun, or `toolkit` — not after a constraint](code-quality/mature-npm-ecosystems-name-a-shared-package-after-the-durable-concept-it-owns-core-runtime-util-to-output.md) — TanStack/urql/Sentry/OTel/Smithy/AWS-SDK core, relay-runtime, unified/vfile, mdast/hast-util-to-* naming families.
- [Privy custom signers match GCP KMS cryptographically, but KMS-only wallet ownership does not constrain a compromised runtime](web-security/privy-custom-signers-match-gcp-kms-but-kms-only-ownership-does-not-constrain-a-compromised-runtime.md) — Privy `sign_fns` + GCP P-256/SHA-256 + Vercel OIDC/WIF are compatible; separate the administrative owner from a policy-limited runtime signer because KMS non-exportability alone does not constrain signing authority.
- [Vana serverless services use best-effort Datadog HTTP gauges and Slack for synthetic failures](observability/vana-serverless-services-use-best-effort-datadog-http-gauges-and-slack-for-synthetic-failures.md) — Data Gateway provides the closest Vercel/Datadog precedent; CG can emit bounded gauges from its control pass and route actionable Datadog monitors to Slack without a new platform.

- [Live multi-agent CLI orchestrators (Maniple, AWS cli-agent-orchestrator, Groundcrew) run each worker in its own tmux window/worktree, drive them via MCP tools, and detect idle from JSONL session files rather than screen-scraping](agentic-context-design/live-multi-agent-tmux-orchestration-tools-drive-workers-via-mcp-tools-and-detect-idle-from-jsonl-not-screen-scraping.md) — spawn/message/wait-idle primitives over tmux + capture-pane; JSONL stop markers beat prompt-character scraping; cross-provider Claude/Codex.
- [Proxying Next.js dev HMR (/_next/webpack-hmr) through Traefik needs a dedicated higher-priority router with no auth middleware and an HTTP/1.1-only transport; "malformed HTTP response Unauthorized" is auth bytes on a pooled keep-alive conn](self-hosting/traefik-nextjs-dev-hmr-websocket-needs-a-dedicated-no-auth-http1-router.md) — plus Next `allowedDevOrigins` and the CVE-2026-27977 HMR-Origin fix.

- [ksecretd going alive-but-unresponsive is a known recurring KDE bug class, and no systemd/D-Bus mechanism can detect it — only client-side Ping-then-kill can](self-hosting/ksecretd-wedged-event-loop-is-a-known-kde-bug-class-with-no-systemd-watchdog-mechanism.md) — 2026-07-28 — KDE Bugs 504656/504014/259942 show ksecretd's single Qt event loop wedging on one bad request, hanging every caller; ksecretd installs no SIGTERM handler so bare kill is safe; WatchdogSec= needs app-side sd_notify cooperation ksecretd lacks and dbus-daemon does zero post-activation liveness checking, so no existing mechanism substitutes for a hand-rolled probe; Peer.Ping to a named destination routes through the target's real event loop, making a Ping timeout a trustworthy (not false-positive-prone) hang signal.

- [Modern docs-site sidebar scrollbars use a CSS-only hover/focus-within reveal (transparent-until-interaction thumb), not scrollbar-width:thin always-visible or a fully-hidden scrollbar](css/modern-overlay-scrollbars-hover-focus-reveal.md) — 2026-08-04 — Stripe docs CSS confirmed source: transparent thumb, paints on `:hover` only, no JS; `:focus-within` needed too for keyboard users; `scrollbar-gutter` is inert once thumb-hiding is used; Chrome/Safari don't animate `scrollbar-color` so a webkit-prefixed fallback is needed for a real fade; Roselli/Bailey a11y case against fully-hidden scrollbars — a bottom fade-mask alone doesn't substitute for an interactive reveal cue.

## frontend-libraries
- [A creature whose body is made of legible text needs a spine-plus-width-profile model with glyph rotation fixed at zero; text-along-a-path and the widely-cited "pretext dragon" both fail](frontend-libraries/text-as-creature-body-needs-a-spine-plus-width-profile-not-text-along-a-path.md) — 2026-08-04 — the "pretext dragon made of text" is a myth (official repo is a line-wrapping lib; the real demo is an unlicensed 8-star fork drawing `fillRect` blocks over text and shredding it). argonaut `animal-proc-anim` (MIT) is the transferable model: joint chain + per-joint width table, outline as `joint ± perpendicular × width`, position and rotation independent. GOTCHA: that width table is a HALF-width applied at ±PI/2 — misreading it as full width halves the body and wrongly kills crosswise text. IBM Plex Mono advance verified at 0.6em from `hmtx`, so a 14-char record is 109px at 13px vs a 105px-wide 360px fish — whole records fit across the body; a fine glyph fill needs 8-10px and degrades to texture. Ripple-vs-jelly is a true propagation delay, not index stagger; image-filter ripple defaults (amplitude 10px) don't transfer to type.

## google-workspace-automation

- [Google Slides PPTX import rejects decimal shape coordinates](google-workspace-automation/google-slides-pptx-import-rejects-decimal-coordinates.md) — python-pptx writes `cy="0.0"`; Google's importer asserts non-null and fails the whole file. Strip `.0` from x/y/cx/cy and rezip. Bisect slide ranges to locate; `gog drive upload --convert` 500s regardless of file.
- [Two recurring connector defect classes: setup help_text/help_url pointing at the wrong flow, and numeric fields silently returning 0 for privacy-hidden data instead of null](connectors/credential-setup-copy-and-privacy-gated-zero-values-are-two-recurring-connector-defect-classes.md) — 2026-08-07 — Found during live PDPP UAT: GroupMe/Steam both had help_text naming the right personal-token page while the separately-editable help_url field pointed at an OAuth-app-registration or developer/publisher-onboarding page instead — a live-verification bug a static code-vs-copy audit misses because help_text alone was correct. GitHub help_text omitted required token scopes the connector's own source comment documented, causing silent under-collection. Separately: Steam's playtime_forever returns 0 (not null/error) when profile is public but game-details privacy is set, and the connector had no `?? null` fallback unlike sibling fields — the tell for this class of bug is an asymmetric-nullability field sitting next to siblings that do handle it.

## event-sourcing

- [Event-sourcing frameworks store projection positions independently per consumer with configurable seeding strategies](event-sourcing/projection-position-storage-mechanisms.md) — 2026-08-17 — Marten stores `mt_event_progression` per ProjectionVersion; Axon uses `token_entry(processor_name, segment)` table with `createTailToken()`/`createHeadToken()`/`createTokenAt()` seeding; EventStoreDB stores positions server-side in system streams with configurable batching threshold (default per-ack, tunable to reduce write load); Rama tracks stream progress in internal `$$__streaming-state-<id>` PState with automatic from-offset-0 seeding. All isolate positions independently; checkpoint write overhead is tunable (Axon, EventStoreDB) or implicit (Rama, Marten). At single-process scale (~1M events, ~25 consumers) Rama has lowest overhead, EventStoreDB is simplest operationally, Marten is simplest logically.

## pdpp

- [Standards ecosystems consistently separate open conformance tooling (public good) from foundation-run certification (brand/legal), and implementers layer their own harnesses on top of both](pdpp/standards-bodies-separate-open-conformance-tooling-from-foundation-run-certification-brands.md) — 2026-08-14 — Surveyed OpenID Foundation (self-certification, suite free/OIDF-run, cert fees $700-$5,000, program historically loss-making), CNCF Certified Kubernetes (Sonobuoy=VMware tool, e2e suite=SIG Architecture, cert mark=CNCF, 100%-pass zero-skip required), W3C web-platform-tests (NO certification layer exists — deliberate counter-example, informal cross-vendor project, wpt.fyi is a scoreboard not a gate), HL7 FHIR Inferno/Touchstone (Inferno=MITRE/ONC-funded public good tied to a REGULATORY gate, not a voluntary mark; Touchstone=commercial AEGIS.net product that became a SECOND accredited path to the same ONC (g)(10) certification via Drummond — breaks the clean tooling/cert split), and Matter/CSA + Khronos/USB-IF (open SDK/CTS + free self-test tooling, paid Authorized-Test-Lab or Adopters-Program step for the trademark). Maps directly onto PDPP's intended layering (spec repo=tooling, Vana Foundation=certifier, Data Connect/data-connectors=implementer harnesses) — closest precedent is OpenID/CNCF/CSA, not WPT (which has no cert layer at all). FHIR's regulator-driven dual-accreditation is a caution for if PDPP conformance ever gets referenced by regulation.
- [Source Declaration pointers should reject URI user information](pdpp/source-declaration-pointers-should-reject-uri-userinfo.md) - 2026-08-11 - RFC 3986 allows URI `userinfo` but deprecates password use and documents clear-text credential and misleading-authority risks; an HTTPS Source Declaration pointer therefore needs an explicit no-username/no-password rule.
- [PDPP Source Declarations should separate stable identities, Core conformance, profile extensions, and authorization artifacts](pdpp/source-declarations-separate-identities-profiles-and-authorization-artifacts.md) - 2026-08-11 - Draft synthesis: stable machine identities stay separate from labels, software, and installations; Core remains independent from an optional namespaced Collection Profile; RAR carries approved authorization details and introspection context but does not define consent receipts; grants, snapshots, evidence, and portable credentials remain distinct; additive metadata and authorization semantics need different unknown-handling rules.
- [Slack xoxc/xoxd session auth needs browser-like headers (Origin), not just token+cookie — corroborating, unproven lead for the connector's stars/user_groups/reminders/dm_read_states slack_auth_failed](pdpp/slack-xoxc-xoxd-session-auth-requires-browser-like-headers-not-just-token-and-cookie.md) — 2026-08-10 — third-party Go SDK guide sets Origin: https://app.slack.com for xoxc/xoxd; PDPP's slack-api.ts sends no Origin/Referer/Sec-Fetch-*. Two other incompatible root-cause theories exist on non-current branch lineages (TLS fingerprint; stale credential snapshot) — neither live-verifiable here.
- [PDPP repo's docs/research/ directory retired and imported whole](pdpp/RECOVERY-MANIFEST.md) — 2026-08-07 — 266 paths that ever existed under docs/research/ across both remotes' full history audited; 152 recovered from git history with no other surviving copy, the rest already live on-branch, already migrated to this corpus, or still current. Full corpus (current captures + recovered/) lives at ai/research/pdpp/.
- [RFC 6764 doesn't guarantee .well-known/carddav itself answers current-user-principal — Apple's live iCloud server 404s that property there and requires a PROPFIND retry against the origin root](pdpp/rfc-6764-well-known-carddav-is-not-guaranteed-to-answer-current-user-principal-itself.md) — 2026-08-09 — live-verified against a real iCloud account: well-known answers 207 inline (no redirect) with current-user-principal 404'd via propstat; root PROPFIND then returns it 200 OK. Root cause of apple_contacts carddav_discovery_no_current_user_principal live UAT failure.
- [Privy user-owned embedded wallets can only gain a server signer through client-side user consent, so the security control is the signer's override policy — not where the authorization key is stored](web-security/privy-user-owned-wallet-signers-need-client-side-consent-so-policy-scoping-not-kms-is-the-security-control.md) — 2026-08-13 — Account already enrolls its key quorum as an additional signer at login but with `policyIds: []`, i.e. unconstrained; CG's custody-v2 topology assertion depends on owning the wallet and does not port to user-owned wallets. KMS bounds key theft, policy bounds what a compromised runtime can request — not substitutes.

## biome
- [Biome 2.5.7 does not merge files.includes across an extends chain, and overrides[] targeting .js files (but not .ts) is silently ignored under the same chain](biome/biome-2-5-extends-chain-drops-files-includes-merge-and-js-overrides.md) — 2026-08-18 — A local config's own files.includes REPLACES rather than merges with an extended config's, silently falling back to "include everything" when the local list is negative-only; .ts-scoped overrides[] work fine under extends but .js-scoped overrides[] (formatter/linter toggles) don't. Cost ~half a day on data-connectors/PDPP Move A; workaround is a wrapper script filtering a checked-in exception list out of `biome check --reporter=json` diagnostics.

## npm
- [npm 10.x's --ignore-scripts does not suppress a -w-targeted workspace's own prepare lifecycle script, though npm 12.x does](npm/npm-10-ignore-scripts-does-not-suppress-workspace-scoped-prepare-hook.md) — 2026-08-18 — Confirmed with a minimal repro: a workspace `prepare` script runs despite `--ignore-scripts -w` on npm 10.9.8, not on npm 12.0.2. On interdependent workspaces this races npm's own symlink creation, producing a broken build for whichever workspace's prepare fires second — re-running the same install doesn't self-heal. Cost a full CI-iteration round on data-connectors because local testing used a different ambient npm version than CI's pinned Node.

## agent-browser-automation
- [Coding agents need a real browser extension (not a CDP-launched profile) to reuse an already-authenticated Chrome/Brave session, because Chrome 136+ blocks remote debugging on the default profile](agent-browser-automation/coding-agents-need-a-real-browser-extension-not-cdp-launch-to-reuse-an-authenticated-profile.md) — 2026-08-18 — MCP browser tools (Playwright MCP, chrome-devtools-mcp default mode) launch a clean logged-out profile; reusing a real session needs either a one-time profile copy + `--remote-debugging-port`, or the Chrome M144+ beta `--autoConnect` click-to-allow flow. Claude Code's official "Claude in Chrome" extension avoids both by running inside the user's real browser (Chrome/Edge native, auto-detected in Brave/Arc/Vivaldi/Opera) — `/chrome` to set up. Codex's equivalent extension is desktop-app-only; Codex CLI has no such shortcut.

## semantic-release-monorepo-scoping
- [@semantic-release/commit-analyzer has no built-in option to require a Conventional-Commit scope before triggering a release — the workaround is a releaseRules regex, not a config flag](semantic-release-monorepo-scoping/commit-analyzer-has-no-built-in-scope-filter-use-releaserules-regex-instead.md) — 2026-08-20 — No native "ignore commits without matching scope" toggle exists; achieve it via an explicit `releaseRules` scope allowlist (commits matching no rule trigger no release). Path-based filtering also has no native support — only a third-party `semantic-release-monorepo` wrapper. Don't copy another repo's `.releaserc` verbatim if that repo has no scope gating and yours needs it.

## audiobooks
- [In August 2026, converting an epub to a high-quality audiobook converges on a pattern (extract-clean, chunk, TTS-synthesize, m4b-package) with the TTS model chosen by hardware and quality priority, not a single dominant tool](audiobooks/epub-to-audiobook-in-2026-converges-on-a-tool-pattern-not-one-winner.md) — 2026-08-23 — Kokoro (82M, Apache 2.0) is the fast/cheap CPU-capable default with no voice cloning; Chatterbox/Chatterbox-Turbo (Resemble AI, MIT) is the reported open-source narration-quality leader with zero-shot cloning and emotion control (maker-run blind test: 65.3% preference over ElevenLabs); VibeVoice (Microsoft, research license) targets long-form multi-speaker/dialogue fiction. ebook2audiobook is the most feature-complete turnkey wrapper; epub2tts forks let you swap the backend per quality/speed tradeoff. r/LocalLLaMA-specific consensus could not be directly verified — web search doesn't reliably surface Reddit thread content — so the "fragmented by use case" read is inferred from adjacent maintainer/ecosystem signal, not a confirmed subreddit read.
- [Fish Audio S2 Pro's voice-clone reference-audio encode step can OOM a 24GB GPU when combined with the loaded model, and the cause is reference-clip length, not torch.compile](audiobooks/fish-s2-pro-voice-clone-oom-scales-with-reference-clip-length-not-compile-mode.md) — 2026-08-25 — On a 3090, model load alone uses ~15.1GB; a 13.9s reference clip pushed reserved memory to 23.10GB before generation even started (reproduced in both compiled and eager mode, ruling out torch.compile), while a 7.0s clip of the same speaker completed a full generate() call with 22.1GB peak. Keep voice-clone reference clips to 7-10s on ~24GB cards with this harness.

## linux-memory-management
- [A 100%-full Linux swap is a stock measurement (cold pages never faulted back in) and is not itself harmful; the correct alarm signal is a flow/pressure measurement — sustained pswpout/si-so rate plus /proc/pressure/memory 'full', not swap-used percentage](linux-memory-management/full-swap-occupancy-is-a-stock-not-a-flow-psi-and-pswpout-rate-are-the-real-alarm.md) — 2026-08-21 — Confirms the operator's claim via kernel docs (watermark-driven reclaim, PSI accounting.md) and Chris Down's "In defence of swap" (upstream PSI/cgroup-v2 author): swap occupancy only grows because the kernel has no incentive to fault cold pages back in early; PSI's `full` line and vmstat's pswpin/pswpout are the actual harm signals. Host measurement (Kubuntu 26.04, 124GB RAM, swap 16Gi/16Gi full): PSI full avg10=0.10/avg60=0.48 (low), pswpout(33.6M pages) >> pswpin(14.1M pages) over 20d uptime — holding cold pages, not thrashing. Host's own earlyoom config already encodes `-s 100,100` with the comment "full swap is expected on this box," predating this investigation. `swapoff -a && swapon -a` would be actively harmful here (forces a wasted synchronous read-back), justified only for diagnostics or swap reconfiguration. Not verified: no continuous rate monitoring across the full 20-day window (only cumulative counters + one 5s sample); one LKML quote sourced via search synthesis, not direct re-fetch.
- [DECISION for waspflow: an agent-facing CLI whose consumers are shell-having coding agents should stay primary and add ONE Skill (plus an optional SessionStart push), not an MCP server — Microsoft steers coding agents away from its own 67-tool playwright MCP server ("CLI invocations are more token-efficient"), Gas Town solves a 292-verb discoverability problem with zero MCP via a machine-readable `polecatSafe` allowlist + `gt prime --hook` role push, and 4 of 6 re-cloned agent-supervisor competitors ship no MCP at all (vibe-kanban is the lone query-oriented one, and its 34-tool MCP is a thin stdio client over its own HTTP API, i.e. additive). Also settles the query-surface crux: the brief's premise that hooks are react-only is REFUTED (SessionStart stdout and `additionalContext` are documented push channels in both Claude Code and Codex), evidence classes should ride in-envelope as SLSA-style adversary-cost grades that default to unknown (OTel `Unset`) and whose absence must not read as confidence (RFC 9111 `Age`), and coverage must be structural and unconditional like Elasticsearch `_shards` — because HTTP `Warning` was obsoleted for being "entirely advisory" and Codd's A/I-marks lost to complexity, while kubectl still prints "No resources found" to stderr even when the real failure was a connection error](agentic-cli-design/an-agent-facing-cli-should-stay-primary-and-add-a-skill-not-an-mcp-server-while-its-query-surface-keeps-evidence-class-and-coverage-structural.md) — 2026-08-21 — Competitor claims cited file:line against repos re-cloned to ~/.tmp/wf-competitor-research/ (the earlier sweep's clones were gone); fleet-query warts measured live on this host (systemd LOAD/ACTIVE/SUB yields 12 real states incl. 35 `not-found` hiding among 207 genuinely-inactive; `systemctl --output=json` returns bare `[]` dropping the coverage hint the text path prints; `docker ps --format json` truncates values by default). NOT DETERMINED: whether `--help` depth changes agent behaviour (no eval exists), the push-vs-pull staleness tradeoff as a documented finding, kubectl's exit-code contract; firstmate/agent-deck/multiclaude/agent-orchestrator/oragent could not be located and are uncovered. Revisit MCP if a shell-less consumer appears, the surface grows server-held state, or agents burn turns chaining reads (the Stripe suite found CLI the most expensive modality on chained writes: 19 turns vs 4).
- ops/codex-goodwill-resets-tracker.md — OpenAI issues surprise goodwill Codex quota resets (tracked at codex-resets.com); pacing inverts to spend-before-reset when one is likely
- [Mature data-sync products treat upstream retention loss as a scope/capability fact recorded once at the boundary, not a health signal — none surveyed degrade primary health for data the provider no longer serves, and the closest in-system irrecoverability record is a structural non-health marker (Plaid's per-institution history caps, Arq's "Deleted/Kept" status, PyPI/crates.io yank records)](product-design/upstream-retention-loss-health-ux-prior-art.md) — 2026-08-26 — Third axis beyond the two companion entries (data-possession×work-in-flight; lifecycle×verdict): permanent, provider-caused, unrecoverable absence. Denominator finding, corroborated across 7 disjoint product areas with no counter-example: every surveyed product (Plaid, QuickBooks, Xero/open-banking, Backblaze, Google Takeout, PyPI, crates.io) scopes completeness to provider-servable-now, never to all-data-ever, so retention-cliff data is outside the denominator, not a gap within it — QuickBooks community framing is explicit: "nothing is broken, you're looking at the smaller of two independent numbers" (90-day Intuit policy cap vs. bank's own window). Retention loss never gates the primary health/connection signal anywhere surveyed (Plaid institution health is keyed to recent update recency/success, not historical depth). Closest structural irrecoverability records: PyPI/PEP 592's yank (`data-yanked` attribute, reversible, reason-carrying, explicitly "non-destructive alternative to deletion") and Arq's per-file "Deleted/Kept" tombstone status (bug-confirmed to persist as durable per-file state across snapshots). Genuine gap found, not resolved by prior art: no surveyed financial aggregator exposes a structured "provider confirmed cutoff date" field — the boundary lives only in help-center prose. PDPP recommendation: a third `coverage`/`provenance` axis (`coverage_horizon: {earliest_available, confirmed_at, basis}`) separate from both the lifecycle and verdict axes in the companion entries, with `basis` as a closed machine vocabulary (`provider_retention_policy`/`provider_deletion_confirmed`/`provider_never_had_it`/`regulatory_consent_window`) so "confirmed gone" is a checkable record with provenance, not asserted prose — and FAQ-register copy ("nothing is broken") rather than incident-register copy. Several sources retrieved via WebSearch summary rather than direct fetch (support.plaid.com and arqbackup.com both 403'd direct WebFetch) — flagged per-source; MX/Yodlee/Finicity and IMAP expunge semantics searched but not found citable, marked not-covered rather than negative evidence.
- [DECISION for PDPP: a global "not healthy" banner must fire only from the VERDICT axis after cadence-derived hysteresis, never from raw data age — every system surveyed (Plaid, Grafana, Prometheus, Kubernetes probes, Datadog, PagerDuty, Healthchecks.io) gates staleness through an N-consecutive-miss or grace-multiplier hysteresis before a "no new data" fact is allowed to become an alarm, and none uses a fixed global age threshold across heterogeneous-cadence sources](product-design/health-banner-staleness-vs-freshness-design-prior-art.md) — 2026-08-26 — Fourth axis in this cluster (data-possession×work-in-flight; lifecycle×verdict; coverage/provenance): staleness as time-since-last-success relative to an EXPECTED CADENCE, gating a global banner. Plaid's `last_successful_update`/`last_failed_update` are bare possession-independent timestamps with no built-in threshold — Plaid elevates only `ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED` (owner-actionable) to a verdict, auto-retrying generic connectivity failure "at least once a day" without ever surfacing it. Grafana names four explicit NoData policies (No Data/Alerting/Normal/Keep Last State) and explicitly warns "relying solely on the 'Keep Last State' option is discouraged" for strict monitoring. Prometheus's `for:` holds a true condition in `Pending` before `Firing` ("almost every alert should have a non-zero for duration"); `keep_firing_for` guards the opposite flap direction. Kubernetes readiness-probe failure withholds traffic without restarting or declaring anything broken (`unknown`-withholds-`healthy` pattern), and its grace period is a literal formula `initialDelaySeconds + failureThreshold × periodSeconds` — the docs name too-tight tuning (`periodSeconds:1`) as a documented cause of pods "flap[ping] in and out of Service endpoints" under normal GC-pause jitter, i.e. the mechanism's own failure mode IS crying wolf. Node-lease grace (40s ≈ 4× the 10s lease interval) and pod-eviction-timeout (5min, a second larger multiple) show the same system nesting two cadence-derived thresholds at two consequence levels. Datadog has no automated flap-detection (confirmed absent, unlike Nagios); its `evaluation_delay` (15min recommended for CloudWatch) and "do not require full window" default both bias toward under- not over-alarming on sparse/delayed sources. PagerDuty adds an orthogonal actionability gate on top of timing ("For each alert, determine whether it was actionable... cut" non-actionable ones) and cites Dan Slimmon's positive-predictive-value framing — independently corroborating this corpus's `data-quality-alerting-high-confidence-only.md` (Stripe Radar) from a disjoint domain. Healthchecks.io is the sharpest cadence model found: named states New→Up→Late→Down→Paused, Grace Time defined as "a little above the expected duration," and worked arithmetic showing NO alert fires during the entire Late window — only Down (Late + Grace Time elapsed) notifies. Clinical alarm-fatigue literature (NCBI/AHRQ secondary source, substituting for a Joint Commission Sentinel Event Alert PDF that failed text extraction) grounds the "crying wolf" cost as measured and compounding, not metaphorical: 72–99% documented false-alarm rates causally lead staff to "turn down the volume, ignore, or deactivate the alarms." PDPP design: banner fires only when verdict axis reads `needs_you`/`blocked`; raw overdue-ness sets only a lifecycle `stale` value (Kubernetes-readiness-shaped, withheld not broken) unless the cause is independently owner-actionable; per-source `grace_deadline = last_successful_sync + max(expected_interval × 2–3, floor)`, cron-style sources computed against next-expected-occurrence not rolling elapsed time; freshness (an age/timestamp) stays always-visible per the orthogonal-axes entry's labeled-not-hidden rule, decoupled from banner state. Gaps flagged honestly: Feedbin's staleness sparkline is single-source (a social post, no first-party doc); NetNewsWire has no evidenced dead-feed detection (not-covered, not a negative finding); no primary source anywhere supplied ready-to-reuse consumer copy for the "later than usual, no action needed" register — the proposed copy is this entry's own synthesis.

## desktop-app-secrets
- [Electron's safeStorage on Linux (Chromium os_crypt) has no migration path between keyring backends, and its KWallet/libsecret key derivation is a fixed, reproducible PBKDF2+AES-128-CBC scheme](desktop-app-secrets/electron-safe-storage-on-linux-kwallet-has-no-migration-path-between-backends.md) — 2026-08-29 — PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA1, salt "saltysalt", 1 iteration, AES-128-CBC, 16-space IV, v10/v11 ciphertext prefix; KWallet-folder path and libsecret Secret-Service path store the SAME KIND of password under DIFFERENT, non-interchangeable mechanisms — an app can silently regenerate a new one under either while a stale one lingers under the other. Signal-Desktop issue #7944 confirms no general backend-migration recovery exists upstream. sigtop's `-k [system:]keyfile` flag tests candidate keys against a real SQLCipher DB without touching the live keyring.

## shell-scripting
- [bash's `wait -n` returns immediately (exit 127) when the job table is empty, so a throttle loop that swallows that with `|| true` degenerates into a 100%-CPU busy-spin with zero child processes](shell-scripting/bash-wait-n-with-an-empty-job-table-returns-immediately-turning-a-swallowed-error-into-a-100-percent-cpu-busy-spin.md) — 2026-08-29 — Empirically reproduced (not just cited): `while true; do wait -n 2>/dev/null || true; done` pins 100% CPU, state R, zero children — an exact structural match for 7 real orphaned `cleanup --clean` instances found burning 1000+ CPU-minutes. Root cause is a counter-based (`inflight`) throttle desyncing from the real job table rather than tracking an explicit PID array; fix is `wait -n "${pids[@]}"` with an always-accurate array, or a `jobs -rp` non-empty guard before calling `wait -n` at all.
- [flock(2), not a PID file, is the correct single-instance guard for a bash CLI's destructive action, because the kernel releases the lock automatically on process death with no stale-file race, matching apt/dpkg, certbot, and borgbackup](shell-scripting/flock-not-pid-files-is-the-correct-single-instance-guard-for-a-bash-cli-because-the-kernel-releases-the-lock-on-process-death-with-no-stale-file-race.md) — 2026-08-29 — PID files have 3 documented races (PID reuse, TOCTOU, stale-cleanup race per Greg's Wiki / LWN); flock's release-on-fd-close is kernel-guaranteed even under SIGKILL. `flock -n <lockfile>` (fail-fast) fits an interactive CLI; `flock -w N` (bounded wait) fits cron/unattended. Lock file belongs in `/run/lock/`, not bare `/tmp`.
- [Terminal transcripts of TUI agents should be ANSI-stripped at write time (98.7% reduction, beats gzip) and stall detection must be decoupled from transcript byte-growth, because ~37% of redraw frames carry zero content](shell-scripting/terminal-transcript-capture-strip-ansi-at-write-time-and-decouple-stall-detection-from-transcript-bytes.md) — 2026-08-29 — Waspflow's 5,517 transcripts / 24.67 GB are ~98.7% escape bytes on large files; correct ECMA-48 stripping (clause 5.4 params 0x30-0x3F, intermediates 0x20-0x2F, final 0x40-0x7E; clause 5.6 OSC/DCS/APC/PM/SOS to ST or xterm's BEL) beats gzip alone on every file measured. Three reproduced traps: `col -b` does NOT strip CSI (leaves `31m` as visible text — worse than nothing); waspflow's current `strip_ansi` misses CSI-with-intermediate/non-alpha-finals/all OSC, leaving 219k escapes per 40 MB; and a streaming Fe rule `\e[\x30-\x7e]` matches a PARTIAL OSC's `\e]` prefix, leaking payload at read boundaries. Load-bearing structural finding: every provider's `_is_idle` reads a session log (end_turn/task_complete/turn_ended), NEVER the transcript — transcript bytes feed only the 45s stall clock, so this is a bounded change; 249/272 real stalls recorded "no output" (total cessation, not content-only), and the longest content-free spinner run is 11 frames ≈ 0.9-1.4s, a ~30x margin. Retention prior art converges on delete-oldest-whole-file against a derived budget (journald 10%/4G cap, per-file 1/8 targeting ~7 files; k8s 10Mi×5 with "only the latest file" via kubectl logs); nobody rewrites a live-writer log in place (that's what logrotate copytruncate is for, and it documents a lossy race) and nobody ships head+tail elision (GitHub truncates head, Buildkite tail).

## dev-tool-caches
- [mise ships a built-in `mise prune` command that safely removes tool versions no longer referenced by any tracked config, so a disk-cleanup script should delegate to it rather than reimplement version-liveness detection](dev-tool-caches/mise-has-a-built-in-prune-command-that-safely-removes-superseded-tool-versions-so-a-cleanup-script-should-delegate-not-reimplement.md) — 2026-08-29 — Empirically confirmed on a live mise 2026.8.14 install: `mise prune --dry-run` correctly identified 2 superseded Node.js versions with a per-version justification ("node is required at 24.19.0 by ~/.config/mise/config.toml"); `mise ls --prunable` and `--dry-run-code` (exit 1 if prunable) fit this codebase's existing count/bytes probe convention. One honest gap (mise's own, not a script limitation): blind to configs mise has never been invoked against — degrades gracefully since mise re-fetches on next use.
- [NVIDIA's own support docs recommend deleting the GLCache (OpenGL shader) and ComputeCache (CUDA JIT) caches as standard troubleshooting, so both are safe for automated cleanup — but should be size-gated, not age-gated, since eviction is size-driven and cache-hit mtime-update behavior is undocumented](dev-tool-caches/nvidia-shader-and-cuda-jit-caches-are-officially-endorsed-as-safe-to-delete-and-should-be-sized-not-age-gated.md) — 2026-08-29 — NVIDIA KB 5735 + an NVIDIA staff forum reply both confirm safe/regenerable; corruption is a reason TO delete, not to avoid it. Two separate real paths on this machine: `~/.cache/nvidia/GLCache` (248M) and `~/.nv/ComputeCache` (66M, easily missed by a `~/.cache`-only scan). Do NOT reuse this codebase's node_modules/venv mtime-staleness pattern here — no NVIDIA doc confirms cache-hit mtime semantics, unlike project-source recency which IS a meaningful signal for those artifact types.
- [apt-get autoclean + apt-get autoremove --purge is the documented safe, kernel-protected pair for reclaiming apt/kernel disk space, but a real machine can carry old kernels marked apt-mark manual that autoremove will never touch regardless of age](dev-tool-caches/apt-autoremove-is-kernel-protected-by-default-but-a-real-machine-can-still-carry-old-kernels-marked-manually-installed-that-autoremove-will-never-touch.md) — 2026-08-29 — `APT::Protect-Kernels`/`VersionedKernelPackages` confirmed real and populated via `apt-config dump` on a live machine. But `apt-get -s autoremove --purge` found "0 to remove" despite 1.5G of old kernels in `/boot`, because 2 of them were `apt-mark showmanual` — invisible to autoremove's candidate pool entirely. Reclaiming that needs an explicit `apt-mark auto` relabel, a bigger and more consequential action than a passive `--yes` cleanup tool should take unprompted; surface as info-only instead.
- [Archive-eligibility must be proven by durable-ref reachability, not object presence](shell-scripting/archive-eligibility-must-be-proven-by-durable-ref-reachability-not-object-presence.md) — a fully-merged branch is the case a naive fork-point guard archives most expensively; 98.8% of a 14.4 GB bundle store was redundant, but "present in a repo" and "on a ref" both wrongly classify only-copies as safe
